entitlements, quotas, DPIA screening, audit.
P0217: The Search Index is downstream of the catalogue and ingestion; it serves ranked recall to the Reader's search, the AI Advisor's candidate retrieval, and — via the host's /api/v1/catalogue/search and AI proxy — classroom smart search (FR-CLIENT-007). The AI Advisor takes the active tier and credentials from Settings & Security and holds no credential logic itself. The Classroom Client consumes the host exclusively through ILibraryHostClient; an architecture-level rule forbids it depending on OgmaLibrary.Infrastructure.LanHost internals (ADR-0012).
P0218: \newpage
P0219: 54. Data Architecture
P0220: 5.14.1 Storage Model — As Built
P0221: The data architecture has two halves. The catalogue of record is a single SQLite database accessed through EF Core 10.0.9 (CatalogueDbContext, src/OgmaLibrary.Infrastructure/Catalogue), authoritative for book identity, metadata, shelves, progress, annotations, AI history, and — since Phases 16–18 — host settings, sessions, and school-admin policy. The sidecar asset folder holds large or regenerable artefacts on disk, referenced by content hash rather than embedded as blobs. The split keeps the database compact for the 2 s catalogue-load budget (NFR-OGMA-002) and keeps derived assets disposable.
P0222: Schema evolution is migration-only: 11 EF Core migrations exist, applied idempotently at startup by CatalogueMigrator:
P0223: InitialCatalogue — the 20-table core schema (Section 4.3), including Works/Editions.
P0224: AddQualityScoreToBooks — metadata quality score column (FR-META-007).
P0225: Phase09Annotations — AnnotationsV2, AnnotationLayers, ReadingMemory.
P0226: Phase10SearchIndexSchema — FTS5 virtual table, triggers, extraction columns and indexes.
P0227: Phase10MetadataSearchIndex — metadata search covering index.
P0228: Phase11EmbeddingSchema — embedding model/dimension columns and indexes.
P0229: Phase12AiGatewayTables — AiAuditEvents, AiConsentRecords.
P0230: Phase15OcrPowerReaderSchema — ExtractedPages.Source and OCR-derived flags.
P0231: Phase15SmartShelfIndexes — smart-shelf evaluation indexes.
P0232: Phase16LanHostTables — HostModeSettings, HostClientSessions.
P0233: Phase18SchoolAdminTables — the 6 school-admin tables.
P0234: 5.24.2 On-Disk Layout — As Built
P0235: The application owns one data directory (default: Ogma Library Data under OS app-data; CatalogueServiceExtensions.GetDefaultDataDirectory()).
P0236: Ogma Library Data/├── ogma.db                 SQLite catalogue of record (+ SearchFts5 virtual table)├── covers/                 cover images, 200×300 JPEG├── thumbnails/             per-page thumbnail JPEGs├── spines/                 spine strip JPEGs (7×100) for the 3D shelf├── ocr/                    hOCR output files (Phase 15)├── text/                   gzip-compressed extracted text pages (JSON)├── embeddings/             serialised embedding vectors (binary)├── backups/                pre-write-back PDF originals + pre-migration snapshots├── export/                 portable export bundles (ZIP)├── citations/              citation-card exports (text)└── classroom/              Phase 17 client-side root    ├── profiles/<profileId>/private.db   per-student private state (ADR-0012)    └── cache/                            offline book cache with size limit + purge
P0237: SidecarService resolves every asset as <class-folder>/<content-hash>[-variant].<ext> via Resolve(contentHash, SidecarClass, variant). Keying by content hash (not book ID or path) means a renamed or moved file keeps its assets, and identical files share them. This closes the sidecar-naming context gap carried from the v1.0 HLD. Each class is independently regenerable except backups/, which is the one class holding non-derived data and is therefore covered by the reversibility protocol (Section 9.3).
P0238: 5.34.3 Entity Map — As Built
P0239: The full table set, grouped by owning context:
P0240: Group
P0241: Tables
P0242: Notes
P0243: Catalogue core
P0244: Books, BookFiles, BookMetadataFields, MetadataLookups, Authors, BookAuthors, Shelves, ShelfBooks, Works, Editions
P0245: Authors and shelves are join-table normalised so FR-CAT-002 filters are index-backed. Works/Editions are schema-present from InitialCatalogue; the merge/split behaviour (FR-CAT-007) is V2 design-to-be.
P0246: Reading
P0247: ReadingProgress, Bookmarks, Annotations, AnnotationBodies, AnnotationsV2, AnnotationLayers, ReadingMemory
P0248: AnnotationsV2 + AnnotationLayers (Phase 09) supersede the initial Annotations/AnnotationBodies pair for new writes; ReadingMemory backs FR-READ-014.
P0249: Search
P0250: ExtractedPages, SearchChunks, EmbeddingVectors, plus the SearchFts5 virtual table
P0251: ExtractedPages.Source distinguishes "OCR" from text-layer extraction (ADR-0011).
P0252: AI
P0253: AiQueryHistory, AiConsentRecords, AiAuditEvents
P0254: Consent and audit are separate from general AuditEvents so AI-specific retention and erasure (FR-AI-009, NFR-PROD-014) operate without touching the general trail.
P0255: Operations
P0256: Jobs, AuditEvents
P0257: Jobs carries per-item checkpoints for NFR-OGMA-009 recovery.
P0258: LAN host (Phase 16)
P0259: HostModeSettings (IsEnabled, Port, ContentMode, DisplayName), HostClientSessions (ClientId, ExpiresUtc, RevokedUtc, …)
P0260: ContentMode records the per-deployment page-render versus file-stream choice (ADR-0010); RevokedUtc implements FR-LAN-007 revocation.
P0261: School admin (Phase 18)
P0262: EnrolledProfiles, LibraryPublishSettings, SharedShelves, SharedShelfBooks, SchoolAiEntitlements, AiUsageLedger
P0263: Entitlements hold per-student/per-class quotas and rate limits; the ledger is the quota-reservation and cost record (FR-ADMIN-008/009/010).
P0264: Key relationships in prose: a Book has one or more BookFiles; a Book has many BookMetadataFields, each linked to the MetadataLookups rows that supplied candidate values (field-level provenance, FR-META-004); a BookFile owns its Bookmarks, annotations, ReadingProgress, and ExtractedPages; each ExtractedPage yields SearchChunks; each SearchChunk may have one EmbeddingVectors row. Student private state lives outside this database entirely, in per-profile private.db files (ADR-0012) — a file-level privacy boundary, not a row filter.
P0265: 5.44.4 Book-Identity Resolution Chain — As Built
P0266: BookIdentityService.ResolveAsync implements a 4-tier chain returning a typed BookMatchResult:
P0267: Relative path match within the library root → ExactMatch.
P0268: SHA-256 content hash (Books.Sha256Hash) → ExactMatch; an unchanged hash re-matches a moved or renamed file (FR-LIB-003).
P0269: Size + modification time fast path → FuzzyMatch at confidence 0.90; this is also the incremental-rescan skip rule (FR-LIB-006): unchanged size and mtime means no re-hash.
P0270: PDF fingerprint → FuzzyMatch at confidence 0.75.
P0271: No tier matching yields NewBook; an unreadable path yields Unresolvable with a reason. Deleting a record is never automatic: an unavailable file is flagged and its metadata, shelves, notes, and progress are retained (FR-LIB-004). Confidence values ride on the match result so ingestion can route fuzzy matches through review rather than silently rebinding identity.
P0272: 5.54.5 Full-Text Index — As Built
P0273: Full-text search is served by the SearchFts5 FTS5 external-content virtual table (content='SearchChunks'), created in raw SQL by the Phase10SearchIndexSchema migration. Consistency is maintained by 3 triggers — SearchChunks_Fts_Insert, SearchChunks_Fts_Update, SearchChunks_Fts_Delete — so the index tracks the content table without duplicating chunk text. Two defensive mechanisms exist because external-content indexes can silently diverge:
P0274: FtsIndexService.CheckIntegrityAsync compares index and content and reports divergence.
P0275: The Index Manager surface (IndexManagerService, FR-SEARCH-006) exposes a user-invokable rebuild alongside progress, pending OCR, failures, and index size.
P0276: Ranking uses BM25 from FTS5, blended downstream (Section 4.6).
P0277: 5.64.6 Embedding Storage and Hybrid Ranking Flow — As Built
P0278: Embeddings support semantic and hybrid search (FR-SEARCH-004/005, FR-AI-006). The as-built flow:
P0279: SearchExtractionWorker extracts text into ExtractedPages and chunks it into SearchChunks (ExtractionPipelineService).
P0280: EmbeddingGenerationWorker calls EmbeddingGenerationService, which produces vectors through the gateway path — locally via OllamaEmbeddingAdapter when the local tier is active — and records model and dimensions per the Phase11EmbeddingSchema; vector payloads live in the embeddings/ sidecar class.
P0281: At query time SemanticSearchService embeds the query and scans stored vectors by brute-force cosine similarity — correct and adequate at the 2,000-book MVP scale (ADR-0006); a vector index (for example sqlite-vec) is adopted only if Phase 20 measurement pushes the scan past the P95 budget.
P0282: CombinedSearchService merges three result streams — MetadataSearchService exact/filtered matches, FtsIndexService BM25 keyword matches, semantic hits — and HybridRankingService computes the final score as a normalised weighted sum of 5 signals: exact match, recency (RecencyScore), reading status (StatusScore), rating (RatingScore), and semantic similarity, with HybridRankingWeights.Default and weight normalisation guarding degenerate configurations.
P0283: Every result carries its match location (title, author, note, text page, semantic) for the explanation surface (FR-SEARCH-003).
P0284: Erasure is first-class: EmbeddingErasureService deletes vectors and sidecar payloads for a book or the whole library (NFR-PROD-014), and the Index Manager exposes it.
P0285: 5.74.7 Design-to-Be Deltas in This Section
P0286: Optional at-rest encryption of ogma.db, backups/, and classroom private databases — Phase 19 (key in OS credential storage).
P0287: Orphaned-sidecar garbage collection — Phase 19.
P0288: Work/Edition merge/split behaviour (FR-CAT-007) — V2; schema already present.
P0289: EF Core 10 provider migration decision — ADR-0014, due at Phase 19 entry.
P0290: \newpage
P0291: 65. PDF Pipeline and Reader Architecture
P0292: 6.15.1 Pipeline Stages, Tools, and Outputs — As Built
P0293: The PDF pipeline transforms an untrusted source file into catalogue records, search content, and rendered surfaces. Every stage names its real tool and output. All imported PDFs are untrusted input; isolation and resource bounds are specified in Section 9.
P0294: Stage
P0295: As-built tool
P0296: Output
P0297: Open / render page
P0298: PdfiumAdapter via PDFtoImage 5.2.1 (PDFium) + SkiaSharp (ADR-0004)
P0299: SKBitmap page surface
P0300: Password unlock
P0301: IPasswordProvider → Windows DPAPI credential store / macOS Keychain
P0302: Unlocked document after consent (FR-READ-009)
P0303: Extract metadata
P0304: PdfPig
P0305: Bibliographic candidates (title, author, ISBN signals)
P0306: Extract text
P0307: PdfPig / PDFium text layer via ExtractionPipelineService
P0308: ExtractedPages rows, gzip JSON in text/ sidecar
P0309: OCR (scanned pages)
P0310: TesseractOcrProvider (Tesseract 5.2.0, ADR-0011)
P0311: hOCR in ocr/ sidecar; ExtractedPages.Source = "OCR"
P0312: Thumbnail / cover / spine
P0313: PDFium render + SkiaSharp resize
P0314: JPEGs in covers/, thumbnails/, spines/
P0315: Metadata write-back
P0316: PdfWriteBackService (PDFsharp)
P0317: Modified DocInfo under backup-diff-verify-restore (FR-META-005)
P0318: Annotation
P0319: Catalogue-first (AnnotationsV2)
P0320: Annotation record; no file mutation (ADR-0008)
P0321: Two rules constrain the pipeline:
P0322: Extracted text may not be in reading order. Per-page output carries an extraction-quality flag so search and AI consumers weight or warn on low-quality text rather than treating it as authoritative prose; OCR-derived pages are additionally marked by Source.
P0323: Write-back is reversible by construction. PdfWriteBackService backs the original into backups/ (content-hash keyed), presents the field diff for confirmation, verifies the written file opens, and restores the original on any failure (NFR-PROD-010). Annotation write-back to PDF remains out of scope by decision (ADR-0008).
P0324: 6.25.2 OCR Strategy — As Built
P0325: Scanned, image-only PDFs are detected during ingestion; OCR is queued as an optional background job (FR-READ-010) on OcrWorker/OcrJobProcessor, never blocking reading. Jobs render pages through the same IPdfRendererFactory the reader uses, process one page at a time, and mark the book OCR-derived only after completion. Missing native binaries or language data fail the individual job with a recorded reason — never application startup. OCR text is local-only by decision (ADR-0011): routing OCR through a cloud provider would silently promote scanned books to content-aware exposure, so the tier model forbids it structurally.
P0326: 6.35.3 Reader Architecture — As Built
P0327: The Reader context (src/OgmaLibrary.Reader) owns rendering, navigation, zoom, selection, bookmarks, annotations, citations, reading memory, and progress events — and is test-forbidden from referencing Infrastructure or Search (Section 2.4).
P0328: ReaderView ── ReaderViewModel                   │                   ▼         ReaderSessionService   (current book, page, zoom, layout, navigation history)   │             │               │                │               │   ▼             ▼               ▼                ▼               ▼PageRenderCache TextLayerService AnnotationService BookmarkService ReadingProgressService(PdfiumAdapter  (selection +     AnnotationLayerService            (resume, status, + eviction)    InDocumentSearch) CitationService                   progress events)                                  ReadingMemoryService
P0329: ReaderSessionService holds the live session: book, page, zoom, layout (single, spread, continuous), and navigation history (FR-READ-002/003/004). It resolves files through IBookFileLocator — which in classroom mode transparently materialises streamed content via ClassroomBookFileMaterializer (Section 8.5), so the reader is mode-agnostic.
P0330: TextLayerService and InDocumentSearchService drive selection and in-document search with match highlighting (FR-READ-006).
P0331: AnnotationService, BookmarkService, and AnnotationLayerService persist to the catalogue before reporting saved (NFR-OGMA-008) and publish through IAnnotationEventPublisher so overlays reload accurately (FR-READ-008); layers back FR-READ-015.
P0332: CitationService produces citation cards into the citations/ sidecar class (FR-READ-011); ReadingMemoryService records the per-book reading memory (FR-READ-014).
P0333: ReadingProgressService persists resume position and status (FR-READ-001).
P0334: Split view (SplitViewViewModel) renders two documents or two locations side by side (FR-READ-012).
P0335: 6.45.4 Page-Render Cache — As Built
P0336: PageRenderCache is the mechanism behind the 100 ms cached page-turn budget (NFR-OGMA-005), instrumented through IBenchmarkContext from construction:
P0337: Holds the current page at active zoom, pre-rendered previous and next pages, and a low-resolution preview for instant display during navigation.
P0338: Renders are non-blocking and cancellable: a render for a page the user has left is cancelled, keeping the UI thread within the 100 ms non-blocking limit (NFR-PROD-005).
P0339: Eviction is by memory budget: pages furthest from the current position and high-resolution variants evict first, so a 285-page document (the gc-large golden fixture) cannot exhaust memory.
P0340: 6.55.5 Error and Recovery Paths
P0341: A malformed or oversized document fails per-file with a recorded reason, visible and retryable in the scan-health surface (FR-LIB-005, FR-LIB-007); it never aborts the batch.
P0342: A failed page render degrades to the low-resolution preview plus an error state; the session survives.
P0343: A password-protected document without a stored credential surfaces PasswordUnlockViewModel; a declined unlock leaves the book catalogued but unopened.
P0344: A write-back failure at any step (backup, write, verify-open) restores the original and records the failure in the audit trail — the source file is never left corrupt.
P0345: 6.65.6 Design-to-Be Deltas in This Section
P0346: Phase 19: untrusted-PDF isolation hardening (dedicated process or sandbox for render/OCR beyond project-boundary isolation); SAST over the interop surface.
P0347: Phase 20: formal NFR-OGMA-005 acceptance on W-REF-01 with the golden corpus.
P0348: \newpage
P0349: 76. 3D Bookshelf Architecture
P0350: 7.16.1 WebView-Hosted Three.js Surface — As Built
P0351: The 3D shelf is a WebGL2 surface hosted in the platform WebView — WebView2 on Windows, WKWebView on macOS — running Three.js r181, bundled locally in src/shelf3d with no CDN and no network egress (ADR-0003). Native binding is isolated behind IWebViewBridge, with WebView2Bridge and WKWebViewBridge selected by platform in CompositionRoot, and the host adapter seam (IWebViewHostAdapter) keeps the concrete WebView package replaceable — a deliberate hedge, because the Avalonia-native WebView path is still consolidating and final package binding ships with Phase 22 packaging.
P0352: The WebView is a rendering boundary for a constrained surface: it loads only bundled assets, receives no file-system paths, and reaches local images exclusively through the ogma://assets/<class>/<file> scheme handler, which enforces traversal protection and explicit MIME types and serves only sidecar-folder content. The Bookshelf Presentation context owns scene state and the bridge contract but not catalogue truth: a clicked book resolves to the same book-detail surface as the grid, list, and directory views (FR-CAT-001).
P0353: 7.26.2 The Typed Bridge Contract — As Built
P0354: Phase 14 delivered discriminated C# and TypeScript message unions (BridgeCommand/BridgeEvent) covering scene, layout, theme, camera, interaction, WebGL2 status, and performance warnings. The inbound dispatch is validated before any application side effect: malformed book IDs, unknown message types, non-finite camera values, and non-finite FPS warnings are rejected (SI-3) — behaviour proven by the Phase 01 Spike 3 harness (7 of 7 checks) and retained as tests.
P0355: Outbound (C# → JS), the scene model is compact; textures are ogma:// references, never paths:
P0356: {  "theme": "warm-oak",  "layout": "shelf",  "selectedBookId": "b_01H...",  "books": [    {      "id": "b_01H...",      "title": "The Name of the Wind",      "authors": ["Patrick Rothfuss"],      "spineTexture": "ogma://assets/spines/<content-hash>.jpg",      "coverTexture": "ogma://assets/covers/<content-hash>.jpg",      "status": "reading"    }  ]}
P0357: Inbound (JS → C#), the honoured event set is closed: bookClicked, bookDoubleClicked, bookHovered, cameraChanged, plus WebGL2-status and FPS-warning reports. Click and double-click resolve through the catalogue to book detail (parity is UI-tested); cameraChanged persists view state; FPS warnings surface performance degradation to the native side rather than failing silently.
P0358: 7.36.3 Performance Posture — As Built and Phase 20 Gate
P0359: A deterministic 500-book layout budget runs as npm run perf:budget, with the baseline recorded at docs/benchmarks/phase-14/layout-baseline-20260601.json — this is the CI trend guard.
P0360: The Phase 01 spike scene (500 textured spine meshes, WebGL2 capability check, 10 s mean-FPS sampler) exists for the formal measurement.
P0361: Design-to-be: the binding ≥ 60 FPS at 500 books gate (NFR-OGMA-006) is measured on M-REF-01/W-REF-01 in Phase 20 (TRACK-P01-S4-MACOS-FPS); GPU-less CI runners cannot produce acceptance evidence. The 1,000- and 2,000-book measurements establish the degradation curve and the threshold at which the shelf falls back to paged presentation.
P0362: 7.46.4 Accessibility Fallback — As Built
P0363: The 3D shelf is one of four browsing modes, never the only path to a book. Bookshelf3DView ships an accessible fallback and a localised toolbar; where WebGL2 is unavailable (the capability check reports over the bridge) or a screen-reader or keyboard-only user requires it, the application falls back to the first-class grid or list view with no loss of catalogue function (NFR-PROD-007, NFR-PROD-008). A headless fallback render test keeps the fallback path from rotting. This satisfies the vision principle that 3D is functional, not gimmick — the 3D surface is an enhancement layered over an accessible core, and no core capability is blocked by the open macOS FPS gate.
P0364: \newpage
P0365: 87. AI Architecture
P0366: 8.17.1 Provider-Neutral Gateway — As Built
P0367: All AI traffic routes through AiGateway : IAiGateway (src/OgmaLibrary.Infrastructure/AI), the single off-device egress path (ADR-0007). The chokepoint is not a convention: Architecture_OnlyInfrastructureUsesHttpClient fails the build if any other project references System.Net.Http, so no feature module can open a provider connection. The registered default provider is AiDisabledProvider — with no key configured, AI is off and the full core remains functional (FR-AI-001).
P0368: Providers implement IAiProvider (declared in OgmaLibrary.Application/Ai) and are created by AiProviderFactory:
P0369: AnthropicProvider — Anthropic-compatible endpoints.
P0370: OpenAiCompatProvider — OpenAI-compatible endpoints, which also covers DeepSeek-compatible services (FR-AI-002).
P0371: OllamaChatProvider — local inference, no egress.
P0372: OllamaEmbeddingAdapter — local embeddings for semantic search (FR-AI-006).
P0373: Swapping or adding a provider is an adapter plus composition-root registration; advisor, privacy, ranking, and plan logic do not change. AiCostCalculator and AiCostFormatter surface per-call model usage and cost estimates (FR-AI-010).
P0374: 8.27.2 Privacy Enforcement — As Built
P0375: AiPrivacyService and AiPayloadBuilder sit between callers and the gateway:
P0376: AiPayloadBuilder assembles the request from exactly the fields the active tier permits — redaction is constructive (the payload is built from an allowlist), not subtractive.
P0377: The payload preview shows the exact content, field set, and destination provider before any off-device send; the send proceeds only after confirmation (NFR-PROD-011).
P0378: Consent is recorded in AiConsentRecords and every off-device call writes an AiAuditEvents row (provider, purpose, payload scope, active tier) — the Phase12AiGatewayTables migration.
P0379: The Privacy Center surface (FR-AI-011) lists provider keys (status only), recent calls with exact payloads and tiers, and delete controls; history erasure and embedding erasure (EmbeddingErasureService) satisfy FR-AI-009 and NFR-PROD-014.
P0380: 8.37.3 Privacy Tiers — As Built
P0381: Tier
P0382: Off-device payload
P0383: As-built enforcement
P0384: 0 — Offline (default)
P0385: None
P0386: AiDisabledProvider registered by default; no key, no UI block on core reading.
P0387: 1 — Metadata-only
P0388: Title, author, tags, categories, descriptions; notes only when enabled (FR-AI-004)
P0389: AiPayloadBuilder allowlist; the classroom default (ADR-0013).
P0390: 2 — Content-aware (opt-in)
P0391: Retrieved document chunks, per library or per query (FR-AI-005)
P0392: Explicit opt-in plus payload preview before any chunk leaves; admin opt-in in classrooms.
P0393: 3 — Local (Ollama)
P0394: None — on-device inference
P0395: OllamaChatProvider / OllamaEmbeddingAdapter; no egress.
P0396: A tier change is audited, and the permitted payload is re-evaluated on every request — the tier is enforced at build time of the payload, not at UI time.
P0397: 8.47.4 Advisor Pipeline — As Built and Deliberate Stub
P0398: AdvisorService (OgmaLibrary.Application/Ai) orchestrates over Infrastructure pipelines in AI/Advisor:
P0399: Recommendations (as-built): RecommendationPipeline builds candidates from the catalogue (AdvisorCatalogueReader, CatalogueRecommendationSource), enriches metadata payloads (MetadataPayloadEnricher), merges hybrid-ranking signals (HybridRankerConsumer, HybridRecommendationMerger), prompts through RecommendationPromptTemplate, and validates parsed output (RecommendationResponseParser, RecommendationValidators) so a malformed model response degrades to a typed AdvisorParseException, never a crash (FR-AI-003).
P0400: Reading plans (as-built): ReadingPlanPipeline with ReadingPlanPromptTemplate/ReadingPlanParser produces sequence, rationale, difficulty, and checkpoints (FR-AI-007).
P0401: Answer mode (deliberate stub): AdvisorService throws NotImplementedException for V2 answer mode (FR-AI-008). This is a scoped deferral, not slop: the view models catch it explicitly beside AiDisabledException, and the classroom answer path is implemented separately with host-catalogue grounding (Section 8.7, ADR-0013). Personal answer mode ships when local retrieval-grounded answering is scheduled.
P0402: History (as-built): the advisor persists AiQueryHistory through application contracts — never direct database writes (Section 3.2).
P0403: A Phase 13 evaluation harness regression-tests advisor behaviour, including payload honesty — the preview must match what is sent.
P0404: 8.57.5 Error and Degradation Paths
P0405: No provider key / AI disabled: AiDisabledException renders the disabled state; no core surface blocks.
P0406: Provider failure or timeout: the typed failure surfaces with the audit entry; recommendations degrade to catalogue-signal-only ordering where possible.
P0407: Malformed model output: parse-and-validate rejects it (AdvisorParseException); partial responses are never presented as complete.
P0408: Cost visibility: the estimate renders before confirmation, so an oversized Tier 2 request is visible before it spends.
P0409: 8.67.6 Design-to-Be Deltas in This Section
P0410: FR-AI-008 personal answer mode with local-evidence citation (V2).
P0411: NFR-OGMA-007 gateway-overhead acceptance (≤ 10 s metadata-only, measured as gateway-side elapsed minus provider round-trip) on reference hardware — Phase 20; the gateway already records application-only and provider latency separately to support the oracle.
P0412: \newpage
P0413: 98. LAN / Classroom E-Library Architecture
P0414: This section is new at baseline v2.0. It specifies the networked product realised in grand-plan Phases 16 (Library Host), 17 (Classroom Client), and 18 (School Administration and Managed AI), governed by ADR-0010, ADR-0012, and ADR-0013 and reconciled against docs/plans/grand-plan/LAN-CLASSROOM-ARCHITECTURE.md in the codebase. All mechanisms below are as-built unless marked design-to-be.
P0415: 9.18.1 Constraint Resolution and Runtime Modes
P0416: The v1.0 baseline constraint CI-2 — no inbound listener, no server endpoint — is scoped, not deleted (ADR-0010). CI-2 remains mandatory for the default Standalone mode; the classroom track introduces a deliberately designed, opt-in server surface. One binary, three runtime modes, selected by configuration:
P0417: Mode
P0418: Listener
P0419: Role
P0420: Standalone (default)
P0421: None
P0422: The single-user product exactly as in Sections 1–7.
P0423: Library Host
P0424: Kestrel HTTPS on the LAN
P0425: Holds the corpus and catalogue of record; serves projections, assets, streams, and the managed AI proxy.
P0426: Client/Classroom
P0427: None (outbound only)
P0428: Discovers and joins a host; reads, searches, keeps private per-profile state; degrades to its local library offline.
P0429: The bounded-context discipline extends unchanged: the catalogue of record lives on the host and remains the single identity authority; clients are projection consumers, exactly as the in-process Reader and 3D shelf are (FR-CLIENT-010: clients never mutate host files or metadata).
P0430: 9.28.2 Host Surface — KestrelHostModeListener
P0431: The LAN Host context (src/OgmaLibrary.Infrastructure/LanHost) is the only place in the application with an inbound listener. LibraryHostService owns activation: host mode is off by default (HostModeSettings.IsEnabled), enabling it is an explicit, audited admin action, and disabling it is immediate and reversible (FR-LAN-001). KestrelHostModeListener.StartAsync binds a Kestrel HTTPS endpoint on the address selected by LanBindAddressSelector, guarded by LanClientAddressPolicy (LAN-scope addresses only). The versioned endpoint set:
P0432: Endpoint
P0433: Purpose
P0434: GET /api/v1/health
P0435: Liveness and host identity for discovery UX.
P0436: POST /api/v1/auth/session
P0437: Per-profile session issue against enrollment (FR-LAN-005).
P0438: GET /api/v1/catalogue
P0439: Paged summaries of published books only (FR-LAN-009).
P0440: GET /api/v1/catalogue/search
P0441: Host-index search with match-location explanations (FR-CLIENT-007).
P0442: GET /api/v1/catalogue/{bookId}
P0443: Book detail projection.
P0444: GET /api/v1/assets/{assetClass}/{contentHash}
P0445: Cover/spine/thumbnail sidecar assets; the asset class is allowlist-mapped (TryMapAssetClass) and the hash is escaped — the LAN equivalent of the ogma:// scheme.
P0446: GET /api/v1/books/{bookId}/page/{pageNumber}
P0447: Server-side page render (LanPageRenderer), throttled by LanPageRenderLimiter.
P0448: GET /api/v1/books/{bookId}/file
P0449: Range-request file streaming (FR-LAN-006, FR-CLIENT-004).
P0450: POST /api/v1/ai/search/preview, POST /api/v1/ai/search
P0451: The managed AI proxy (Section 8.7).
P0452: PUT /api/v1/profile/sync, GET /api/v1/profile/sync
P0453: Opaque per-student sync blobs (Section 8.6).
P0454: POST /admin/ai/test-connection
P0455: Host-local admin key check; never callable as a student.
P0456: Two content-serving strategies exist per deployment, recorded in HostModeSettings.ContentMode (ADR-0010): page renders (host renders, streams images — raw PDFs never leave the host) or file streams (client renders locally — higher performance, corpus leaves the host machine). The trade is privacy versus throughput and is the administrator's explicit choice.
P0457: Path discipline: LanBookFileResolver validates every served file against the published library root before a byte moves — unpublished content is not merely unlisted, it is unresolvable (FR-LAN-009). Every connection, stream, and denial writes to the host activity audit, exportable by the administrator (FR-LAN-010).
P0458: 9.38.3 Discovery and Join
P0459: MdnsAdvertiser (Makaretu.Dns.Multicast) advertises _ogma._tcp with the host display name, so a student selects "Room 12 Library" instead of typing an address; MdnsResolver performs client-side discovery, and manual address entry is the documented fallback. Enrollment uses join codes: the host renders a QR join code (QRCoder 1.8.0) that ClassroomJoinParser decodes into host address, port, and certificate fingerprint — binding discovery and trust into one artefact (FR-LAN-005).
P0460: Known constraint (Phase 01 Spike 7): mDNS requires UDP 5353 through the host firewall; the Phase 22 installer must add the rule, and manual entry covers locked-down networks (TRACK-P01-S7-MDNS).
P0461: 9.48.4 TLS and the Trust Model
P0462: The host generates its own certificate via LocalCertificateProvisioner (ICertificateProvisioner / IHostServerCertificateProvider), with the private key DPAPI-protected on Windows; HostCaStores manage the host-side material. Transport is HTTPS only — no plaintext content on the wire (FR-LAN-002).
P0463: Clients pin trust on first use: TlsHostCertificateFingerprintProbe reads the presented certificate, the fingerprint is displayed for verification (or arrives pre-verified inside the QR join code), and HostTrustService persists the pinned fingerprint in CredentialBackedHostTrustStore (FR-LAN-004). A fingerprint mismatch on reconnect is a hard failure, not a warning.
P0464: Transport selection is evidence-backed: the Phase 01 spike measured Kestrel HTTPS at 196.75 MB/s over loopback, 39 times the 5 MB/s acceptance bar (ADR-0010 amendment record).
P0465: Sessions are bearer-scoped per profile: ClientSessionService issues, lists, limits, and revokes sessions (HostClientSessions.ExpiresUtc/RevokedUtc); revocation takes effect at next connection, and a revoked client keeps its local notes (FR-LAN-007, FR-CLIENT-009).
P0466: 9.58.5 Client Mode and Streamed Reading
P0467: The Classroom Client context (src/OgmaLibrary.Infrastructure/ClassroomClient) consumes the host exclusively through the typed ILibraryHostClient contract (LibraryHostHttpClient, wrapped by CachingLibraryHostClient); it is architecture-forbidden from referencing host-server internals (ADR-0012). Integration with the core is by decoration, not forking:
P0468: ClassroomCatalogueReadModel wraps the standalone CatalogueReadModel: connected, it projects the host catalogue; otherwise it passes through to the local library (FR-CLIENT-011).
P0469: ClassroomBookFileLocator wraps BookFileLocator: for a shared book it invokes ClassroomBookFileMaterializer to materialise streamed content through the offline cache, so ReaderSessionService and the entire reader stack are mode-agnostic. The client never requires a full-file copy to begin reading (FR-CLIENT-004).
P0470: DiskOfflineCacheService caches recently read books under classroom/cache/ with an explicit size limit and purge control (FR-CLIENT-005); a dropped LAN link degrades to cache plus the local library, and reading state is preserved for re-sync (FR-LAN-008).
P0471: ClassroomModeService (FileClassroomModeService) holds the mode state; Standalone is the default and client mode never writes to the standalone catalogue.
P0472: 9.68.6 Identity, Roles, and Private State
P0473: Per ADR-0012, each profile (ProfileId UUID v4; roles Student, Teacher, Guest) owns a private SQLite database at classroom/profiles/<profileId>/private.db, managed by FileClassroomProfileService and StudentPrivateRepository. Privacy is a file boundary, not a row filter: reading state, bookmarks, notes, and AI history are per-profile and never passively visible to peers or the host (FR-CLIENT-002, FR-CLIENT-008). Guest sessions are transient and create no database. Optional sync (ClassroomSyncService) pushes an opaque, codec-sealed blob (ClassroomSyncBlobCodec) to the host per student (FileProfileSyncBlobStore host-side) with last-writer-wins-per-field conflict rules and no silent data loss (FR-CLIENT-006). Teacher-role dashboards are aggregate and disclosed, not covert surveillance (FR-CLIENT-012); entitlements limit which shelves and books a profile may open (FR-CLIENT-013).
P0474: 9.78.7 School Administration and Managed AI
P0475: The School Administration context (src/OgmaLibrary.Infrastructure/SchoolAdmin, ADR-0013) is host-local — there is no internet-facing admin console — and starts as a disabled scaffold gated on host mode plus administrator role:
P0476: Publication and curation: SchoolAdminCatalogueService publishes and unpublishes folders (LibraryPublishSettings) and manages shared shelves (SharedShelves, SharedShelfBooks) (FR-ADMIN-001/002).
P0477: Enrollment: SchoolProfileEnrollmentService enrolls, edits, and revokes profiles (EnrolledProfiles) (FR-ADMIN-003).
P0478: Managed keys: SchoolAiKeyProvider holds the school's provider key in the host OS credential store, separate from any standalone key; public APIs expose key status, never material; keys are never transmitted to clients (FR-ADMIN-004).
P0479: The AI proxy: AiProxyEndpointHandler serves the two-step preview/confirm flow. A student request must carry an authenticated classroom session; the handler builds the payload preview under the class tier (default metadata-only; content-aware is admin opt-in — FR-ADMIN-006/007), blocks until the student confirms, runs SchoolDpiaScreeningService (missing jurisdiction, missing legal basis, or an unapproved minor/content-aware combination returns a typed blocked result, never a best-effort provider call), atomically reserves quota against SchoolAiEntitlements, then routes through the same AiGateway of Section 7 — one egress chokepoint for the whole classroom.
P0480: Grounding: ClassroomAnswerGrounder strips citations not present in the host catalogue before a response reaches a student (FR-ADMIN-011).
P0481: Metering and audit: successful calls write the AiUsageLedger; SchoolUsageDashboardService shows usage, cost, and quota utilisation (FR-ADMIN-010); per-student rate limits are enforced pre-call (FR-ADMIN-009); students can delete their own AI history and the administrator can purge institutionally (SchoolAiHistoryManagementService, FR-ADMIN-012/013). Audit rows do not retain raw student query text absent an owner-ratified policy.
P0482: Admin-originated classroom searches are rejected (school_ai_admin_forbidden) — the proxy is for managed student sessions only.
P0483: 9.88.8 Failure and Degradation Paths
P0484: Host unreachable: client shows connection state and falls back to cache plus local library (FR-CLIENT-011); state re-syncs on reconnect (FR-LAN-008).
P0485: Certificate mismatch: connection refused; the student is directed to re-enrol via a fresh join code.
P0486: Quota exhausted / rate-limited / DPIA-blocked: typed error results; no provider call occurs and no spend happens.
P0487: Host outage pauses classroom AI; cached reading continues (accepted consequence, ADR-0013).
P0488: Render overload: LanPageRenderLimiter sheds load by queueing or rejecting renders rather than degrading the host desktop.
P0489: 9.98.9 Design-to-Be Deltas in This Section
P0490: Phase 19: STRIDE threat model and penetration exercise of the host surface (the highest-risk asset in the product); admin-authentication hardening; audit-integrity controls.
P0491: Phase 20: concurrency and render-throughput budgets for the ~20–40-client classroom envelope, measured on reference hardware.
P0492: Per-jurisdiction minors'-data DPIA execution accompanies every classroom deployment (the DPIA screening service enforces fail-closed until it exists).
P0493: Phase 22: installer firewall rule for UDP 5353 (mDNS).
P0494: \newpage
P0495: 109. Cross-Cutting Concerns
P0496: 10.19.1 Security Architecture
P0497: The standalone threat model is that of a local-first desktop application: dominant risks are provider-secret leakage, malicious or malformed PDFs, path traversal against the library root, supply-chain tampering, and silent exfiltration of user content. Host mode adds an inbound LAN surface with its own model (Section 8; full STRIDE execution is Phase 19).
P0498: 10.1.19.1.1 Credential Storage — As Built
P0499: Provider keys and credentials live in OS-native storage behind IPasswordProvider: WindowsPasswordProvider (DPAPI / Credential Manager), MacOsKeychainPasswordProvider, with UnsupportedPasswordProvider failing closed elsewhere. School AI keys use a separate host credential entry (SchoolAiKeyProvider), never shared with standalone keys and never transmitted to clients. Keys are absent from settings files, the catalogue, logs, and audit rows — a correlation identifier stands in for the key. Removing a provider deletes its secret in the same action and writes a credential-removal audit entry.
P0500: 10.1.29.1.2 Single Egress Chokepoint — Architecture-Test Enforced
P0501: Exactly one rule governs off-device traffic: only OgmaLibrary.Infrastructure may reference System.Net.Http, enforced by Architecture_OnlyInfrastructureUsesHttpClient in tests/OgmaLibrary.Tests.Architecture. Within Infrastructure, off-device AI flows only through AiGateway (Section 7) and metadata lookups only through the enrichment providers (which are themselves test-forbidden from AI dependencies). In classroom mode the chokepoint is the host's gateway (Section 8.7) — students have no egress at all. Consequence for an extending engineer: a new feature cannot acquire a network path without either using the gateway or failing the architecture suite.
P0502: 10.1.39.1.3 Untrusted-PDF Isolation — As Built plus Phase 19 Delta
P0503: All imported PDFs are untrusted regardless of source. Rendering and OCR run within the worker boundary (OgmaLibrary.Workers, test-isolated from App), under per-file time and memory bounds, so a malformed document degrades to a per-file failure with a recorded, retryable reason — never a batch abort or crash. Embedded JavaScript and document-initiated fetches are not honoured; the render path produces bitmaps only. Design-to-be (Phase 19): process-level sandboxing of the render/OCR workers and SAST over the native-interop surface.
P0504: 10.1.49.1.4 Path Validation — As Built
P0505: Every file operation validates the resolved, canonicalised path: reader and ingestion against the configured library root; LanBookFileResolver against the published root for every LAN-served byte (Section 8.2); the ogma:// scheme handler and the LAN asset endpoint against the sidecar folder with traversal protection and an asset-class allowlist. Write-back targets only validated in-root files under the reversibility protocol below.
P0506: 10.1.59.1.5 Reversibility Pattern — Backup → Diff → Verify → Restore
P0507: The single pattern behind every destructive operation, as-built in PdfWriteBackService and generalised across the product:
P0508: Backup the artefact (original PDF to backups/, content-hash keyed; catalogue snapshot before each migration).
P0509: Diff — present the exact intended change (field-level metadata diff, FR-META-005; bulk-edit preview, FR-CAT-005) for explicit confirmation.
P0510: Verify — prove the result usable (written PDF re-opened; post-migration verification).
P0511: Restore on any failure — the original returns; the failure is audited; the user's data is never left in a half-state (NFR-PROD-010, NFR-PROD-012).
P0512: Bulk operations are additionally undoable through CommandHistory, the ring-buffer undo stack registered in the composition root.
P0513: 10.1.69.1.6 Local Audit Trail — As Built
P0514: Three append-oriented stores: AuditEvents (sensitive actions: credential changes, tier changes, write-backs, bulk destructive operations, host activation, job recovery), AiAuditEvents (every off-device AI call with provider, purpose, payload scope, tier), and host-side activity audit plus AiUsageLedger for classrooms. Logs are user-viewable and exportable and are not silently truncated (NFR-PROD-013). Design-to-be (Phase 19): tamper-evidence (hash chaining) and audit-integrity controls for the host.
P0515: 10.1.79.1.7 Data at Rest — Design-to-Be (Phase 19)
P0516: Optional encryption of ogma.db, backups/, and classroom private.db files, keys held in OS credential storage. Not yet implemented; scheduled with hardening.
P0517: 10.29.2 Background-Job and Worker Model — As Built
P0518: All long-running work runs on 4 hosted workers in OgmaLibrary.Workers, off the UI thread (NFR-PROD-005): BookIngestionWorker (scan, hash, identity, thumbnails), SearchExtractionWorker (text extraction and chunking), EmbeddingGenerationWorker, and OcrWorker (via OcrJobProcessor). Three properties hold:
P0519: Resumable and recoverable. Progress checkpoints per item in the Jobs table. At startup JobRecoveryService.RecoverAsync resets jobs interrupted by a crash to a resumable state and writes a JobRecovered audit event; completed work is not repeated (NFR-OGMA-009), which fault-injection tests verify.
P0520: Per-file failure isolation. Each file is an independent unit; failures carry reasons and are visible and retryable in the scan-health and index-manager surfaces (FR-LIB-005/007, FR-SEARCH-006).
P0521: Bounded. Per-item time and memory bounds consistent with the untrusted-document posture.
P0522: 10.39.3 Internationalisation and Accessibility — As Built plus Phase 21 Delta
P0523: All UI strings resolve through ILocalizationService (InMemoryLocalizationService); English and French ship complete, switchable at runtime without restart (FR-UX-004). Pseudolocale tests catch hard-coded strings; the planned OGMA0001 Roslyn analyzer is not built — enforcement is convention plus tests, an honest known gap carried in _context/build-status.md.
P0524: Keyboard operability across core flows (FR-UX-005), the accessible 3D fallback (Section 6.4), and headless UI tests exist as scaffolding.
P0525: Design-to-be (Phase 21): Spanish, Italian, German resources; the formal WCAG 2.2 AA audit as a release gate; the analyzer decision (build it or retire the line item).
P0526: 10.49.4 Theming and Icon System — As Built plus Owner Dependency
P0527: Design tokens live in Themes/Tokens.axaml — a single token source for light, dark, and system themes (FR-UX-008) so contrast fixes for the WCAG gate happen in one place. Icons/IconCatalog.cs centralises icon references; current assets are placeholders, and premium PNG icon procurement from the owner is outstanding and is a release blocker by policy — a named dependency, not a footnote.
P0528: 10.59.5 Observability — As Built plus Phase 20 Delta
P0529: Structured logs are written with secrets redacted at the sink. Performance budgets are instrumented through IBenchmarkContext (StopwatchBenchmarkContext), registered first in the composition root so cold-start measurement spans startup; PageRenderCache and the workers report through it. Crash and performance telemetry is opt-in and device-local by default; the crash-free-session rate (NFR-PROD-006) computes only from opted-in data. Design-to-be (Phase 20): the formal acceptance runs on W-REF-01/M-REF-01 — until then every number is trend-only by definition (_context/reference-hardware.md).
P0530: 10.69.6 Update and Migration Safety — As Built plus Phase 22 Delta
P0531: As-built: CatalogueMigrator applies idempotent EF Core migrations behind a pre-migration snapshot, reversible on verification failure (NFR-PROD-012); migration tests exercise the chain. Design-to-be (Phase 22, ADR-0009): Velopack feed with signature-verified delta updates, MSIX, notarized DMG, Authenticode/Developer ID signing, and the packaging of native assets (PDFium, tessdata/eng.traineddata, WebView2 runtime) plus the UDP 5353 firewall rule. No packaging configuration exists in the repository yet.

P0653: Closed since v1.0: the sidecar naming convention (content-hash keying, Section 4.2), reference hardware and budgets (_context/reference-hardware.md, decision CON-1), and the NFR-OGMA-007 oracle (gateway-side elapsed minus provider round-trip).

P0657: 12Controlled Product and Document Design System
P0658: 12.1Direction
P0659: Ogma should feel like an archival reading room translated into a disciplined research tool: quiet, information-dense, tactile, and legible. The identity avoids generic AI-dashboard styling.
P0660: Token
P0661: Value
P0662: Use
P0663: Typeface/display
P0664: Spectral
P0665: Editorial headings and bookish hierarchy
P0666: Typeface/body/UI
P0667: Public Sans
P0668: Long-form reading and controls
P0669: Typeface/technical
P0670: JetBrains Mono
P0671: Hashes, paths, commands, evidence IDs
P0672: Ink navy
P0673: #132338
P0674: Primary text/nav
P0675: Oxblood
P0676: #7A2533
P0677: Selected state and measured emphasis
P0678: Vellum
P0679: #F4EBDD
P0680: Main surface
P0681: Muted brass
P0682: #A77A35
P0683: Secondary accent, never body text on vellum
P0684: The current implementation dependency Avalonia.Fonts.Inter and placeholder icon styling do not conform. This is an explicit design release blocker, not silently repaired by the document theme.
P0685: 12.2Interface Contract
P0686: Minimum 44×44 px touch/click target where layout permits; keyboard equivalents for every action.
P0687: Persistent visible focus using a 3 px oxblood/brass ring with ≥3:1 adjacent contrast.
P0688: Body copy 16 px minimum; reading surface user-adjustable to 200% without clipped controls or horizontal text scrolling.
P0689: Information hierarchy: library/sidebar, work surface, contextual detail; no more than one primary action per panel.
P0690: Status never relies on colour alone; pair icon, label, and optional explanation.
P0691: Empty, loading, offline, permission, provider, malformed-PDF, conflict, and destructive states each name what happened and the safe next action.
P0692: The 3D shelf is optional enhancement; grid/list offers feature-equivalent keyboard and screen-reader access.
P0693: Motion respects reduced-motion and does not block completion; skeletons preserve final geometry to control layout shift.
P0694: 12.3Reader and Classroom Controls
P0695: The reader prioritises page content, then navigation, then annotation. AI actions show privacy tier, exact outbound fields, estimated cost where available, and cancel/erase controls before transmission. Classroom screens separate aggregate administration from student-private reading state; unavailable/disabled institutional services explain the limitation rather than presenting a dead action.
P0696: 12.4Acceptance Evidence
P0697: Release design approval requires token implementation, removal of Inter as primary UI font, complete licensed icon inventory, automated contrast/name-role-value checks, manual keyboard and 200% zoom journeys, screen-reader sampling on Windows and macOS, reduced-motion verification, and representative screenshots from signed release-candidate builds. Headless synthetic screenshots are supporting test evidence only.
P0699: Kaizen refresh - 2026-09-10
P0700: Refresh status: Applied local remediation and current-source updates. Visual/render/browser inspection was skipped at the requester's direction and is NOT ASSESSED.
P0701: Source: docs/references/Ogma-Library_HLD_v2.1_2026-08-13.docx; repository head at evidence capture: e2be72fb332e
P0702: Current-source requirements, design, and test update
P0703: Preserve the source document styles; no new typeface, colour system, or visual restyle is claimed without visual QA.
P0704: Cover loading, empty, unavailable, malformed PDF, page overflow, no-results, permission-denied, and interrupted/retry reader states.
P0705: Treat physical accessibility, 3D shelf input, and platform-specific interaction as separate evidence obligations.
P0706: Verification and release posture
P0707: Current automated baseline: 42 architecture, 955 core, and 163 UI tests (1,160 total); shelf3d typecheck/build/performance baseline was green.
P0708: Keep release, PDF containment, signing, platform/accessibility, and reviewer evidence open until directly observed.
P0709: Operational control
P0710: Do not promote on automated green tests alone; require artifact hashes, signing evidence, rollback evidence, and supported-runtime proof.

P0022: 2Findings and Root Causes
P0023: Markdown truth evolved after DOCX generation; export scripts had no controlled allow-list or freshness check.
P0024: “Implemented,” “Done,” “As-built,” phase completion, and release acceptance were used as if equivalent.
P0025: Current implementation evidence was not rerun before July dependency/test statements were repeated.
P0026: Business-plan, website, finance, and design controls were implicit despite commercial and visual promises.
P0027: Registry and governance summaries were manually maintained and drifted from SRS identifiers.
P0028: Historical audit and anti-slop verdicts were presented without expiry or evidence scope.
P0029: Root cause: the project had strong document volume but no single evidence taxonomy and no atomic source → DOCX → export → render → hash release transaction.

P0033: 1.1Version 2.0 Baseline Note
P0034: This version documents the full product defined by the scope expansion of 2026-07-06: the single-user desktop product plus the classroom/LAN e-library (FR-LAN, FR-CLIENT, FR-ADMIN groups), the Extension SDK (FR-EXT), promoted premium-experience requirements (FR-UX-001 to FR-UX-008), multilingual scope (English + French at MVP; Spanish, Italian, German at final), and store distribution. It records implementation status honestly against the codebase: grand-plan Phases 00–18 are implemented; Phases 19–23 (hardening, performance acceptance, full i18n and accessibility audit, packaging and store submission, launch operations) are outstanding. The scope expansion carries a Change Impact Analysis entry in _registry/change-impact.yaml.
P0036: Confidential — Internal Use Only. This document is the intellectual property of Chwezi Core Systems. No part may be reproduced or disclosed without written permission.
P0038: 2Executive Summary
P0039: Ogma Library is a local-first, premium desktop application that turns a folder of Portable Document Format (PDF) files into a managed, searchable, beautiful personal library — and, in its classroom mode, into a shared school e-library served over the local network. It is not a PDF viewer with Artificial Intelligence (AI) features bolted on. It is a private, immersive library operating system in which scanning, metadata repair, three-dimensional browsing, reading progress, annotations, full-text and semantic search, explainable AI recommendations, and portable ownership reinforce one another as one system.
P0040: The product promise governs every requirement that follows:
P0041: Ogma Library gives readers private, durable, beautiful command over their PDF collections.
P0042: Privacy, durability, beauty, and command are acceptance criteria, each decomposed into measurable thresholds in Section 8 of this document and in the baselined Software Requirements Specification (SRS): cold start ≤ 3 s at P95 on the binding reference machine (NFR-OGMA-001), metadata search ≤ 150 ms at P95 on a 2,000-book catalogue (NFR-OGMA-003), crash-free session rate ≥ 99.5% (NFR-PROD-006), 0 bytes transmitted off-device on a default install, and 0 irreversible destructive operations.
P0043: Baseline v2.0 (this document) supersedes the v1.0 single-user baseline of 2026-05-30 in three ways:
P0044: Scope. The product now spans two deployment modes of one codebase (owner decision D-008): a zero-friction standalone mode with no login, and a classroom/LAN host mode in which a school computer serves the catalogue and PDF streams over TLS to student clients on the local network only, with school-managed AI keys, per-student privacy, quotas, and audit (FR-LAN, FR-CLIENT, FR-ADMIN groups). An Extension SDK (FR-EXT), promoted premium-experience requirements (FR-UX-001 to FR-UX-008), English + French at MVP with Spanish, Italian, and German at final, and distribution through Velopack, MSIX + Microsoft Store, notarized DMG + Mac App Store, and GitHub releases complete the expansion.
P0045: Market grounding. Section 11 records the researched competitive landscape (2026-07-06): no competitor combines Windows-first premium desktop, local-first zero-telemetry defaults, one-time licensing, local OCR + local embeddings behind a provider-neutral gateway, and a spatial 3D library metaphor — and no competitor has any classroom/LAN mode. Pricing is an owner decision pending, hypothesised at a one-time USD 149–249 license plus an optional ~USD 39/yr update plan, with education licensing as a second lane.
P0046: Honest implementation status. The codebase at C:\wamp64\www\Ogma-Library implements grand-plan Phases 00–18 — ingestion, catalogue, metadata enrichment with reversible write-back, the PDFium reader, FTS5 + semantic + hybrid search, the four-tier AI gateway with Privacy Center, the Three.js 3D shelf, OCR, and the complete LAN/classroom/school-admin track — verified by 800 executed automated cases in the 2026-08-13 canonical Release run. Phases 19–23 (security hardening, formal performance acceptance on reference hardware, es/it/de localization and the WCAG 2.2 AA audit, packaging/signing/store submission, and launch operations) are outstanding. All performance figures to date are trend-only; formal NFR acceptance occurs at Phase 20.
P0047: The engagement is Hybrid (Water-Scrum-Fall): the Waterfall SRS baseline was signed before development, and delivery proceeds Agile across the 24-phase grand plan summarized in Section 9. Ogma Library is owned by Chwezi Core Systems / Peter Bamuhigire. It serves readers, researchers, collectors, power librarians, and privacy-sensitive users who hold their own corpus on their own device — and students, teachers, and school administrators who share one collection on one network without surrendering it to a cloud service.
P0048: 3Problem Statement: Knowledge Trapped in a Folder of PDFs
P0049: 3.1The Core Failure
P0050: People who read seriously accumulate hundreds or thousands of PDF files over years — books, papers, manuals, reports, scans. The files sit in nested folders with names like scan_0042_final_v2.pdf. The operating system file browser can list them, but it cannot tell the reader what a file is, whether they have read it, where they stopped, what they highlighted, why they kept it, or which of their own documents answers a question they have right now. The knowledge exists, but it is not visible, not usable, and not intelligent. The collection grows faster than the reader's ability to command it, and the larger it gets, the more intimidating and less useful it becomes.
P0051: Generic PDF viewers do not solve this. They open one file at a time, treat the collection as invisible, lose reading position across sessions, store annotations inside the file or not at all, and offer no search across the whole corpus. Cloud reading services solve some of this but at the cost of ownership: they ingest the user's files into a proprietary container on someone else's server, which is unacceptable to a reader who treats their library as private property held on their own device.
P0052: 3.2The Institutional Failure
P0053: Schools and small institutions face the same failure at shared scale, plus three problems of their own. First, a school's PDF collection typically lives on one computer, so only one person at a time can use it; there is no e-library product that serves a folder of PDFs to every computer on a school's local network without a cloud subscription and an internet dependency. Second, when institutions do adopt AI search tools, the API keys, spend, and student data handling are uncontrolled — untenable where the users are minors subject to GDPR children's-consent rules, US COPPA/FERPA considerations, and the Uganda Data Protection and Privacy Act 2019 (owner decision D-003 targets all three regimes at the strictest common denominator). Third, student reading state — progress, notes, questions — either does not exist or is not private. No product on the researched market (Section 11) offers a LAN-only classroom library with school-managed AI keys, per-student privacy, quotas, and audit.
P0054: 3.3The Pain, Framed by Persona
P0055: Each of the 8 personas defined in Section 4 experiences the underlying failure through a different lens.
P0056: Persona
P0057: The pain in their words
P0058: What the failure costs them
P0059: Serious reader
P0060: "I know I started that book but I cannot find it or remember where I stopped."
P0061: Re-reading, abandoned books, a collection too large to enjoy.
P0062: Researcher
P0063: "The answer is somewhere in my own documents and I cannot get to it in time."
P0064: Intent-to-source latency measured in minutes, not seconds.
P0065: Collector
P0066: "Half my files have no title, no cover, and the wrong author, and fixing one risks corrupting the file."
P0067: Decayed metadata; fear of irreversible edits; no safe path to repair.
P0068: Power librarian
P0069: "I cannot see which files are duplicates, which are missing covers, or which have gone missing."
P0070: No maintenance surface for a large collection; silent rot.
P0071: Privacy-sensitive user
P0072: "I want recommendations from my own library without uploading my documents to anyone."
P0073: Forced choice between intelligence and privacy.
P0074: Student
P0075: "The library is on one computer in one room, and my notes vanish when someone else logs in."
P0076: No access from the lab or classroom; no private reading state.
P0077: Teacher
P0078: "I cannot see whether the class is actually reading the assigned shelf."
P0079: No class-level visibility; assignment by verbal instruction only.
P0080: School administrator
P0081: "I cannot give students AI search without handing out an API key I cannot control or afford."
P0082: Uncontrolled AI spend; unmanaged minors'-data exposure; no audit trail.
P0083: 3.4Why the Problem Persists
P0084: The four needs — visibility, usability, intelligence, and privacy — are usually delivered by different products that contradict one another. File browsers preserve privacy but offer no intelligence. Cloud reading services offer intelligence but sacrifice privacy and ownership. PDF editors edit files but do not manage collections. Classroom platforms manage students but do not serve a local PDF collection. The researched market (Section 11) confirms the gap concretely: the only premium one-time-license incumbents (DEVONthink 4, Bookends) are macOS-only, everything else is subscription or cloud-dependent, and no competitor has any classroom/LAN mode. No single product treats the user's own folder of PDFs as a first-class library that is simultaneously private, durable, beautiful, and commanded by the reader — on one machine or across one school network. That gap is the problem Ogma Library exists to close.
P0085: 4Product Vision and Principles
P0086: 4.1Product Promise
P0087: Ogma Library gives readers private, durable, beautiful command over their PDF collections.
P0088: The four nouns in the promise — private, durable, beautiful, command — are acceptance criteria, not adjectives. Privacy is verified by the privacy-tier and payload-preview requirements (FR-AI-004, FR-AI-005, FR-AI-011, FR-ADMIN-007); durability by the catalogue-of-record, backup, and reversibility requirements (FR-META-005, NFR-OGMA-008); beauty by the calm-control experience requirements (FR-UX-001 to FR-UX-008) and multi-mode browsing (FR-CAT-001); command by search within budget (NFR-OGMA-003, NFR-OGMA-004), reading progress, and the library health dashboard (FR-META-008).
P0089: 4.2The Three Core Promises
P0090: The product makes the library visible, usable, and intelligent. These promises map onto the feature groups in Section 6 and the success metrics in Section 8.
P0091: Promise
P0092: Definition
P0093: Delivered through
P0094: Visible
P0095: The collection renders as covers, spines, shelves, and metadata across multiple browsing modes, so the reader sees a library rather than a list of file names.
P0096: 3D shelf, grid, list, and directory views; covers and spine textures; full metadata inspection.
P0097: Usable
P0098: The reader resumes, navigates, annotates, searches, and measures reading without remembering file paths — alone or as an enrolled classroom profile.
P0099: Reading progress, bookmarks, highlights and notes, search, reading statistics, per-student private state.
P0100: Intelligent
P0101: An AI reading advisor recommends books from the reader's own collection (or the school's shared collection), with a stated reason and stated confidence, never as an opaque black box.
P0102: Provider-neutral AI gateway, explainable ranked recommendations, reading plans, host-proxied managed AI for classrooms.
P0103: 4.3Two Deployment Modes, One Codebase
P0104: Owner decision D-008 (2026-05-30) fixes the deployment model that shapes every architectural requirement:
P0105: Property
P0106: Standalone (home, MVP default)
P0107: Classroom / LAN host (school)
P0108: Login
P0109: None — the OS user account is the boundary
P0110: Admin-created student/teacher accounts with roles
P0111: AI key
P0112: Set by the user, in the local OS credential store
P0113: Set by the admin only, in the host's OS credential store; masked after entry; never transmitted to clients
P0114: AI calls
P0115: Made by the local app
P0116: Proxied by the host — a single egress chokepoint
P0117: Data
P0118: Local, per computer
P0119: Central on the host; clients cache for offline; the host is the source of truth
P0120: Network surface
P0121: No inbound listener
P0122: LAN only; no inbound internet listener; internet/remote access out of scope until post-V2, behind a fresh DPIA
P0123: 4.4The Seven Product Principles
P0124: These principles are binding design constraints. Every requirement in the SRS is checked against them, and a requirement that violates one is rejected at the verification gate.
P0125: Local-first by default. The catalogue, reading state, annotations, thumbnails, and indexes live on the user's machine (or the school's host) unless the user explicitly opts into off-device transmission. The default install transmits 0 bytes off-device.
P0126: AI is explainable. Every recommendation states why a book matched, which metadata or content supported the match, and the system's confidence. An AI output with no stated rationale is a defect, not a feature.
P0127: Files remain the user's files. Ogma augments the folder with a catalogue of record; it never replaces the folder, never moves files without consent, and never locks books into a proprietary container. Classroom clients never mutate host files or host metadata (FR-CLIENT-010).
P0128: Metadata is reversible. Any write-back to a PDF first creates a backup, shows a field-level diff, and permits restore (FR-META-005). A destructive operation with no restore path is prohibited — the pattern is backup, diff, verify, restore.
P0129: 3D is functional, not gimmick. The 3D shelf exists to aid discovery and spatial memory. Grid and list views remain first-class; no capability is reachable only through the 3D view, and an accessible fallback exists.
P0130: Offline remains useful. Reading, browsing, metadata editing, search, and local AI operate without an internet connection wherever technically possible. A classroom client that loses its host keeps local reading state and re-syncs on reconnect (FR-LAN-008, FR-CLIENT-011).
P0131: Premium means calm control. The product feels refined rather than busy and makes a large collection less intimidating rather than more. Premium is expressed as the measurable thresholds in Section 7 and the FR-UX group, never as marketing language.
P0132: 4.5Baseline v2.0 Construction Principles
P0133: The grand plan (codebase docs/plans/grand-plan/README.md §2) binds four further principles that baseline v2.0 adopts:
P0134: Multilingual by construction. No hard-coded user-facing string (lint-gated, pseudolocale-tested). MVP ships full English + French (FR-UX-004); Spanish, Italian, and German complete the launch scope at Phase 21.
P0135: Beautiful and iconified. Every primary surface carries an icon from the flat full-color premium icon system (owner decisions D-001, D-004, D-005: SVG masters from Flaticon Premium, PNG exports at 16/24/32/48 px). Icons always pair with a localized text/aria label; color is never the sole carrier of meaning.
P0136: Accessible as a gate. WCAG 2.2 Level AA — keyboard and screen-reader operability of all core flows (FR-UX-005) — is a release gate audited at Phase 21, not an aspiration.
P0137: Everything is tested. Each phase carries unit, integration, fault-injection, performance, and accessibility tests asserted against a fully synthetic golden corpus (owner decision D-007/CON-9); the codebase currently holds 800 executed automated cases in the 2026-08-13 canonical Release run across unit, headless-UI, and architecture layers.
P0138: 5Personas and Jobs to Be Done
P0139: Ogma Library serves 8 primary personas. The first three derive from the input specification; the next two expose design requirements a premium product must satisfy; the last three are the classroom personas added by the baseline v2.0 scope expansion. Each persona is defined by a primary job, a single measurable success signal, and the critical features that must exist for the persona to succeed. Section 8 grounds each success signal in a numeric threshold.
P0140: 5.1Persona Summary
P0141: Persona
P0142: Primary job
P0143: Product success signal
P0144: Critical features
P0145: Serious reader
P0146: Re-discover and continue books accumulated over years
P0147: Can find, resume, and browse books without remembering file names
P0148: 3D shelf, grid view, reading progress, virtual shelves, reading statistics
P0149: Researcher
P0150: Find the right document for a topic or question
P0151: Can move from intent to relevant source in seconds
P0152: Full-text search, semantic search, AI advisor, annotations, citation export
P0153: Collector
P0154: Correct and preserve metadata
P0155: Can repair, enrich, export, and write metadata safely
P0156: ISBN detection, Google Books / Open Library lookup, metadata editor, backups
P0157: Power librarian
P0158: Maintain a large collection over time
P0159: Can audit duplicates, missing metadata, unavailable files, and broken covers
P0160: Library health dashboard, duplicate detection, batch jobs
P0161: Privacy-sensitive user
P0162: Use AI without exposing documents unnecessarily
P0163: Can choose local-only or metadata-only AI modes
P0164: Privacy tiers, payload preview, local embeddings, explicit content-upload controls
P0165: Student (classroom client)
P0166: Read and search the school's shared collection from any LAN computer
P0167: Can join with a code, read a streamed book, and keep private notes
P0168: Classroom join, streamed reading, private per-profile state, explainable AI search
P0169: Teacher
P0170: Guide a class through a shared collection
P0171: Can see class-level reading dashboards and assign shelves
P0172: Shared shelves, class dashboards, role-scoped access
P0173: School administrator
P0174: Operate the shared library and control AI spend and privacy
P0175: Can publish content, enroll/revoke profiles, set AI quotas and tiers, audit usage
P0176: Host mode, entitlements, managed AI keys, quotas, audit export
P0177: 5.2Persona Narratives
P0178: 5.2.1Serious Reader
P0179: The serious reader has a folder of books built over many years and reads across several at once. Their failure mode is forgetting what they own and where they stopped. They succeed when they open Ogma, see their library as covers and spines, recognise a book on sight, and resume it at the exact page and scroll position they left. FR-UX-007 makes this a scripted, measured journey: locate a known book and resume reading within 60 s of app launch on reference hardware. The 3D shelf and grid view make the collection recognisable; reading progress and bookmarks make every book resumable; virtual shelves and reading statistics let them shape and reflect on their habit.
P0180: 5.2.2Researcher
P0181: The researcher arrives with a question, not a file name. They succeed when typing a natural-language query surfaces the right document — by title, by a note they wrote, by a passage in the text, or by semantic similarity — and when the result explains where it matched (FR-SEARCH-003). Full-text search, semantic search, the AI advisor, durable annotations, and citation export to BibTeX, RIS, and CSL JSON (FR-READ-013) are critical; without them the researcher's intent-to-source latency stays in minutes. Their signals are the 150 ms metadata-search and 500 ms full-text-search budgets (NFR-OGMA-003, NFR-OGMA-004) in Section 8.
P0182: 5.2.3Collector
P0183: The collector cares that each book carries a correct title, author, cover, and identifier, and that fixing one file never corrupts it. They succeed when Ogma detects an International Standard Book Number (ISBN), looks it up against Google Books and Open Library, proposes a reviewable merge with per-field provenance, and — only on confirmation — writes metadata back to the PDF after creating a backup and showing a field-level diff (FR-META-005). The collector's trust depends on reversibility; a single irreversible corruption breaks the relationship permanently, which is why write-back reversibility is a 100% gate in Section 8.
P0184: 5.2.4Power Librarian
P0185: The power librarian owns a large, living collection and needs a maintenance surface, not just a reader. They succeed when the library health dashboard (FR-META-008) surfaces duplicates, files missing covers or ISBNs, files that have gone unavailable, and failed thumbnail or extraction jobs — and when batch operations fix issues at scale with preview and undo (FR-CAT-005). Smart shelves (FR-CAT-006) and the Work/Edition model (FR-CAT-007) exist for this persona.
P0186: 5.2.5Privacy-Sensitive User
P0187: The privacy-sensitive user wants the intelligence of the AI advisor without surrendering their documents. They succeed when the default privacy tier transmits nothing, metadata-only mode is the cloud default (FR-AI-004), content-aware mode is opt-in per library or per query (FR-AI-005), and a payload preview shows the exact data that will leave the device before any off-device call. Local embeddings via Ollama (FR-AI-006) give semantic search with zero cloud upload, and the Privacy Center (FR-AI-011) exposes every provider key, recent call payload, and erasure control on one surface.
P0188: 5.2.6Student (Classroom Client)
P0189: The student sits at any computer on the school's network. They succeed when they join the school library with a QR join code (FR-LAN-005), browse the shared catalogue, read a streamed book without needing a full-file copy (FR-CLIENT-004), and keep reading progress, bookmarks, and notes that are private to their own profile — invisible to other students (FR-CLIENT-008). When they use AI search, they see the privacy-tier label and payload preview before anything leaves the device (FR-ADMIN-007), and they can delete their own AI history (FR-ADMIN-012). When the host is down, they keep their local reading state and re-sync on reconnect (FR-CLIENT-011).
P0190: 5.2.7Teacher
P0191: The teacher guides a class through a shared collection. They succeed when they can assign shared shelves and view class-level reading dashboards that are aggregate by design — per-student visibility is disclosed to students, never covert surveillance (FR-CLIENT-012). Their capabilities are role-scoped (FR-CLIENT-003): more than a student, less than an administrator.
P0192: 5.2.8School Administrator
P0193: The administrator operates the host. They succeed when they can publish or unpublish folders (FR-ADMIN-001) — unpublished content is never even enumerable by clients (FR-LAN-009) — enroll and revoke profiles (FR-ADMIN-003), store the school's AI key in the host OS credential store where no client and no other person can read it (FR-ADMIN-004), set the class privacy tier with metadata-only as the default (FR-ADMIN-006), enforce per-student quotas and rate limits (FR-ADMIN-008, FR-ADMIN-009), and export the audit log (FR-LAN-010). The administrator sees usage, cost, and quota utilization (FR-ADMIN-010) — never students' private reading content (per D-008).
P0194: 5.3Persona Research Status
P0195: The first three personas derive from the owner's input specification and the owner's own use; the classroom personas derive from the owner's expanded vision recorded in the grand plan. No external user research has yet validated them. Per the PRD-generation skill's Field-of-Dreams check, this PRD is research-pending: the Phase 23 public beta is the planned validation instrument, and willingness-to-pay validation is bound to the pricing decision (OQ-11, Section 10) before store submission.
P0196: 6Scope, MVP, and Release Tiers
P0197: 6.1Release Tier Model
P0198: Every feature carries a priority tier — MVP, V1, or V2 — fixed in _context/features.md (the authoritative, closed feature inventory) and preserved by stable requirement-ID prefixes throughout the SRS. The tiers define a shippable spine first, then progressive enrichment. Tier is a release concept, not a build-order concept: the codebase already implements most V1 and V2 functional groups (Section 9), but a tier ships only when its gates — including the Phase 19–21 hardening gates — pass.
P0199: MVP delivers a usable single-user library: choose a root, scan it, browse it in every view, repair metadata through online lookup, read with resume and annotations, search metadata within budget, and ask a provider-neutral AI advisor for explained recommendations — with AI fully disableable, in full English and French, with the premium-experience floor (FR-UX-001 to FR-UX-005, FR-UX-007, FR-UX-008).
P0200: V1 deepens the product: scan health reporting, bulk editing, provenance, confirmed reversible PDF write-back, password-protected and OCR PDFs, full-text/semantic/hybrid search, reading plans, local embeddings, query history and cost visibility, smart shelves, health dashboard, citation export, reading memory, annotation layers, Privacy Center, and in-app help.
P0201: V2 extends the product: split view, AI answer mode with local-evidence citation, the Work/Edition model, the full classroom/LAN product (FR-LAN, FR-CLIENT, FR-ADMIN), the Extension SDK (FR-EXT), and Spanish/Italian/German.
