P0251: 6.3MVP Acceptance Boundary
P0252: The MVP is accepted when a reader can, with no internet for the core path and no AI provider key: select a library root, scan a collection of 2,000 PDF files with the catalogue browsable while scanning continues (FR-UX-001, install-to-browsable ≤ 5 min on reference hardware), browse it in 3D shelf, grid, list, and directory views, open any book and resume its last position, annotate it with highlights and notes that survive reopen, search metadata within 150 ms at P95 (NFR-OGMA-003), operate every core flow by keyboard alone (FR-UX-005), switch between English and French at runtime (FR-UX-004), and — if and only if they choose to enable it — receive AI recommendations drawn from their own collection with a stated reason and confidence. AI being absent or disabled must never block any core reading, browsing, or metadata path (FR-AI-001).
P0253: 6.4Classroom Acceptance Boundary (V2)
P0254: The classroom product is accepted when: a default install runs no inbound listener and enabling host mode is explicit, reversible, and audited (FR-LAN-001); a student joins with a code, streams a published book over TLS without a full-file copy (FR-LAN-002, FR-CLIENT-004), and holds private per-profile reading state (FR-CLIENT-002, FR-CLIENT-008); the school's AI key never leaves the host and every student AI call routes through the host gateway under the class privacy tier, quotas, and rate limits (FR-ADMIN-004 to FR-ADMIN-009); revocation takes effect at next connection (FR-CLIENT-009); and the administrator can export the host audit log (FR-LAN-010). Access is LAN-only by owner decision D-008 — no inbound internet listener ships.
P0255: 6.5Explicitly Out of Scope
P0256: The following are deliberately excluded. The data model admits them later, but no committed requirement depends on them.
P0257: Cloud sync. The catalogue, reading state, and annotations remain on the device or the school host. The schema is designed to allow encrypted sync later; any future sync feature requires a fresh DPIA before shipping (Section 10, OQ-08 resolution).
P0258: Internet/remote access to a classroom host. Owner decision D-008 confines classroom access to the school's local network. Remote access is revisited post-V2 only, behind a fresh DPIA, because the users include minors.
P0259: EPUB and CBZ formats. The schema is format-extensible, but the product supports PDF only through V1 (Section 10, OQ-07 resolution).
P0260: PDF write-back of highlight annotations. Highlights and notes persist in the catalogue of record (FR-READ-008); only bibliographic metadata write-back is in scope, with backup and diff (FR-META-005). Any future annotation write-back inherits the same reversibility contract (OQ-03 resolution).
P0261: Local knowledge graph. Noted in the source material; not committed in this baseline.
P0262: These exclusions are ratified scope decisions (owner decisions D-002, D-007, D-008), not deferrals of unresolved design.
P0263: 7Feature Overview
P0264: This section summarises the product feature surface at module level. Each module group owns a stable requirement-ID prefix carried through the SRS, where the detailed stimulus-response requirements are authored; the inventory in _context/features.md is authoritative and closed. Implementation status is stated per group against the codebase as verified 2026-07-06 (_context/build-status.md); "implemented" means the code and its automated tests exist through grand-plan Phase 18, with formal performance acceptance still pending at Phase 20.
P0265: 7.1Core Module Groups
P0266: 7.1.1LIB — Library Setup and Scanning (implemented)
P0267: LIB establishes the library from the user's own folder. The user chooses a root, Ogma recursively discovers PDF files while respecting excluded folders, and each file receives a stable identity from its relative path plus a content hash so that renamed or moved files re-match rather than duplicate (FR-LIB-003). Removed or unavailable files are detected without deleting user metadata, shelves, notes, or progress (FR-LIB-004). Thumbnails and spine textures are generated in background jobs that keep the interface responsive, with visible and retryable failures (FR-LIB-005). Rescans are incremental (FR-LIB-006), and a scan health report surfaces failed, password-protected, unsupported, and metadata-gap files (FR-LIB-007). Code: IngestionOrchestrator, BookIdentityService, BookIngestionWorker.
P0268: 7.1.2CAT — Catalogue Browsing (implemented, including smart shelves and bulk edit)
P0269: CAT renders the collection as a browsable library across 3D shelf, grid, list, and directory views, all opening the same book detail and reader (FR-CAT-001). Sorting and filtering combine predictably and clear cleanly (FR-CAT-002). Virtual shelves let a book belong to multiple collections independent of file path (FR-CAT-003); smart shelves are saved filter definitions whose membership auto-updates (FR-CAT-006, implemented as SmartShelfCondition plus evaluator). Bulk editing is previewed before commit and undoable (FR-CAT-005). The Work/Edition model for grouping duplicate files and editions under one intellectual work (FR-CAT-007) is specified for V2 and not yet implemented as a distinct layer.
P0270: 7.1.3META — Metadata Enrichment (implemented, including write-back and health dashboard)
P0271: META repairs and enriches bibliographic metadata. Ogma detects ISBN-10 and ISBN-13 from filename, XMP, DocInfo, and extracted first pages, then normalises, validates, and ranks candidates (FR-META-001); looks them up in Google Books and Open Library with source, timestamp, and confidence stored (FR-META-002); and merges through a reviewable confidence policy (FR-META-003) with per-field provenance (FR-META-004). Write-back to the PDF occurs only after confirmation, with the original backed up and a field-level diff shown; failure leaves the original intact (FR-META-005, implemented via PdfWriteBackService on PDFsharp). Batch enrichment respects provider rate limits (FR-META-006). The library health dashboard aggregates duplicates, missing metadata, unavailable files, broken covers, and index gaps with per-finding remediation actions (FR-META-008, implemented as LibraryHealthService); a metadata quality score filters incomplete books (FR-META-007).
P0272: 7.1.4READ — PDF Reader (implemented except split view)
P0273: READ is the reading surface, rendered through a PDFium-backed adapter (PdfiumAdapter on PDFtoImage/SkiaSharp with a PageRenderCache). Books open from the catalogue and resume their last page and scroll position (FR-READ-001). Navigation, zoom, display modes, and full-screen (FR-READ-002 to FR-READ-005) persist per book. In-document text search highlights matches (FR-READ-006). Bookmarks (FR-READ-007) and highlights/notes (FR-READ-008) persist in the catalogue of record and reload accurately. Password-protected PDFs open via the OS credential flow (FR-READ-009); scanned PDFs are OCR-indexed as an optional background job on Tesseract 5.2.0, with results marked OCR-derived (FR-READ-010). Citation capture (FR-READ-011), citation and research export to BibTeX, RIS, CSL JSON, and Markdown digest (FR-READ-013), reading memory (FR-READ-014), and named annotation layers with per-layer show/hide and export (FR-READ-015) are implemented in OgmaLibrary.Reader. Split view (FR-READ-012, V2) is not yet implemented.
P0274: 7.1.5SEARCH — Search and Indexing (implemented)
P0275: SEARCH moves the reader from intent to source. Metadata search responds while typing within 150 ms at P95 on 2,000 books (FR-SEARCH-001, budget NFR-OGMA-003). The FTS5 index covers extracted text, notes, tags, descriptions, and tables of contents (FR-SEARCH-002); results explain their match location (FR-SEARCH-003); semantic search supports natural-language intent (FR-SEARCH-004); hybrid ranking blends exact match, recency, reading status, rating, and semantic similarity (FR-SEARCH-005); and an index manager exposes progress, indexed books, pending OCR, failed extraction, and index size (FR-SEARCH-006). Code: FtsIndexService, SemanticSearchService, HybridRankingService, IndexManagerService.
P0276: 7.1.6AI — AI Advisor (implemented except answer mode)
P0277: AI is the intelligence layer, optional by construction: the user can disable it completely, no provider key is required, and no AI UI blocks core reading (FR-AI-001 — the default provider is AiDisabledProvider). Providers — OpenAI, Anthropic-compatible, DeepSeek-compatible, Ollama/local — sit behind one contract (FR-AI-002), with architecture tests enforcing a single HTTP-egress chokepoint. Recommendations arrive as ranked cards with explanation and confidence (FR-AI-003). Metadata-only is the default cloud tier (FR-AI-004); content-aware is opt-in with a privacy label and payload preview (FR-AI-005); local embeddings run without cloud upload (FR-AI-006). Reading plans (FR-AI-007), locally stored deletable query history (FR-AI-009), per-call cost estimates (FR-AI-010), and the Privacy Center — keys, recent calls with exact payloads, per-call tier, erasure controls, audit export (FR-AI-011) — are implemented. Answer mode citing local evidence (FR-AI-008, V2) is a deliberate NotImplementedException stub in AdvisorService.
P0278: 7.2Classroom Module Groups (V2 product, implemented in code)
P0279: 7.2.1LAN — Library Host Mode (implemented, Phase 16)
P0280: An opt-in host serves the catalogue and PDF streams to the local network only. The default install runs no inbound listener; enabling host mode is explicit, reversible, and audited (FR-LAN-001). The host serves over TLS (FR-LAN-002) with certificate provisioning and a trust-on-first-use client trust store with fingerprint display (FR-LAN-004), advertises via mDNS (FR-LAN-003), authenticates clients with per-profile credentials and QR join codes (FR-LAN-005), streams PDF content by range request with server-side path validation against the published root (FR-LAN-006), manages and revokes sessions (FR-LAN-007), serves only admin-published folders/shelves — unpublished content is never enumerable (FR-LAN-009) — and keeps an exportable activity audit log (FR-LAN-010). Host loss is non-destructive to clients (FR-LAN-008). Code: KestrelHostModeListener.
P0281: 7.2.2CLIENT — Classroom Client Mode (implemented, Phase 17)
P0282: Clients discover or enter a host, browse the shared catalogue (FR-CLIENT-001), and hold per-student profiles with private reading state (FR-CLIENT-002) under student/teacher/administrator roles (FR-CLIENT-003). Reading streams content — no full-file copy required (FR-CLIENT-004) — with an offline cache under an explicit size limit and purge control (FR-CLIENT-005). Reading state syncs with latest-writer-wins per field and no silent data loss (FR-CLIENT-006). Client search runs against the host index with the same match-location explanations as local search (FR-CLIENT-007). Students' reading state and AI history are invisible to other students (FR-CLIENT-008); revocation applies at next connection while local notes survive (FR-CLIENT-009); clients are read-only against the shared library (FR-CLIENT-010); connection state is shown, degrading to the local library when the host is unreachable (FR-CLIENT-011); teacher dashboards are class-level aggregate with disclosed visibility (FR-CLIENT-012); and entitlements govern which shelves and books a profile may open (FR-CLIENT-013). Code: ClassroomClient.
P0283: 7.2.3ADMIN — School Administration and Managed AI (implemented, Phase 18)
P0284: The administrator publishes and unpublishes folders (FR-ADMIN-001), curates shared shelves (FR-ADMIN-002), and enrolls, edits, and revokes profiles (FR-ADMIN-003). The school AI key lives in the host OS credential store, masked after entry and never transmitted to clients (FR-ADMIN-004); every student AI query routes through the host AI gateway (FR-ADMIN-005) under a class default of metadata-only (FR-ADMIN-006), with the privacy-tier label and payload preview shown to the student before any off-device call (FR-ADMIN-007). Per-student and per-class quotas (FR-ADMIN-008) and per-student rate limits (FR-ADMIN-009) are enforced; the dashboard shows usage, cost, and quota utilization (FR-ADMIN-010); classroom answer mode cites only host-catalogue evidence (FR-ADMIN-011); students can delete their own AI history (FR-ADMIN-012) and the administrator can purge all student AI history for the institution (FR-ADMIN-013). Code: SchoolAdmin, including DPIA screening and audit export.
P0285: 7.3Extension and Experience Groups
P0286: 7.3.1EXT — Extension SDK (V2, specified; not yet implemented)
P0287: Plugin interfaces for metadata, export, OCR, and AI providers, with all extension off-device traffic routed through the AI gateway privacy tiers (FR-EXT-001); a documented local read API over the catalogue and search index (FR-EXT-002); and importers for Zotero, Calibre, and Goodreads plus theme and icon packs (FR-EXT-003). This group is the open-source-readiness surface and lands with Phase 23 extension points.
P0288: 7.3.2UX — Premium Experience Requirements (en/fr implemented; es/it/de and the WCAG audit pending Phase 21)
P0289: The premium thresholds formerly stranded in PRD prose are first-class requirements: zero-mandatory-configuration onboarding with install-to-browsable ≤ 5 min for 2,000 PDFs (FR-UX-001); designed loading, empty, error, and in-progress states on every primary surface — no blank panels (FR-UX-002); a command palette over all catalogue, reader, and search commands (FR-UX-003); runtime en/fr language switch without restart, es/it/de at V2 (FR-UX-004); full keyboard operability of all core flows (FR-UX-005); offline in-app contextual help and searchable manual (FR-UX-006, V1); the 60 s locate-and-resume journey (FR-UX-007); and light/dark/system themes with reader typography controls and WCAG 2.2 AA contrast in both themes (FR-UX-008).
P0290: 8Premium Positioning
P0291: Ogma Library is premium, world-class product work. In this PRD, premium is never a marketing adjective: it is expressed only as measurable quality, trust, onboarding, support, usability, reliability, and privacy, each tied to a threshold that passes or fails at a release gate. Since baseline v2.0, every premium threshold that previously lived in this section's prose is a first-class requirement in the FR-UX group or an NFR-OGMA budget, so the premium promise gates releases mechanically rather than rhetorically. The unifying expression remains the premium-means-calm-control principle from Section 3.
P0292: 8.1The Premium Buyer and the Expensive Problem
P0293: Two buyers, two decision paths:
P0294: The individual buyer (serious reader, researcher, collector) is their own economic buyer, user, and technical approver. Their expensive problem is a personal corpus accumulated over years that they can no longer command; their rejection triggers are subscription pricing, cloud lock-in, and any hint that an edit could corrupt a file. Proof they can inspect before purchase: the payload preview, the reversibility contract (backup, diff, verify, restore), the export formats, and signed store-distributed builds.
P0295: The education buyer separates roles: the school administrator or head is the economic buyer, students and teachers are daily users, an ICT teacher is the technical approver, and minors'-data compliance is the risk owner's veto. Their expensive problem is a physical collection only one person can use at a time, plus AI tools whose spend and student-data handling they cannot control. Proof required: LAN-only architecture with no inbound internet listener (D-008), the managed-AI key model where the key is never disclosed after entry, per-student privacy guarantees, quota and cost dashboards, and exportable audit logs — each an implemented requirement (FR-ADMIN-004 to FR-ADMIN-010, FR-LAN-010), not a brochure claim.
P0296: 8.2Value Made Visible Through Usable UX
P0297: Premium value is visible the moment a large folder becomes a recognisable library. The metric is comprehension speed and operability, not aesthetics.
P0298: Quality dimension
P0299: Requirement and threshold
P0300: Onboarding
P0301: Zero mandatory configuration beyond choosing the library root; install-to-browsable ≤ 5 min for 2,000 PDFs on W-REF-01 (FR-UX-001).
P0302: Locate and resume
P0303: A known book found and resumed at last position within 60 s of launch, as a scripted journey on reference hardware (FR-UX-007).
P0304: State completeness
P0305: Every primary surface defines loading, empty, error, and in-progress states; a blank panel is a defect (FR-UX-002).
P0306: Keyboard command
P0307: All core flows operable end-to-end by keyboard alone (FR-UX-005); a command palette exposes all catalogue, reader, and search commands (FR-UX-003).
P0308: Accessibility
P0309: WCAG 2.2 Level AA conformance as a release gate, audited at Phase 21 (FR-UX-005, FR-UX-008).
P0310: Responsiveness under load
P0311: Background scanning, thumbnailing, and indexing never block the interface; catalogue interactive during jobs on the 2,000-book corpus (FR-LIB-005).
P0312: Language
P0313: Runtime English/French switch without restart at MVP; Spanish, Italian, German at final (FR-UX-004).
P0314: 8.3Buyer and User Proof
P0315: Premium products earn trust through inspectable proof, not claims.
P0316: Every metadata write-back shows a field-level diff and a backup reference before commit (FR-META-005).
P0317: The payload preview shows the exact data leaving the device before any off-device AI call, for individuals (FR-AI-005) and for students (FR-ADMIN-007).
P0318: The Privacy Center lists provider keys, recent AI calls with exact payloads, per-call privacy tier, and erasure controls, with audit-log export (FR-AI-011).
P0319: The library health dashboard and index manager surface the true state of the collection — duplicates, gaps, failures — rather than hiding it (FR-META-008, FR-SEARCH-006).
P0320: The school dashboard shows AI usage, cost, and quota utilization to the buyer who pays for the key (FR-ADMIN-010).
P0321: 8.4Trust and Privacy as Measurable Quality
P0322: Trust dimension
P0323: Threshold
P0324: Default transmission
P0325: 0 bytes leave the device on a default install; AI disabled by default, no provider key required (FR-AI-001).
P0326: Payload transparency
P0327: 100% of off-device AI calls present a payload preview before transmission; a call without one is a defect.
P0328: Erasure
P0329: AI query history and embeddings fully erasable on request, verified by audit-log entry plus absence on re-inspection (FR-AI-009); students erase their own history (FR-ADMIN-012).
P0330: Reversibility
P0331: 0 irreversible destructive operations; every destructive metadata or catalogue operation has a restore path (NFR-OGMA-008 for annotation durability).
P0332: Classroom privacy
P0333: A student's reading state and AI history are never visible to other students (FR-CLIENT-008); teacher dashboards are aggregate with disclosed visibility (FR-CLIENT-012).
P0334: 8.5Service Quality, Reliability, and Distribution
P0335: Reliability. Crash-free session rate ≥ 99.5% over any rolling 30-day window of opted-in telemetry; any reproducible crash that loses unsaved annotation or catalogue state is a release blocker regardless of the aggregate (NFR-PROD-006).
P0336: Performance. The binding P95 budgets on W-REF-01 — cold start ≤ 3 s, catalogue load ≤ 2 s, metadata search ≤ 150 ms, full-text search ≤ 500 ms warm, page turn ≤ 100 ms cached, 3D shelf ≥ 60 FPS at 500 books — are Section 8's release gates (NFR-OGMA-001 to NFR-OGMA-006).
P0337: Durability and migration. Schema migrations are idempotent and back up the catalogue before running; the codebase carries 10 EF Core migrations under this contract.
P0338: Distribution integrity. Builds ship signed: Velopack direct, MSIX through the Microsoft Store, notarized DMG through the Mac App Store, and GitHub releases (Phase 22). Visual identity uses the flat full-color premium icon system with localized accessible labels (D-001, D-004, D-005).
P0339: Support surface. Offline contextual help and a searchable manual in-app (FR-UX-006); Phase 23 establishes support runbooks and incident response before launch.
P0340: 8.6Pricing Power
P0341: The competitive analysis in Section 11 shows the premium one-time-license lane (DEVONthink 4 at USD 199–299) is macOS-only, while Windows users face subscription fatigue. The pricing hypothesis — one-time USD 149–249 plus an optional ~USD 39/yr update plan, with a separate education licensing lane for host + seats — is defensible precisely because the thresholds above are inspectable. It remains an owner decision pending (OQ-11, Section 10).
P0342: 8.7What Premium Is Not
P0343: Ogma Library does not express premium through superlatives, undefined claims of speed or polish, or feature volume. A requirement asserting the product is "fast", "intuitive", "reliable", or "seamless" without a measured threshold is rejected at the verification gate. Premium here is the sum of the thresholds above, governed by calm control.
P0344: 9Success Metrics
P0345: Every metric below is a measurable threshold with a pass/fail oracle. Performance budgets are binding at P95 on the reference machine W-REF-01 (Intel Core i5-10210U, 8 GB DDR4, SATA SSD, Intel UHD 620, Windows 10 22H2), with M-REF-01 (MacBook Air M1, 8 GB) as the macOS check; the Windows value is the binding gate (_context/reference-hardware.md, decision CON-1). Where an NFR-PROD domain default and an NFR-OGMA budget address the same attribute, NFR-OGMA is binding for release gates at MVP scale (2,000 books) and the NFR-PROD figure is the V1 large-library target (50,000 items) with its own gate at Phase 20.
P0346: Evidence status: all measurements taken before Phase 20 are trend-only (developer hardware and CI). Only Phase 20 measurements on W-REF-01/M-REF-01 or owner-approved equivalents constitute formal NFR acceptance evidence. No metric below is currently claimed as formally passed.

P0497: One gap remains open in this section: the real-world ISBN precision/recall floor (Collector table). It is assigned to the Phase 20 acceptance work, where the synthetic-corpus oracle is complemented by measured figures; every other threshold above is bound to a ratified budget or an identified requirement.
P0498: 10Build Phases — The 24-Phase Grand Plan
P0499: Delivery follows the 24-phase grand plan (docs/plans/grand-plan/ in the codebase), which supersedes this PRD's original 8-phase roadmap. The original Phase 0–7 spine is fully contained within it: spikes/decisions became Phases 00–01, catalogue foundation 04–06, metadata/health 07, reader 08–09, search 10–11, AI 12–13, the 3D shelf 14, and polish/launch 21–23. The grand plan adds what the original roadmap deferred: the design and icon system, the LAN/classroom product, the hardening tracks, store distribution, full localization, and post-launch operations. Phase numbers are permanent; estimates are engineer-weeks for a 3–5 person team; the indicative total is ~75 engineer-weeks after the SRS baseline.
P0500: The Hybrid gate still applies: the Waterfall SRS baseline was signed before development, the hybrid-synchronization skill runs before any Phase 07 agile artifact in this documentation engine, and python -m engine validate Ogma-Library must pass the hybrid gate.
P0501: 10.1Status Summary (verified 2026-07-06 against the codebase)
P0502: Implemented: Phases 00–18. All functional module groups — LIB, CAT, META, READ (except FR-READ-012), SEARCH, AI (except FR-AI-008), LAN, CLIENT, ADMIN — exist in code with 800 executed automated cases in the 2026-08-13 canonical Release run, English + French resources, and 10 EF Core migrations (latest: Phase18SchoolAdminTables).
P0503: Outstanding: Phases 19–23. Security hardening, formal performance acceptance, es/it/de localization and the WCAG 2.2 AA audit, packaging/signing/store submission, and beta/launch operations. No packaging configuration exists in the repository yet.
P0504: Evidence caveat. All performance numbers to date are trend-only; formal NFR acceptance on W-REF-01/M-REF-01 happens at Phase 20.

P0636: 10.9Release-Tier Mapping
P0637: The MVP gateable subset is Phases 00–10 plus 12 (metadata-only AI), 14, en/fr localization, the icon system, and the applicable core of 19–23; V1 adds Phases 11, 13, and 15 capabilities; V2 ships split view, answer mode, the hardened classroom product (16–18), the Extension SDK, and es/it/de. Building ahead of tier (Phases 11–18 already implemented) does not move a tier's release gates: nothing ships to any tier until Phases 19–21 pass for that tier's surface.
P0638: 10.10Gate Discipline
P0639: Each phase carries a Definition of Done gated against measurable thresholds; the product owner ratifies each phase gate; and any change to a baselined FR/NFR/CTRL requires a Change Impact Analysis entry with a rollback plan before acceptance. Two engineering items ride the outstanding phases: the OGMA0001 hard-coded-string Roslyn analyzer is not yet built (the rule is enforced by convention plus pseudolocale tests), and the repository's own status docs (CLAUDE.md, CHANGELOG.md) and duplicate ADR trees need reconciliation before the Phase 22 release audit.
P0640: 11Open Questions and Decision Record
P0641: Version 0.1 of this PRD listed 8 open questions blocking the SRS baseline. All 8 are now closed: owner decision D-007 (2026-05-30, recorded in docs/plans/grand-plan/DECISIONS.md) ratified the Phase 00 decision closure, docs/governance/REFERENCE-HARDWARE.md (decision CON-1) fixed the performance anchors, and D-008 fixed the deployment and data-location model. This section restates each original question with its recorded resolution, then lists the 2 decisions that remain genuinely open. The former EF Core alignment question is closed by ADR-0016.
P0642: 11.1Closed Decisions (OQ-01 to OQ-08)
P0643: ID
P0644: Question
P0645: Resolution
P0646: Decided where
P0647: OQ-01
P0648: .NET 10 LTS vs .NET 8
P0649: Closed: .NET 10 LTS. The codebase targets net10.0 with warnings-as-errors.
P0650: ADR-0001, reconfirmed by D-007.
P0651: OQ-02
P0652: PDFium wrapper selection
P0653: Closed: candidates fixed as PDFtoImage (sungaila) and Docnet.Core, both MIT + PDFium BSD and store-redistributable; the Phase 01 benchmark selected PDFtoImage, and the shipped reader runs PdfiumAdapter on PDFtoImage/SkiaSharp.
P0654: D-007 (candidates), ADR-0004 amendment (winner).
P0655: OQ-03
P0656: Annotation write-back strategy
P0657: Closed: highlights and notes persist only in the catalogue of record (FR-READ-008), implemented in OgmaLibrary.Reader. Any future PDF annotation write-back inherits the backup-diff-verify-restore contract of FR-META-005.
P0658: Phase 00 decision closure under D-007.
P0659: OQ-04
P0660: Thumbnail and spine-texture storage
P0661: Closed: generated images live in the sidecar asset folder on disk, referenced by the catalogue — implemented as the Phase 04 sidecar layout.
P0662: Phase 00 closure under D-007; grand-plan Phase 04.
P0663: OQ-05
P0664: Full-text search in the MVP
P0665: Closed: FTS5 is implemented (Phase 10, FtsIndexService) and Phase 10 sits inside the MVP gateable subset; FR-SEARCH-001 metadata search remains the MVP-tier requirement and FTS5 the V1 badge.
P0666: Phase 00 closure under D-007; grand plan §4.
P0667: OQ-06
P0668: OCR in the MVP
P0669: Closed: OCR is a V1 optional background job (FR-READ-010), implemented on Tesseract 5.2.0 at Phase 15 with results marked OCR-derived. MVP search scope is the native PDF text layer.
P0670: Phase 00 closure under D-007.
P0671: OQ-07
P0672: EPUB and CBZ support
P0673: Closed as a scope decision: PDF only through V1; the catalogue schema stays format-extensible; revisit post-V1. No phase gate is blocked.
P0674: Phase 00 closure under D-007.
P0675: OQ-08
P0676: Cloud sync
P0677: Closed: excluded from MVP and V1. Multi-machine sharing is delivered instead as the LAN-only classroom host (D-008) with no inbound internet listener; internet/remote access is out of scope until post-V2 and then only behind a fresh DPIA. Any future encrypted sync also requires a DPIA before shipping.
P0678: D-008, D-002.
P0679: Related closures recorded for traceability: reference hardware (CON-1: W-REF-01/M-REF-01 and the NFR-OGMA budgets), command-palette set (CON-4), Work/Edition rules (CON-5), provider trust weights (CON-8), synthetic golden corpus (CON-9), jurisdictions (D-003: Uganda DPPA 2019 + EU/UK GDPR + USA, strictest common denominator), icon direction and vendor (D-001, D-004, D-005), and the Avalonia community licence (D-006).
P0680: 11.2Genuinely Open Decisions
P0681: 11.2.1OQ-09: Premium Icon Procurement
P0682: Decision needed: Purchase and deliver the flat full-color premium icon sets (Flaticon Premium, SVG masters per D-004/D-005) named in the per-phase icon manifests and consolidated in _icons/MASTER-MANIFEST.md.
P0683: Status: Placeholders are in use throughout the implemented UI. By policy this is a release blocker: no store submission ships on placeholder icons, and free-tier Flaticon assets would force an in-app attribution screen that Premium licensing avoids.
P0684: Owner: Peter Bamuhigire (procurement); design/UX lead (manifest completeness).
P0685: Deadline: Before the Phase 22 packaging gate opens.
P0686: 11.2.2OQ-10: EF Core Alignment — Closed
P0687: The inspected baseline uses EF Core SQLite 10.0.9, Microsoft.Extensions 10.0.9, and 11 catalogue migrations on net10.0. The 2026-08-13 Release test run passed 800/800 cases and the dependency audit reported no vulnerable package. ADR-0016 records the decision and its continuing upgrade gates.
P0688: 11.2.3OQ-11: Pricing and Licensing Model
P0689: Decision needed: Ratify the pricing hypothesis from Section 11: a one-time license in the USD 149–249 band, an optional annual update plan at ~USD 39/yr, and a separate education licensing lane for the classroom host + seats.
P0690: Impact if unresolved: Store listings (Phase 22), the education sales motion, and the business case cannot be finalised; willingness-to-pay validation (Section 4 research-pending status) has no anchor to test against.
P0691: Recommended default: Adopt the hypothesised band for beta-cycle validation with target-level buyers, including at least one school buyer for the education lane; fix the final figure from beta evidence.
P0692: Owner: Peter Bamuhigire.
P0693: Deadline: Before Phase 22 store submission; education-lane pricing may trail to Phase 23 launch if the V2 classroom release ships later.
P0695: The 2 open decisions above require review and sign-off by Peter Bamuhigire. Neither blocks Phases 19–21 from starting; both block a commercially complete Phase 22 release.
P0696: 12Competitive Landscape
P0697: This section grounds the product's positioning in web research conducted 2026-07-06 across 11 competing products: Zotero 7, Calibre, DEVONthink 4, Readwise Reader, Bookends, Papers/ReadCube, Mendeley, PDF Expert, Recoll, Anytype, and Obsidian (_context/competitive-analysis.md). Pricing figures are vendor-published as of 2026-07. Competitor performance figures circulating in user communities are estimates and are deliberately not asserted here; Ogma's own budgets are those anchored in Section 8.
P0698: 12.1The Market Gap
P0699: The reviewed set did not reveal one product combining the five proposed attributes: Windows-first premium desktop, local-first zero-telemetry default, a one-time-license hypothesis, local OCR plus provider-neutral AI, and a spatial library metaphor. The same review did not observe a directly comparable classroom/LAN offer. These are dated observations, not proof of an uncontested market; buyer interviews and a refreshed competitor screen are required before pricing or public claims are approved.

P0724: 20–120/yr storage
P0725: Obsidian
P0726: Free local; paid sync
P0727: Sync 4/mo
P0728: Pricing hypothesis (owner decision pending, OQ-11): a one-time license in the USD 149–249 band plus an optional annual update plan at ~USD 39/yr — undercutting DEVONthink while being the only Windows-first entrant in that band — with education/classroom licensing (LAN host + seats) as a second, uncontested revenue lane.
P0729: 12.32026 Table Stakes a Premium Buyer Assumes
P0730: The researched products define the floor a premium entrant must clear, and Ogma meets each with an identified requirement:
P0731: Metadata auto-extraction with ISBN/DOI lookup and citation export in BibTeX/RIS/CSL JSON — bar set by Zotero and Bookends; met by FR-META-001 to FR-META-003 and FR-READ-013.
P0732: Durable annotation with export — bar set by Mendeley and PDF Expert; met by FR-READ-008 and FR-READ-015.
P0733: Full-text search at collection scale — bar set by DEVONthink and Recoll; met by FR-SEARCH-002 within the 500 ms warm budget (NFR-OGMA-004).
P0734: AI features with a stated privacy posture — DEVONthink 4's optional local Ollama is the reference implementation; Readwise Ghostreader shows demand; Mendeley's cloud-only "Ask My Library" draws privacy complaints; met and exceeded by the four-tier model (FR-AI-001 to FR-AI-006).
P0735: Dark mode, configurable reading typography, and data export with no lock-in — met by FR-UX-008 and the files-remain-the-user's-files principle.
P0736: 12.4Differentiators Mapped to Requirements
P0737: Each differentiator below is specified as a verifiable requirement, not marketing prose:
P0738: #

P0740: Strongest competitor position
P0741: Ogma requirement basis

P0744: Not observed in the reviewed set; validate with buyer research

P0770: 12.5Sentiment Lessons (what users punish)
P0771: Subscription fatigue. Readwise at USD 119.88/yr and PDF Expert's annual pricing draw persistent complaints — supporting the one-time-license positioning of OQ-11.
P0772: Learning-curve punishment. Anytype's object model, Obsidian's plugin maze, and DEVONthink's dense interface show that capability without calm onboarding is punished — supporting FR-UX-001's zero-mandatory-configuration requirement.
P0773: Trust destruction. Mendeley's retirement of mobile and collaboration features and opaque AI providers (Ghostreader) show cloud dependence and unexplained AI destroy trust permanently — supporting explainable AI (FR-AI-003), the Privacy Center (FR-AI-011), and the no-lock-in export guarantees.
P0774: 12.6Competitive Risks
P0775: Two risks temper this analysis. First, DEVONthink or another incumbent could ship a Windows port or a classroom mode faster than Ogma completes Phases 19–23; the mitigation is that the classroom track is already implemented in code and the moat is the combination, not any single feature. Second, pricing anchors move — the figures above must be re-verified before the OQ-11 decision is ratified, and any figure older than the decision date is not evidence.

P0043: Proposed claim
P0044: Permitted wording now

No fabricated testimonial, logo, user count, rating, security badge, compliance seal, performance number, or availability date is allowed.
P0062: 4Website Quality, Analytics, and Launch Gate
P0063: Typography: Spectral for editorial headings, Public Sans for body/interface copy, JetBrains Mono for technical evidence. Palette: ink navy, oxblood, vellum, and muted brass; Inter and other banned generic primaries are prohibited.
P0064: Accessibility: WCAG 2.2 AA target; keyboard complete; visible focus; 200% zoom without loss; reduced motion; meaningful alt text; form errors associated with fields.
P0065: Performance budgets at P75 mobile: LCP ≤2.5 s, INP ≤200 ms, CLS ≤0.1; JavaScript ≤180 KB compressed on brochure pages and optimized images with explicit dimensions.
P0066: SEO: unique title/description, canonical URL, sitemap, robots policy, structured Product/SoftwareApplication data only when the corresponding claims are approved.
P0067: Analytics: consent-dependent, content-free events for CTA view/click, form start/submit/error, evidence-page view, and qualified pilot request. Never capture document titles, search terms, or AI payloads.
P0068: Download integrity: signed artifacts only, SHA-256 displayed, version/OS/architecture explicit, release notes and rollback/support links adjacent.
P0069: Launch requires automated accessibility/performance checks, manual keyboard/screen-reader sampling, broken-link scan, privacy/legal/content approval, analytics validation, and a rollbackable deployment. A website build or hosted deployment is NOT ASSESSED in this workspace.

P0021: Classification
P0022: Confidential — Internal Use Only
P0023: Version 1.0 split risks across two formats (bulleted security risks, tabular delivery risks). Version 2.0 normalises all risks into one table, updates every status against the as-built evidence in _context/build-status.md and the codebase git history, and adds six risks (RISK-018 through RISK-023) arising from the classroom scope, the delivery model, and the open build items.
P0024: 1.11. Scoring Method
P0025: Likelihood (L) and Impact (I) each 1–5 (1 = Rare/Negligible, 3 = Possible/Moderate, 5 = Almost Certain/Catastrophic). Score = L × I.
P0026: Tolerance: 1–4 Low, 5–9 Medium, 10–15 High, 16–25 Critical. Scores of 10+ require a named mitigation with a dated verification point.
P0027: Status values: Open (mitigation pending), Mitigated — verification pending (controls implemented in code; adversarial or formal verification scheduled), Retired (risk event can no longer occur; evidence cited), Accepted (owner-recorded acceptance).
P0028: Ratings shown are post-mitigation (residual). Owners are roles per _context/stakeholders.md.

P0037: This Software Requirements Specification (SRS) is baseline v2.0 of the Ogma Library engagement. It supersedes the signed v1.0 baseline of 2026-05-30 under a Change Impact Analysis covering the classroom/LAN scope expansion (_registry/change-impact.yaml). Baseline v2.0 specifies the full product — the standalone personal library plus the Library Host, Classroom Client, and School Administration modes — and records per-requirement implementation status against the codebase at C:\wamp64\www\Ogma-Library as verified in _context/build-status.md (2026-07-06). Verification and Validation (V&V) follow IEEE Std 1012. Terminology follows IEEE Std 610.12.
P0039: \newpage
P0040: 21. Introduction
P0041: 2.11.1 Purpose of This Software Requirements Specification
P0042: This Software Requirements Specification (SRS) defines the functional, non-functional, data, security, and privacy requirements for Ogma Library, a local-first desktop application that converts a collection of Portable Document Format (PDF) files into a managed, searchable personal library, and — in its opt-in networked modes — into a shared classroom e-library on a Local Area Network (LAN). The document conforms to ISO/IEC/IEEE 29148:2018, the governing standard for requirements processes and requirement quality; the withdrawn IEEE Std 830 is cited as heritage only. This SRS is the requirements baseline the product owner signs off under the Hybrid (Water-Scrum-Fall) methodology recorded in _context/methodology.md.
P0043: Version 2.0 differs from the signed v1.0 baseline in three ways:
P0044: Coverage: v1.0 specified 47 functional requirements across 6 modules; v2.0 specifies 101 functional requirements across 11 modules, adding the LAN Library Host (LAN), Classroom Client (CLIENT), School Administration and Managed AI (ADMIN), Extension (EXT), and Premium Experience (UX) modules and promoting the v1.0 "world-class additions" prose into identified requirements.
P0045: Grounding: every requirement carries an implementation status (Implemented, Partial, or Not started) verified against the codebase per _context/build-status.md, and cites the real test class that verifies it where one exists.
P0046: Anchoring: every performance budget is bound to the defined reference hardware W-REF-01 / M-REF-01 (_context/reference-hardware.md), closing the v1.0 reference-hardware context gap and the NFR scale contradictions.
P0047: The intended audience is the product design, engineering, Artificial Intelligence (AI), Quality Assurance (QA), and launch-planning team identified in the stakeholder register, plus — for the classroom scope — the school administrator role that operates a deployed host.
P0048: 2.21.2 Business Goals
P0049: This SRS traces every functional requirement to at least one of the following business goals derived from the product vision. The goals anchor the per-requirement traceability table in Section 8. The classroom scope expansion (vision, 2026-07-06) applies the same 5 goals to the shared LAN deployment: a school library must be equally visible, usable, intelligent, durable, and private for each enrolled student.
P0050: BG-001 Visible library. Make a PDF collection visible through covers, shelves, metadata, and multiple browsing modes (3D shelf, grid, list, directory).
P0051: BG-002 Usable library. Make the collection usable through reading progress, bookmarks, annotations, search, and statistics — locally and, in classroom mode, from any LAN computer.
P0052: BG-003 Intelligent library. Make the collection intelligent through an AI reading advisor that recommends books from the user's own (or the school's shared) collection with explanation and confidence.
P0053: BG-004 Durable ownership. Keep the user's files in their original format under the user's control; the catalogue augments the folder rather than replacing it, and nothing locks the collection into a proprietary container.
P0054: BG-005 Private by default. Keep catalogue, reading state, annotations, and indexes on the device; treat every off-device transmission as an explicit, consented, audited, reversible exception; in classroom mode, keep each student's reading state private from other students.
P0055: 2.31.3 Scope
P0056: Ogma Library is a single .NET 10 desktop application (OgmaLibrary.sln) that runs in one of three runtime modes — Standalone (default), Library Host, or Classroom Client — selected by configuration, not by separate products. Its capability set is organised into 11 functional modules whose identifier prefixes are fixed in _context/features.md:
P0057: Library Setup and Scanning (LIB) — 7 requirements, MVP/V1.
P0058: Catalogue Browsing (CAT) — 7 requirements, including smart shelves and the Work/Edition model.
P0059: Metadata Enrichment (META) — 8 requirements, including PDF write-back and the library health dashboard.
P0060: PDF Reader (READ) — 15 requirements, including citation export, reading memory, and annotation layers.
P0061: Search and Indexing (SEARCH) — 6 requirements.
P0062: AI Advisor (AI) — 11 requirements, including the Privacy Center.
P0063: LAN Library Host Mode (LAN) — 10 requirements (V2 classroom product; implemented, grand-plan Phase 16).
P0064: Classroom Client Mode (CLIENT) — 13 requirements (V2; implemented, Phase 17).
P0065: School Administration and Managed AI (ADMIN) — 13 requirements (V2; implemented, Phase 18).
P0066: Extension and Interoperability (EXT) — 3 requirements (V2 open-source and plugin ambitions).
P0067: Premium Experience (UX) — 8 requirements (onboarding, states, command palette, localization, keyboard operability, help, journeys, themes).
P0068: The FR inventory is closed at these 101 identifiers; adding a requirement requires a Change Impact Analysis entry against this baseline.
P0069: Out of scope for the Minimum Viable Product (MVP), per the vision:
P0070: Cloud synchronisation. The data model permits encrypted synchronisation later; planning is deferred to V2.
P0071: Electronic Publication (EPUB) and Comic Book Archive (CBZ) formats. The schema is prepared; support is post-MVP.
P0072: PDF write-back of highlight annotations. Annotations persist in the catalogue of record; write-back of highlights is post-MVP (metadata write-back is in scope as FR-META-005).
P0073: 2.41.4 Definitions, Acronyms, and Abbreviations
P0074: All domain terms, technical terms, acronyms, and standards used in this SRS are defined in the project glossary at projects/Ogma-Library/_context/glossary.md. Each acronym is spelled out on first use in this document and abbreviated thereafter. Any term used but not defined there is flagged inline as [GLOSSARY-GAP: <term>] and collected in Section 8.

P0086: 2.61.6 Overview of the Document
P0087: Section 2 describes the product perspective (including the three runtime modes), functions, user characteristics (8 personas), constraints, assumptions, and the privacy-tier model. Section 3 specifies external interface requirements, including the opt-in LAN host surface that amends the v1.0 no-inbound-listener rule. Section 4 specifies all 101 functional requirements with priority tier, deterministic acceptance oracle, implementation status, and trace line. Section 5 specifies non-functional requirements at two explicit scales: binding MVP budgets (NFR-OGMA, 2,000 books on W-REF-01) and V1 large-library targets (NFR-PROD, 50,000 items, gated at Phase 20), plus LAN capacity budgets. Section 6 specifies data requirements aligned to the as-built Entity Framework Core schema and its 11 migrations. Section 7 specifies the 32-control security and privacy catalogue, extended for the LAN/classroom attack surface. Section 8 provides the per-requirement traceability table, the V&V approach, and the open-items register.
P0088: \newpage
P0089: 32. Overall Description
P0090: 3.12.1 Product Perspective
P0091: Ogma Library is a self-contained, locally installed desktop application built as a Clean/Onion modular monolith on .NET 10: a single deployable divided into bounded modules (Library, Catalogue, Metadata, Reader, Search, AI, Bookshelf3D, Sharing/Host, Classroom, School Admin) with a single dependency-injection composition root (OgmaLibrary.App/CompositionRoot.cs). NetArchTest architecture tests enforce inward dependencies and a single HTTP-egress chokepoint (the AI gateway).
P0092: The application is local-first by default. The catalogue of record — the embedded SQLite database, accessed through Entity Framework Core — is the authoritative source of truth for book identity, metadata, virtual shelves, reading progress, and annotations. The database augments the user's folder of PDF files; it never locks them into a proprietary container. Generated artifacts (covers, thumbnails, spine textures, Optical Character Recognition (OCR) text, extracted text, embeddings, backups) live in a sidecar asset folder on disk, outside the database.
P0093: The desktop shell is Avalonia. The 3D bookshelf is rendered with Three.js r181 inside the platform web control (WebView2 on Windows, WKWebView on macOS), driven by a typed C#-to-JavaScript message bridge and an ogma:// asset scheme.
P0094: 3.1.12.1.1 Runtime Modes
P0095: One codebase runs in one of three modes; the mode is runtime configuration, not a separate product (docs/plans/grand-plan/LAN-CLASSROOM-ARCHITECTURE.md).
P0096: Mode
P0097: Operator
P0098: Behaviour
P0099: Standalone (default)
P0100: Any user
P0101: The local-first single-user product. No inbound network listener.
P0102: Library Host
P0103: The central classroom or office computer
P0104: Holds the PDF folder and catalogue of record; serves catalogue projections, cover/spine assets, PDF content streams, and gated AI search to LAN clients through KestrelHostModeListener. Opt-in, explicitly enabled by an administrator, reversible, audited (FR-LAN-001).
P0105: Classroom Client
P0106: Student, teacher, or guest computers on the LAN
P0107: Discovers and connects to a host; browses, reads, and searches the shared catalogue; keeps per-student private reading state; degrades to its own local library when the host is unreachable (FR-CLIENT group).
P0108: The bounded-context discipline is preserved across modes: the Library Catalogue context on the host remains the single source of truth; clients are projection consumers and never mutate host files or host metadata (FR-CLIENT-010).
P0109: 3.22.2 Product Functions
P0110: At summary level, detailed as numbered requirements in Section 4:
P0111: Select a library root and discover, identify, and track PDF files within it (LIB).
P0112: Browse the catalogue in 3D shelf, grid, list, and directory modes; organise books into virtual shelves and smart shelves (CAT).
P0113: Detect identifiers, look up and merge bibliographic metadata under a reviewable confidence policy, write metadata back with backup and diff, and audit collection health (META).
P0114: Open, read, navigate, zoom, search, bookmark, and annotate PDF files; capture citations, reading memory, and annotation layers; resume the last reading position (READ).
P0115: Provide metadata search, full-text search over an FTS5 index, semantic search over embeddings, and hybrid ranking with an index manager (SEARCH).
P0116: Recommend books and generate reading plans through a provider-neutral AI gateway under a tiered privacy model, with a Privacy Center over every off-device payload (AI).
P0117: Serve the catalogue and PDF streams to LAN clients from an opt-in host, with TLS, discovery, authentication, and session control (LAN).
P0118: Let students read and search the shared collection from any LAN computer with per-student private state, offline cache, and sync (CLIENT).
P0119: Let a school publish content, enroll and revoke profiles, custody its own AI keys, and enforce privacy tiers, quotas, and audit over student AI use (ADMIN).
P0120: Expose plugin interfaces, a documented local read API, and importers for Zotero, Calibre, and Goodreads (EXT).
P0121: Deliver the premium experience floor: zero-configuration onboarding, complete surface states, command palette, runtime language switch, keyboard-only operability, offline help, measured journeys, and themes (UX).
P0122: 3.32.3 User Characteristics
P0123: The application is designed for 8 personas drawn from _context/stakeholders.md. Each persona defines a success signal and the critical feature set the requirements in Section 4 must satisfy.
P0124: Persona
P0125: Primary job
P0126: Product success signal
P0127: Critical feature focus
P0128: Serious reader
P0129: Re-discover and continue books accumulated over years
P0130: Finds, resumes, and browses without recalling file names
P0131: 3D shelf, grid view, progress, shelves, reading statistics
P0132: Researcher
P0133: Find the right document for a topic or question
P0134: Moves from intent to relevant source quickly
P0135: Full-text search, semantic search, AI advisor, annotations, citations
P0136: Collector
P0137: Correct and preserve metadata
P0138: Repairs, enriches, exports, and writes metadata safely
P0139: ISBN detection, provider lookup, metadata editor, backups
P0140: Power librarian
P0141: Maintain a large collection over time
P0142: Audits duplicates, gaps, unavailable files, broken covers
P0143: Health dashboard, duplicate detection, batch jobs
P0144: Privacy-sensitive user
P0145: Use AI without exposing documents unnecessarily
P0146: Chooses local-only or metadata-only AI modes
P0147: Privacy labels, local embeddings, Privacy Center
P0148: Student (classroom client)
P0149: Read and search the school's shared collection from any LAN computer
P0150: Joins with a code, reads a streamed book, keeps private notes
P0151: Classroom join, streamed reading, private per-profile state, explainable AI search
P0152: Teacher
P0153: Guide a class through a shared collection
P0154: Sees class-level reading dashboards and assigns shelves
P0155: Shared shelves, class dashboards, role-scoped access
P0156: School administrator
P0157: Operate the shared library and control AI spend and privacy
P0158: Publishes content, enrolls/revokes profiles, sets AI quotas and tiers, audits usage
P0159: Host mode, entitlements, managed AI keys, quotas, audit export
P0160: The application targets users with general desktop-computing competence and does not assume software-engineering knowledge. Students may be minors; the classroom scope therefore carries child-data protections (Section 7, CTRL-032). Keyboard-only and screen-reader users are a first-class audience; WCAG 2.2 Level AA conformance is a release gate (Section 5, NFR-PROD-007 and NFR-PROD-008).
P0161: 3.42.4 Constraints
P0162: CON-1 Runtime baseline. The application targets the .NET 10 Long-Term Support runtime and builds net10.0 with warnings-as-errors. The inspected baseline uses EF Core SQLite 10.0.9 and Microsoft.Extensions 10.0.9; ADR-0016 records the aligned runtime decision. Current support dates are controlled through _context/source-register.md and must be refreshed before release.
P0163: CON-2 Local-first operation. Core operations function with no network connection present (NFR-PROD-001).
P0164: CON-3 File ownership. Source files stay in their original format under the user's control; the application never rewrites them into a proprietary container (NFR-PROD-009).
P0165: CON-4 Single egress chokepoint. All off-device network calls route through the AI gateway (AiGateway); no feature module opens its own provider connection. Architecture tests enforce this (Section 7, CTRL-016).
P0166: CON-5 Accessibility. WCAG 2.2 Level AA is a release gate; the formal audit is Phase 21 scope (Section 8.6).
P0167: CON-6 Platform targets. The product ships on Windows 10 22H2 or later (WebView2) and macOS 13 or later (WKWebView). Linux is not a release target of this baseline; the Linux Secret Service reference in CTRL-001 is forward-compatibility only. Distribution channels per the vision: Velopack direct, MSIX + Microsoft Store, notarized DMG + Mac App Store, GitHub releases.
P0168: CON-7 Classroom capacity. A Library Host is a desktop machine, not a server: the design capacity is a classroom of 20–40 concurrent clients, with binding budgets NFR-LAN-001 through NFR-LAN-003 (Section 5.4).
P0169: 3.52.5 Assumptions and Dependencies
P0170: ASM-1. The PDF collection resides on a local or locally mounted file system reachable by an absolute path under a single library root.
P0171: ASM-2. Performance acceptance binds on the defined reference machines: W-REF-01 (Intel Core i5-10210U, 8 GB DDR4, SATA SSD, Intel UHD 620, 1920×1080, Windows 10 22H2) is the binding gate; M-REF-01 (MacBook Air M1, 8 GB, macOS 13.6) is expected to meet or exceed it (_context/reference-hardware.md, decision CON-1 of REFERENCE-HARDWARE.md v1.0).
P0172: ASM-3. In classroom deployments the school is the data controller for student personal data; the applicable data-protection law is jurisdiction-specific and is fixed per deployment through the DPIA screening (Section 7, CTRL-024 and CTRL-032).
P0173: DEP-1. PDF rendering and password handling depend on PDFium via PdfiumAdapter (PDFtoImage/SkiaSharp); text and metadata extraction on PdfPig; metadata write-back on PDFsharp via PdfWriteBackService; image and texture work on SkiaSharp.
P0174: DEP-2. Full-text search depends on the SQLite FTS5 extension (FtsIndexService); vector search on a SQLite vector extension (sqlite-vec / Vec1) behind SemanticSearchService.
P0175: DEP-3. The 3D bookshelf depends on WebGL2 in the platform WebView; a capability helper confirms availability and the application falls back to grid or list view where WebGL2 is absent.
P0176: DEP-4. Optional cloud AI and metadata enrichment depend on third-party providers (OpenAI-compatible, Anthropic-compatible, DeepSeek-compatible, Ollama local, Google Books, Open Library) whose availability is outside the application's control.
P0177: DEP-5. Optional OCR depends on Tesseract 5.2.0, executed in background jobs.
P0178: DEP-6. LAN host mode depends on the ASP.NET Core Kestrel server (KestrelHostModeListener) and mDNS advertisement; client discovery falls back to manual host-address entry where multicast is filtered.
P0179: 3.62.6 Privacy-Tier Model Overview
P0180: AI and enrichment data handling operates under 4 explicit privacy tiers, implemented in AiGateway with AiDisabledProvider as the default provider. The tier in force is shown at the point of any off-device action, and a payload preview is presented before any data leaves the device (Section 7; NFR-PROD-011).
P0181: Tier 0 — Offline. No off-device transmission. AI features requiring a provider are unavailable; reading, browsing, metadata editing, and local search remain fully functional.
P0182: Tier 1 — Metadata-only (default for cloud AI, and the enforced class default in classroom mode per FR-ADMIN-006). Off-device requests carry only bibliographic and organisational metadata (title, author, tags, categories, descriptions, notes). No document content is transmitted.
P0183: Tier 2 — Content-aware (opt-in). Off-device requests may carry document content chunks, enabled per library or per query only after the user — or, in classroom mode, the administrator — confirms a privacy label and previewed payload.
P0184: Tier 3 — Local AI. Inference and embeddings run on-device through Ollama; no user content leaves the machine.
P0185: In classroom mode the tier model operates on the host on behalf of the class: the student still sees the active tier and a payload preview before any off-device search (FR-ADMIN-007), preserving explainability and consent in managed mode.
P0186: \newpage
P0187: 43. External Interface Requirements
P0188: 4.13.1 User Interfaces
P0189: The application presents a single main window hosting the catalogue and a set of focused surfaces. All surfaces are keyboard-operable and expose accessible names, roles, and states to the platform accessibility Application Programming Interface (API), per NFR-PROD-007 and NFR-PROD-008. Every primary surface defines loading, empty, error, and in-progress states (FR-UX-002); no surface may present a blank panel.
P0190: UI-1 Main window. Presents the catalogue in the active browsing mode, a sort-and-filter control region, a virtual-shelf and smart-shelf navigation region, and a status region for background jobs. All 4 browsing modes (3D shelf, grid, list, directory) open the same book-detail surface and the same reader.
P0191: UI-2 Reader. Presents the rendered PDF page area, a navigation region (first, previous, next, last, jump-to-page, history back and forward), zoom and display-mode controls, a full-screen toggle, in-document text search with match highlighting, and the bookmark, annotation-layer, citation, and reading-memory region.
P0192: UI-3 3D shelf. A WebGL2 shelf hosted in the platform WebView and driven by Three.js, presenting book meshes with generated spine textures. Selecting a mesh opens the same book-detail surface as the other modes. Where WebGL2 is unavailable, the application falls back to grid view.
P0193: UI-4 Command palette. A keyboard-invoked palette accepting a typed query and exposing all catalogue, reader, and search commands (FR-UX-003).
P0194: UI-5 Privacy Center. One surface listing provider keys, recent AI calls with exact payloads, per-call privacy tier, delete controls for history and embeddings, and audit-log export (FR-AI-011).
P0195: UI-6 Classroom join and connection surface. Client-side host discovery list, manual address entry, QR join-code entry, certificate fingerprint confirmation, profile selection, and a persistent connection-state indicator with local-library fallback (FR-CLIENT-001, FR-CLIENT-011).
P0196: UI-7 School administration console. Host-side publish/unpublish controls, shared-shelf management, profile enrollment and revocation, session list with revoke actions, AI policy (tier, quota, rate limit) controls, and the usage dashboard with audit export (FR-ADMIN group; FR-LAN-007, FR-LAN-010).
P0197: UI-8 Teacher dashboard. Class-level aggregate reading dashboards with disclosed per-student visibility (FR-CLIENT-012).
P0198: 4.23.2 Hardware Interfaces
P0199: The application runs on commodity desktop hardware and requires no specialised peripheral. The performance-anchored minimum is the binding reference machine W-REF-01: a 2020-class 4-core/8-thread x64 CPU, 8 GB RAM, SATA SSD (~550 MB/s sequential read), and an integrated GPU of Intel UHD 620 class at 1920×1080. A WebGL2-capable GPU is required for the 3D shelf; absence degrades to grid or list view without loss of catalogue function (DEP-3). Local persistent storage must hold the catalogue database and the sidecar asset folder; the index manager surfaces sidecar and index sizes (FR-SEARCH-006). M-REF-01 (MacBook Air M1, 8 GB) anchors the macOS floor.

P0229: Optional OCR of scanned pages
P0230: Local, background worker
P0231: Google Books API
P0232: Bibliographic metadata and cover candidates by ISBN
P0233: Off-device, outbound (gated)
P0234: Open Library API
P0235: ISBN lookup, edition data, cover images
P0236: Off-device, outbound (gated)
P0237: OpenAI / Anthropic-compatible / DeepSeek-compatible providers
