P0546: Traces: BG-004; verified by: AdvisorExtensionPointTests (advisor extension point exists; the full 4-interface plugin surface is not started).
P0547: FR-EXT-002 (V2 — Not started): When a local consumer calls the documented read API, the system shall serve read-only catalogue and search-index queries per the published contract.
P0548: Oracle: each documented endpoint returns the documented shape for a fixture catalogue; no write operation exists on the surface.
P0549: Traces: BG-004; verified by: planned TC-EXT-002.
P0550: FR-EXT-003 (V2 — Not started): When the user imports from Zotero, Calibre, or Goodreads, the system shall map the source's records into the catalogue, and theme and icon packs shall be installable.
P0551: Oracle: a fixture export from each named source imports with documented field mapping and 0 dropped records; an installed theme pack changes the active theme.
P0552: Traces: BG-004; verified by: planned TC-EXT-003.
P0553: 5.114.11 Premium Experience (UX)
P0554: FR-UX-001 (MVP — Partial): When a new user completes first-run setup, the system shall require no mandatory configuration beyond choosing the library root, shall keep the catalogue browsable while scanning continues in background, and shall reach a browsable library within 5 min for 2,000 PDFs on W-REF-01.
P0555: Oracle: a scripted first run on the gc-perf-2000 corpus reaches a browsable catalogue within 5 min with only the root chosen; formal timing acceptance at Phase 20.
P0556: Traces: BG-001, BG-002; verified by: ScanProgressTests (background browsability); planned TC-UX-001 (timed journey).
P0557: FR-UX-002 (MVP — Partial): When any primary surface (catalogue views, reader, search, AI advisor, health dashboard, settings) loads, is empty, errors, or is in progress, the system shall render the corresponding defined state; no surface shall render a blank panel.
P0558: Oracle: driving each surface into each of the 4 states renders its defined state; a blank panel is a failure.
P0559: Traces: BG-002; verified by: SkeletonRenderTests (loading states); planned TC-UX-002 (full state matrix).
P0560: FR-UX-003 (MVP — Not started): When the user invokes the command palette, the system shall expose all catalogue, reader, and search commands by typed query and keyboard invocation.
P0561: Oracle: every command in the command registry is findable by its name in the palette and executes on selection.
P0562: Traces: BG-002; verified by: planned TC-UX-003.
P0563: FR-UX-004 (MVP — Partial): When the user switches the interface language between English and French, the system shall apply the change at runtime without restart; Spanish, Italian, and German complete at V2.
P0564: Oracle: switching en ↔ fr re-renders visible strings in the target language in the same session; pseudolocale tests detect hard-coded strings.
P0565: Traces: BG-002; verified by: pseudolocale test suite (en + fr resources implemented); planned TC-UX-004 (es/it/de, Phase 21).
P0566: FR-UX-005 (MVP — Not started as formal acceptance): When a user operates any core flow (setup, browse, open, read, annotate, search) by keyboard alone, the system shall complete the flow end-to-end without pointer input.
P0567: Oracle: a scripted keyboard-only walkthrough completes each flow; any pointer-required step fails the gate (NFR-PROD-007). Formal audit is Phase 21 scope.
P0568: Traces: BG-002; verified by: planned TC-UX-005.
P0569: FR-UX-006 (V1 — Not started): When the user requests help, the system shall present contextual help for the active surface plus a searchable manual, with no internet connection required.
P0570: Oracle: with the network disabled, each primary surface opens its contextual help and a manual search returns matching topics.
P0571: Traces: BG-002; verified by: planned TC-UX-006.
P0572: FR-UX-007 (MVP — Not started as formal acceptance): When a user who knows a book launches the application, the system shall support finding that book and resuming reading at last position within 60 s, measured as a scripted journey on W-REF-01.
P0573: Oracle: the scripted locate-and-resume journey completes within 60 s at P95 over repeated runs on W-REF-01 (Phase 20 acceptance).
P0574: Traces: BG-001, BG-002; verified by: planned TC-UX-007.
P0575: FR-UX-008 (MVP — Partial): When the user selects light, dark, or system theme, the system shall apply it, and reader typography controls (page fit, zoom persistence per FR-READ-003) shall meet WCAG 2.2 AA contrast in both themes.
P0576: Oracle: each theme applies across all surfaces; the automated contrast audit reports ≥ 4.5:1 for normal text and ≥ 3:1 for large text and UI components in both themes (Phase 21 audit).
P0577: Traces: BG-002; verified by: ReaderViewRenderTests (typography controls); planned TC-UX-008 (contrast audit).
P0578: \newpage
P0579: 65. Non-Functional Requirements
P0580: Every non-functional requirement (NFR) states a measurable threshold and a named verification method (IEEE Std 982.1; ISO/IEC 25010). Two scales are specified explicitly, per the binding rule of _context/reference-hardware.md (source: docs/governance/REFERENCE-HARDWARE.md v1.0, decision CON-1):
P0581: NFR-OGMA budgets bind release gates at MVP scale — 2,000 books, measured at P95 on reference machine W-REF-01 (Intel Core i5-10210U, 8 GB DDR4, SATA SSD ~550 MB/s, Intel UHD 620, 1920×1080, Windows 10 22H2, bundled fixed-version WebView2). M-REF-01 (MacBook Air M1, 8 GB, macOS 13.6) is expected to meet or exceed each budget; the Windows value is the binding gate.
P0582: NFR-PROD figures are V1 large-library targets — 50,000 items (5,000 for cold start) — gated at grand-plan Phase 20, not at the MVP release gate.
P0583: Where both an NFR-OGMA budget and an NFR-PROD target address the same attribute, both scales are stated on each requirement; no attribute carries two unlabelled numbers. The former v1.0 [DOMAIN-DEFAULT] markers are removed: the productivity-domain defaults have been reviewed and adopted, and appear below as normal numbered requirements.
P0584: 6.15.1 Performance Budgets — Binding at MVP Scale (NFR-OGMA)
P0585: NFR-OGMA-001 Cold-start latency. The application shall reach an interactive state within 3 s at P95 on W-REF-01, measured from Process.Start to the first Loaded event on the shell window, against the MVP-scale catalogue. V1 target: the same 3 s budget against a 5,000-item corpus (NFR-PROD-002, Phase 20 gate). Verification: instrumented startup benchmark over repeated cold launches; the build fails if P95 exceeds the budget.
P0586: NFR-OGMA-002 Catalogue-load latency. The application shall render all books in the grid within 2 s at P95 for the 2,000-book gc-perf-2000 corpus, measured from app-idle to all books visible. V1 target: first screen within 1 s at P95 against 50,000 items (NFR-PROD-003, Phase 20 gate). Verification: scripted catalogue-load benchmark on W-REF-01.
P0587: NFR-OGMA-003 Metadata-search latency. The application shall return metadata search results within 150 ms at P95, measured keypress to result list rendered, on the 2,000-book catalogue. There is no separate large-library metadata-search target; full-text and semantic scale targets are NFR-PROD-004. Verification: query-replay benchmark over a fixed query set.
P0588: NFR-OGMA-004 Full-text-search latency (warm). The application shall return full-text results within 500 ms at P95, measured submit to results rendered against a warm FTS5 index, at MVP scale. V1 target: the same 500 ms at P95 against a 50,000-document index, with semantic search within 1.5 s at P95 at that scale (NFR-PROD-004, Phase 20 gate). Verification: query-replay benchmark over a warm index.
P0589: NFR-OGMA-005 Reader page-turn latency (cached). The application shall complete a page turn within 100 ms at P95 when the target page is in the render cache (PageRenderCache), measured navigation action to page rendered. This budget also binds classroom cached-page rendering (NFR-CLIENT-002). Verification: instrumented reader benchmark.
P0590: NFR-OGMA-006 3D shelf frame rate. The 3D shelf shall sustain ≥ 60 frames per second for a 500-book scene on W-REF-01; the Intel UHD 620 is the binding constraint. Verification: WebView frame-timing benchmark reporting sustained rate and 1% low.
P0591: NFR-OGMA-007 AI gateway overhead (metadata-only). A metadata-only AI recommendation request shall complete with gateway-attributable elapsed time ≤ 10 s at P95, where gateway-attributable time is defined as (gateway request received → response rendered) minus (provider round-trip), both intervals instrumented with gateway-side timestamps on every call. Deterministic oracle: both timestamps are recorded per call; P95 of the difference over the benchmark run is ≤ 10 s regardless of provider latency. Verification: instrumented gateway benchmark (AiGateway timestamps); this closes the v1.0 [SMART-FAIL] on this requirement.
P0592: 6.25.2 Reliability and Correctness Attributes
P0593: NFR-OGMA-008 Annotation durability. The application shall persist a created or edited annotation to the catalogue of record before reporting it saved, and shall recover all saved annotations after abnormal termination. Verification: fault-injection test terminates the process after a reported save and asserts presence on restart.
P0594: NFR-OGMA-009 Background-job recoverability. The application shall resume or restart an interrupted background job (thumbnail, indexing, OCR, embedding, enrichment) after restart without duplicating completed work. Verification: per-job-type interrupt-and-restart test asserting completion with no duplicate output.
P0595: 6.35.3 Adopted Productivity-Domain Requirements (NFR-PROD)
P0596: Source: domains/productivity/references/nfr-defaults.md, reviewed and adopted 2026-07-06. Performance items (NFR-PROD-002, NFR-PROD-003, NFR-PROD-004) are V1 large-library targets gated at Phase 20; all other items bind at every release.
P0597: NFR-PROD-001 Local-first core availability. The application shall perform all core operations — open the library, browse the catalogue, read a document, annotate, and execute full-text search — with no network connection present and no degradation of result correctness. Verification: the core-operation suite runs with the network interface disabled and passes with results identical to the connected run.
P0598: NFR-PROD-002 Cold-start latency, V1 scale. The application shall reach an interactive state within 3 s at P95 on W-REF-01 against a corpus of at least 5,000 catalogue items. Gate: Phase 20. Verification: instrumented startup benchmark against the 5,000-item corpus.
P0599: NFR-PROD-003 Catalogue-load latency, V1 scale. The application shall render the first screen of catalogue results within 1 s at P95 and render or scroll any subsequent page within 200 ms at P95 against a corpus of at least 50,000 catalogue items. Gate: Phase 20. Verification: scripted scroll-and-render benchmark; values recorded at each release.
P0600: NFR-PROD-004 Search latency, V1 scale. The application shall return ranked full-text results within 500 ms at P95 against an index of at least 50,000 documents; semantic search, where enabled, shall return within 1.5 s at P95 against the same corpus. Gate: Phase 20. Verification: query-replay benchmark per search mode.
P0601: NFR-PROD-005 UI responsiveness. The application shall not block the UI thread longer than 100 ms during any user-initiated interaction; all long-running work (import, indexing, OCR, enrichment, embedding, provider calls) shall execute on background workers without freezing or modal-locking the foreground. Verification: UI-thread frame timing sampled during a scripted import-and-index session; any stall over 100 ms is a defect.
P0602: NFR-PROD-006 Crash-free session target. The application shall achieve a crash-free session rate ≥ 99.5% across opted-in telemetry over any rolling 30-day window. Any reproducible crash losing unsaved annotation or catalogue state is a release blocker regardless of the aggregate. Verification: opt-in crash telemetry reviewed at each release against both rules.
P0603: NFR-PROD-007 Keyboard operability (WCAG 2.2 AA). Every interactive control shall be reachable and operable by keyboard alone, with a visible focus indicator meeting the WCAG 2.2 Focus Appearance and Focus Not Obscured criteria, and no keyboard trap. Verification: keyboard-only walkthrough of every primary workflow plus an automated accessibility check at each release; formal audit at Phase 21.
P0604: NFR-PROD-008 Screen-reader operability (WCAG 2.2 AA). All non-decorative UI elements shall expose accessible name, role, and state to the platform accessibility API (UI Automation on Windows, NSAccessibility on macOS); dynamic state changes shall be announced; contrast shall meet 4.5:1 for normal text and 3:1 for large text and UI components. Verification: screen-reader pass per primary workflow plus automated contrast audit; formal audit at Phase 21.
P0605: NFR-PROD-009 Data portability and no lock-in. Source files shall remain in their original, openly readable formats under the user's control; the application shall not rewrite them into a proprietary container as a precondition of use. The user shall be able to export the full catalogue, all annotations, and all metadata to documented open formats sufficient to reconstruct the organisation in another tool. Verification: round-trip export/re-import into a clean instance with no loss of metadata or annotation linkage.
P0606: NFR-PROD-010 Reversible, transactional destructive operations. Any destructive operation — delete, bulk move, bulk re-tag, metadata overwrite, or merge — shall execute under a backup-write-verify-restore protocol with a single-action undo or restore; a partial failure shall leave the catalogue in its pre-operation state. Verification: fault-injection interrupts each destructive operation mid-write and asserts exact restoration.
P0607: NFR-PROD-011 Privacy-tier transparency and payload preview. Before any operation transmits user content or metadata off-device, the application shall display the active privacy tier and a preview of the exact payload (content, field set, destination provider) and require explicit confirmation; the default tier shall be the most restrictive (Tier 0, Offline). Verification: integration test asserts no off-device request is issued by any AI or enrichment path without a recorded prior confirmation referencing the previewed payload.
P0608: NFR-PROD-012 Update safety. Every published build shall be code-signed and verified before installation; an unsigned or tamper-detected update shall be refused. Every catalogue-schema migration shall be idempotent, snapshot the catalogue first, and be reversible to that snapshot if post-migration verification fails. Verification: the update pipeline rejects a deliberately corrupted artifact; a migration-failure injection restores the snapshot. Gate: packaging pipeline is Phase 22 scope (Section 8.6).
P0609: NFR-PROD-013 Local audit trail for sensitive actions. The application shall append a local, tamper-evident audit record for each sensitive action: provider-credential add/change/remove, privacy-tier change, off-device transmission, metadata write-back, bulk destructive operation, and export. Each record carries action, timestamp, affected scope, and active tier; the log is viewable and exportable and never silently truncated. Verification: each listed action asserted to produce a complete audit record.
P0610: NFR-PROD-014 AI history and embedding erasure. The user shall be able to delete AI query history and all embeddings derived from a given document or the entire corpus; the deletion shall remove stored vectors and cached provider responses in one operation; where an off-device provider generated the embeddings, the application shall surface that provider's deletion mechanism. Verification: scoped-erasure test asserts no residual vectors or cached responses.
P0611: 6.45.4 LAN and Classroom Capacity Budgets
P0612: Budgets adopted from the implemented Phase 16 and Phase 17 plans (docs/plans/grand-plan/phase-16/README.md, phase-17/README.md); binding on W-REF-01-class host hardware, gated with the other performance budgets at Phase 20.
P0613: NFR-LAN-001 Concurrent catalogue service. With 20 concurrent clients requesting the catalogue projection, host response shall be ≤ 800 ms at P95. Verification: load-test fixture with 20 concurrent catalogue requests (LanHostLoadSmokeTests provides the trend-only smoke measurement).
P0614: NFR-LAN-002 Concurrent page-render streams. With 10 concurrent page-render streams, the first image shall arrive within 2 s at P95. Verification: 10 simultaneous page-render requests measuring first-byte-to-first-image P95.
P0615: NFR-LAN-003 Host-mode non-interference. Enabling or operating host mode shall not disrupt the standalone user's local catalogue access: local grid, reader, and search functions operate while the host serves clients. Verification: regression suite executed with host mode active.
P0616: NFR-CLIENT-001 Client catalogue load. A client shall load a 2,000-book catalogue projection from the host within 2 s at P95 on a warm LAN. Verification: load test over the 2,000-book projection.
P0617: NFR-CLIENT-002 Cached page rendering. A client cache-hit page render shall complete within 100 ms at P95, matching NFR-OGMA-005. Verification: 10 cache-hit page requests, P95 measured.
P0618: NFR-CLIENT-003 Session-token custody. The client session token shall be stored in the OS credential store, never in a plaintext file. Verification: architecture test asserting token storage uses the credential-store abstraction (ClassroomCredentialStoreTests).
P0619: 6.55.5 Evidence Classification
P0620: All performance measurements taken before Phase 20 are trend-only (developer hardware and CI). Only Phase 20 measurements on W-REF-01 and M-REF-01, or owner-approved equivalents, constitute formal NFR acceptance evidence. This distinction is carried into the open-items register (Section 8.6).
P0621: \newpage
P0622: 76. Data Requirements
P0623: 7.16.1 Catalogue of Record
P0624: The catalogue of record is the SQLite database that is the authoritative source of truth for book identity, metadata, virtual shelves, reading progress, and annotations. It is accessed through Entity Framework Core (EF Core 10.0.9 on net10.0; version alignment is an open ADR amendment, Section 8.6) and evolved through 11 ordered migrations under src/OgmaLibrary.Infrastructure/Persistence/Migrations/:
P0625: InitialCatalogue (20 tables: books, files, metadata, shelves, reading state).
P0626: AddQualityScoreToBooks (FR-META-007).
P0627: Phase09Annotations (annotations, layers, citations, reading memory).
P0628: Phase10SearchIndexSchema and Phase10MetadataSearchIndex (FTS5).
P0629: Phase11EmbeddingSchema (semantic search).
P0630: Phase12AiGatewayTables (AI history, audit, consent).
P0631: Phase15OcrPowerReaderSchema and Phase15SmartShelfIndexes.
P0632: Phase16LanHostTables (host mode).
P0633: Phase18SchoolAdminTables (school administration).
P0634: Migration integrity is verified by MigrationTests and CatalogueSchemaTests; every migration is idempotent, snapshots the catalogue first, and is reversible per NFR-PROD-012. The database augments the user's folder of files; generated artifacts live in the sidecar asset folder on disk, referenced from the database.
P0635: 7.26.2 Logical Entities
P0636: The following entities constitute the as-built catalogue data model. Relationships follow in prose to avoid nested structure in table cells.
P0637: Entity
P0638: Purpose
P0639: Books
P0640: The catalogue record for an intellectual item the user reads; carries the metadata quality score.
P0641: BookFiles
P0642: A specific PDF file on disk bound to a Book, carrying the identity attributes of Section 6.3.
P0643: BookMetadataFields
P0644: Field-level metadata values, each carrying provenance (source, confidence, date, user decision).
P0645: Authors
P0646: A distinct normalized author record.
P0647: BookAuthors
P0648: The many-to-many association between Books and Authors.
P0649: Shelves
P0650: A virtual shelf or smart shelf definition; smart shelves persist a SmartShelfCondition filter.
P0651: ShelfBooks
P0652: The many-to-many association between Shelves and Books.
P0653: ReadingProgress
P0654: Reading status, last page, scroll position, and reading time per Book.
P0655: Bookmarks
P0656: A labelled bookmark anchored to a page within a BookFile.
P0657: Annotations
P0658: A highlight or note anchored to a region within a BookFile; carries its annotation-layer assignment.
P0659: AnnotationBodies
P0660: Note text, color, tags, and quote extraction attached to an Annotation.
P0661: ExtractedPages
P0662: Per-page extracted text with extraction status; OCR-derived text is flagged as such.
P0663: SearchChunks
P0664: Text chunks derived from ExtractedPages for FTS5 indexing and embedding.
P0665: EmbeddingVectors
P0666: Embedding model, dimensions, and vector storage reference per SearchChunk.
P0667: AiQueryHistory
P0668: Locally stored AI queries, mode, provider, selected results, and user feedback.
P0669: MetadataLookups
P0670: Raw external-provider responses with source, timestamp, confidence, and merge decision.
P0671: Jobs
P0672: Background job records (thumbnail, index, OCR, embedding, enrichment) supporting NFR-OGMA-009.
P0673: AuditEvents
P0674: The append-only local audit trail for sensitive actions and, in host mode, LAN requests.
P0675: Host and classroom tables (migrations Phase16LanHostTables, Phase18SchoolAdminTables):
P0676: Entity
P0677: Purpose
P0678: HostModeSettings
P0679: Host-mode configuration: enablement, bind address, certificate fingerprint, streaming mode.
P0680: HostClientSessions
P0681: Active and historical client sessions; the basis for listing, limiting, and revoking (FR-LAN-007).
P0682: LibraryPublishSettings
P0683: Which folders are published to the shared library (FR-ADMIN-001, FR-LAN-009).
P0684: SharedShelves
P0685: Admin-curated shared shelf definitions (FR-ADMIN-002).
P0686: SharedShelfBooks
P0687: The many-to-many association between SharedShelves and Books.
P0688: EnrolledProfiles
P0689: Enrolled student/teacher/administrator profiles with role and revocation state (FR-ADMIN-003).
P0690: SchoolAiEntitlements
P0691: Per-student and per-class AI privacy tier, quota, and rate-limit policy (FR-ADMIN-006, -008, -009).
P0692: AiUsageLedger
P0693: Per-query usage and cost entries feeding the admin dashboard (FR-ADMIN-010).
P0694: Key relationships: a Book has one or more BookFiles; a Book has many BookMetadataFields, each linked to zero or more MetadataLookups recording provenance; a Book associates to Authors through BookAuthors and to Shelves through ShelfBooks. A BookFile owns its Bookmarks, Annotations, ReadingProgress, and ExtractedPages; each ExtractedPage yields SearchChunks; each SearchChunk may have one EmbeddingVector. AiQueryHistory, Jobs, AuditEvents, and AiUsageLedger are append-oriented logs. EnrolledProfiles own HostClientSessions and per-student synced reading-state blobs; a student's private client-side state lives in a per-profile SQLite database on the client machine, keyed by profile identifier and unreadable across profiles (FR-CLIENT-002, verified by StudentPrivateRepositoryTests).
P0695: 7.36.3 Book Identity Strategy
P0696: Stable identity lets renamed or moved files re-match their records (FR-LIB-003) and removed files be flagged without data loss (FR-LIB-004). BookIdentityService resolves identity in this order; each later attribute is consulted only when the prior attributes fail to produce a unique match:
P0697: ID-1 Relative path — the path relative to the library root; the primary locator.
P0698: ID-2 SHA-256 content hash — the strong content identity; an unchanged hash re-matches a moved or renamed file.
P0699: ID-3 Size plus modification time — the incremental-rescan key (FR-LIB-006): unchanged size and mtime presume content identity and skip re-hashing; a change triggers re-hash and re-identity.
P0700: ID-4 PDF fingerprint — a structural fingerprint derived from the document, a secondary content signal where available.
P0701: ID-5 ISBN/DOI — the bibliographic identifier, associating files that represent the same edition or work.
P0702: Verification: BookIdentityServiceTests and IncrementalRescanTests cover rename, move, delete, and unchanged-rescan cases.
P0703: 7.46.4 Work / Edition Layer (V2)
P0704: The Work/Edition model (FR-CAT-007, V2, Not started) distinguishes an intellectual work from a specific edition and from a specific PDF file, grouping duplicates and editions for merge/split management. A Book is fully usable without any Work or Edition association; the MVP and the current schema do not require the layer. Its detailed cardinality and merge/split design is produced when FR-CAT-007 is scheduled, under a Change Impact Analysis against this baseline; the FR-CAT-007 oracle (no data loss across merge and split) constrains that design now.
P0705: 7.56.5 Sidecar Asset Folder Layout
P0706: The sidecar asset folder holds generated files outside the database, organised by asset class so a class can be regenerated or cleared independently: covers/, thumbnails/, spines/, ocr/, extracted/, embeddings/, and backups/ (pre-write-back file backups and pre-migration catalogue snapshots). On-disk naming within each class is owned by the implemented SidecarService (verified by SidecarServiceTests); the database reference — not the file name — is the authoritative link between a record and its assets, so asset-file naming is an implementation detail rather than a baselined requirement.
P0707: 7.66.6 Data Retention and Erasure
P0708: AiQueryHistory and EmbeddingVectors are subject to scoped erasure per NFR-PROD-014 and FR-AI-009 (verified by EmbeddingErasureTests). Classroom AI history is deletable by the student for their own scope (FR-ADMIN-012) and purgeable institution-wide by the administrator (FR-ADMIN-013). AuditEvents are append-only and never silently truncated (NFR-PROD-013). The full catalogue, annotations, and metadata are exportable to documented open formats per NFR-PROD-009; citation and research exports per FR-READ-013.
P0709: \newpage
P0710: 87. Security and Privacy Requirements
P0711: The v1.0 threat model — a local-first desktop application with no server surface — still governs the default install. Baseline v2.0 adds a second, deliberately scoped surface: the opt-in Library Host mode (FR-LAN-001, ADR-0010) exposes a TLS, LAN-bounded, authenticated inbound listener on one machine per deployment. The dominant risks are therefore: leakage of provider secrets, malicious or malformed imported documents, path traversal against the library root, supply-chain tampering of the installed binary, silent off-device exfiltration of user content, and — new in v2.0 — the LAN/classroom attack surface: on-wire interception, host impersonation, traversal via streaming requests, stale or stolen client sessions, cross-student data exposure, school AI-key theft, quota abuse, and unlawful processing of minors' data. The controls below use the flat CTRL-### sequence; CTRL-001 through CTRL-024 carry over from the signed v1.0 baseline, CTRL-025 through CTRL-032 are new for the classroom scope. Each control states its requirement, verification method, and the risk it retires. Full threat-model execution (STRIDE, attack trees, SAST) is Phase 19 scope (Section 8.6).
P0712: 8.17.1 Secrets and Credential Storage
P0713: CTRL-001. The system shall store all provider Application Programming Interface (API) keys, tokens, and credentials in OS-native credential storage (Windows Credential Manager / Data Protection API, macOS Keychain; Linux Secret Service named for forward compatibility only) and shall never write a secret to a plaintext settings file, the catalogue database, a log file, or a crash report. Verification: storage audit after configuring a provider finds no secret in any of those locations. Risk: provider-key theft from disk artifacts.
P0714: CTRL-002. The system shall redact secrets from all logs, error reports, telemetry, and the audit trail, identifying a provider call by correlation identifier. Verification: log scan after a provider call finds the correlation identifier and no secret material. Risk: secret leakage through diagnostics.
P0715: CTRL-003. The system shall delete a provider's secret from OS credential storage in the same action that removes the provider and shall record a credential-removal audit entry. Verification: removing a provider leaves no retrievable secret and produces a matching audit entry. Risk: orphaned credentials.
P0716: 8.27.2 Untrusted Document Handling
P0717: CTRL-004. The system shall treat all imported document content as untrusted input regardless of source. Verification: the import path applies the controls below to every imported file in test. Risk: malicious PDF as delivery vector.
P0718: CTRL-005. The system shall execute document rendering and OCR within a worker boundary isolated from the catalogue and the credential store. Verification: fault-injection confirms render and OCR workers hold no handle to the credential store or the catalogue connection. Risk: parser exploit reaching secrets or the catalogue of record.
P0719: CTRL-006. The system shall disable embedded JavaScript and auto-executing actions in documents by default and shall block document-initiated external resource fetches unless explicitly enabled. Verification: a fixture with embedded JavaScript and an external fetch executes neither; the audit records no off-device fetch. Risk: active-content execution and beaconing.
P0720: CTRL-007. The system shall enforce time and memory bounds on render and OCR workers so a malformed document degrades to a per-item failure. Verification: a malformed fixture produces a bounded per-item failure while the application stays responsive. Risk: denial of service via crafted documents.
P0721: 8.37.3 File-Path and Library-Root Validation
P0722: CTRL-008. The system shall validate the resolved, canonicalised path of every file operation against the configured library root and refuse any path resolving outside it. Verification: an outside-root operation is refused and logged. Risk: path traversal.
P0723: CTRL-009. The system shall not follow a symbolic link resolving outside the library root without explicit per-action user consent. Verification: a planted outside-root symlink is not traversed without recorded consent. Risk: symlink escape.
P0724: CTRL-010. The system shall reject or quarantine catalogue-import entries whose names attempt directory traversal (for example .. segments). Verification: a traversal entry is rejected or quarantined, never acted upon. Risk: import-time traversal.
P0725: CTRL-011. The system shall target metadata write-back only to files within the validated root, shall preserve the original (NFR-PROD-010), and shall record a write-back audit entry. Verification: outside-root write-back refused; inside-root write-back produces backup plus audit entry. Risk: destructive write outside user intent.
P0726: 8.47.4 Application Integrity and Updates
P0727: CTRL-012. The system shall code-sign all public builds and verify the signature before installing an update, refusing unsigned or tamper-detected artifacts (NFR-PROD-012). Verification: the update pipeline rejects a deliberately corrupted artifact. Risk: supply-chain tampering. (Packaging pipeline is Phase 22 scope.)
P0728: CTRL-013. The system shall fetch update metadata and artifacts over an authenticated, integrity-checked channel and signature-verify the update descriptor independently of transport. Verification: a tampered descriptor is rejected even over valid transport. Risk: update-channel compromise.
P0729: 8.57.5 Data at Rest
P0730: CTRL-014. The system shall offer optional encryption at rest for the catalogue database, with the key held in OS credential storage rather than alongside the database. Verification: with encryption enabled, the database is unreadable without the OS-held key. Risk: catalogue disclosure from device loss. (At-rest hardening completes in Phase 19.)
P0731: CTRL-015. The system shall apply the same at-rest protection to backups as to the catalogue, and backups shall contain no provider secret. Verification: a backup inherits the catalogue's encryption state and contains no secret material. Risk: protection downgrade via backups.
P0732: 8.67.6 Off-Device Transmission and the Privacy-Tier Model
P0733: The privacy-tier model of Section 2.6 governs every off-device transmission. The default tier is Tier 0 (Offline).
P0734: CTRL-016. The system shall route every off-device call through the AI gateway (AiGateway) and permit no UI or feature module to open its own provider connection; architecture tests enforce the single egress chokepoint. Verification: network-egress test confirms all outbound provider traffic originates from the gateway (ArchitectureTests). Risk: uncontrolled exfiltration path.
P0735: CTRL-017. The system shall not transmit user content or metadata off-device unless the active privacy tier permits it and the user has confirmed the previewed payload (NFR-PROD-011); the payload preview shall show exact content, field set, and destination provider. Verification: integration test asserts no off-device request without a recorded confirmation referencing the previewed payload. Risk: silent data exfiltration.
P0736: CTRL-018. The system shall produce a local audit entry for every off-device transmission naming provider, purpose, payload scope, and active tier (NFR-PROD-013). Verification: each off-device call produces a matching audit entry. Risk: unauditable data flows.
P0737: 8.77.7 Privacy, Consent, and Data-Subject Rights
P0738: For fully local operation (Tier 0 and Tier 3) no controller or processor relationship is created. When opt-in cloud AI or a metadata provider transmits data off-device (Tier 1 and Tier 2), General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) and jurisdictional data-protection principles engage for that payload. In classroom deployments the school is the data controller (ASM-3) and students may be minors, raising the bar (CTRL-032).
P0739: CTRL-019. The system shall obtain an explicit, specific, informed opt-in per privacy tier and per provider before any off-device transmission, never bundled into general application acceptance. Verification: a transmission with no matching consent is blocked and logged; the consent record names tier, provider, timestamp. Risk: unlawful basis for processing.
P0740: CTRL-020. The system shall bound each off-device payload to the minimum needed for the consented purpose and show the payload preview before send. Verification: the transmitted field set equals the previewed, minimised set. Risk: over-collection.
P0741: CTRL-021. The system shall surface the destination provider and, where applicable, its processing region before first use, and shall not silently route user content across borders. Verification: provider metadata includes the region and it is shown before first use. Risk: undisclosed cross-border transfer.
P0742: CTRL-022. The system shall default the provider no-training flag to opt-out, so user content is not used to train any model without a separate, distinct, explicit opt-in. Verification: the flag defaults on; enabling training requires a separate recorded consent. Risk: uncontrolled secondary use.
P0743: CTRL-023. The system shall allow the user to delete AI query history and all embeddings for a chosen document or the entire corpus, removing stored vectors and cached provider responses in one operation, and shall surface the provider's deletion mechanism for data sent off-device (NFR-PROD-014). Verification: scoped erasure leaves no residual vector or cached response. Risk: irreversible retention.
P0744: CTRL-024. The system shall run a Data Protection Impact Assessment (DPIA) screening before shipping any feature that transmits user content or metadata off-device, covering data categories, purpose, lawful basis, provider processing location and retention, erasure path, and residual risk. The applicable jurisdiction is fixed per deployment: for personal use, the user's own jurisdiction under GDPR-style principles; for classroom deployments, the school's jurisdiction, recorded in that deployment's DPIA. Verification: a completed DPIA screening artifact exists per off-device feature before its release gate; the classroom screening service is implemented (SchoolDpiaScreeningServiceTests). Risk: shipping unassessed processing.
P0745: 8.87.8 LAN and Classroom Surface (new in v2.0)
P0746: These controls govern the opt-in Library Host mode and classroom clients (Sections 4.7–4.9). The host is the highest-value asset in a deployment: it holds the school's content, the AI keys, and the enrollment records.
P0747: CTRL-025. TLS provisioning. The host shall generate and provision a TLS certificate at host-mode setup and shall serve all LAN endpoints (catalogue, assets, streams, sync, AI proxy) exclusively over TLS; no plaintext listener shall exist in any mode. Verification: LanHostCertificateProvisionerTests (certificate validity and stored fingerprint); LanHostEndpointTests (plaintext request refused). Risk: on-wire interception of book content, credentials, and student queries on a shared school network.
P0748: CTRL-026. Host trust store (trust-on-first-use). The client shall pin the host certificate fingerprint at first connection after displaying it for confirmation, shall store the pin in its host trust store, and shall refuse a subsequent connection presenting a different certificate with an explicit warning. Verification: HostTrustServiceTests (pinned certificate accepted; different certificate rejected with warning). Risk: host impersonation / machine-in-the-middle on the LAN.
P0749: CTRL-027. Path validation on streaming. The host shall resolve every requested content path server-side against the published library root before serving any byte, shall refuse traversal attempts, and shall never enumerate or serve unpublished content (FR-LAN-006, FR-LAN-009). Verification: LanBookFileResolverTests (outside-root and unpublished requests refused and logged). Risk: traversal exfiltration of unpublished host files through crafted stream requests.
P0750: CTRL-028. Session lifecycle and revocation. The host shall persist every client session in HostClientSessions, enforce the configured session limit, and honour administrator revocation of a session or profile no later than that principal's next request; a revoked token shall authenticate nothing. Verification: LanHostPersistenceTests, SchoolProfileEnrollmentServiceTests (revoked profile receives an authentication error on next connection). Risk: a lost, shared, or stolen student machine retaining access to the shared library.
P0751: CTRL-029. Per-student data isolation. Student reading state, annotations, and AI history shall be stored per profile — client-side in a per-profile database, host-side keyed and authorized by profile identity — and no endpoint shall return one student's data to another student's session. Teacher visibility is limited to the disclosed dashboard of FR-CLIENT-012. Verification: StudentPrivateRepositoryTests (cross-profile reads impossible); SchoolAiHistoryManagementServiceTests (history scoped per student). Risk: exposure of a minor's reading and query behaviour to peers.
P0752: CTRL-030. School AI key custody. The school's AI provider keys shall be held only in the host's OS credential store, shall never appear in any client-bound response, client configuration, log, or sync payload, and students shall never hold or see a key (FR-ADMIN-004). Verification: ClassroomCredentialStoreTests; AiProxyEndpointHandlerTests (no key value in any client HTTP response). Risk: key exfiltration leading to school cost exposure and provider abuse.
P0753: CTRL-031. Quota and rate-limit enforcement before egress. The host shall evaluate the per-student and per-class quota (SchoolAiEntitlements, AiUsageLedger) and the per-student rate limit before issuing any provider call; an exhausted quota or exceeded rate produces a local denial, an audit entry, and no provider traffic (FR-ADMIN-008, FR-ADMIN-009). Verification: SchoolAiPolicyServiceTests (over-quota and over-rate requests produce no provider call). Risk: cost exhaustion and provider abuse through student accounts.
P0754: CTRL-032. Minors' data minimization. Classroom deployments shall default the class privacy tier to metadata-only (FR-ADMIN-006); student profile records shall carry no more personal data than display name and role; every student-facing off-device call shall show the tier label and payload preview (FR-ADMIN-007); students shall be able to delete their own AI history (FR-ADMIN-012) and the administrator to purge it institution-wide (FR-ADMIN-013); and each classroom off-device feature shall pass DPIA screening naming the school as data controller before release (CTRL-024). Verification: SchoolAiPolicyServiceTests (default tier), SchoolDpiaScreeningServiceTests (screening artifact), SchoolAiHistoryManagementServiceTests (erasure). Risk: unlawful or excessive processing of minors' personal data.
P0755: \newpage
P0756: 98. Verification, Validation, and Traceability
P0757: 9.18.1 Verification and Validation Approach (IEEE 1012)
P0758: Verification and Validation (V&V) follow IEEE Std 1012. Each requirement is evaluated against 4 criteria before sign-off:
P0759: Correctness — the requirement mirrors the stakeholder intent in _context/vision.md; deviations are logged as anomalies and returned to the module owner.
P0760: Consistency — terminology and structure are uniform: the privacy-tier model of Section 2.6 is the model enforced in Section 7 and measured in Section 5; the two-scale performance rule of Section 5 leaves no attribute with two unlabelled numbers.
P0761: Completeness — every module in _context/features.md has full stimulus-response coverage (101 of 101 identifiers specified), and every edge case named in context (excluded folders, renamed files, missing files, password PDFs, scanned PDFs, malformed PDFs, host drop, revoked profile, exhausted quota) traces to a requirement.
P0762: Verifiability — every requirement carries a deterministic oracle. The two v1.0 [VERIFIABILITY-FAIL] items (FR-AI-003, FR-AI-007) are resolved by scoping the oracle to structural completeness and delegating quality judgement to the Phase 13 AI evaluation harness (Phase13EvaluationHarnessTests).
P0763: The test base is real: 626 test declarations across 120 declaring files in OgmaLibrary.Tests, OgmaLibrary.Tests.Ui, and OgmaLibrary.Tests.Architecture, plus the golden-corpus harness (SyntheticCorpusGenerator, ManifestVerifier). Planned TC- identifiers below mark verification work not yet written; each is a QA backlog item, not a requirement defect.
P0764: 9.28.2 Verification Method per Module
P0765: Module
P0766: Primary verification method
P0767: Test artifact
P0768: LIB
P0769: Fixture-tree scanning and identity round-trip
P0770: Folder fixtures with rename, move, delete, excluded cases
P0771: CAT
P0772: View-equivalence, filter-conjunction, smart-shelf evaluation
P0773: UI automation over a fixed catalogue
P0774: META
P0775: Provider-stub lookups, confidence merge, write-back fault injection
P0776: Golden corpus with known ISBNs and a malformed PDF
P0777: READ
P0778: Reader-state round-trip, annotation durability, OCR corpus
P0779: Golden corpus incl. scanned and password-protected files
P0780: SEARCH
P0781: Query-replay benchmarks and index-state reconciliation
P0782: Fixed query set over the gc-perf-2000 corpus
P0783: AI
P0784: Gateway payload capture, tier enforcement, evaluation harness
P0785: Stubbed IAiProvider, egress monitor, Phase 13 harness
P0786: LAN
P0787: Endpoint, certificate, discovery, and load tests
P0788: LAN integration fixtures, LanHostLoadSmokeTests
P0789: CLIENT

P0792: ADMIN

P0794: Enrollment fixtures, AiUsageLedger reconciliation
P0795: EXT
P0796: Contract tests per plugin interface
P0797: Test plugin per interface (planned beyond advisor)
P0798: UX

P0800: Phase 20 journey scripts, Phase 21 audit tooling
P0801: The golden corpus (simple, scanned, password-protected, very large, two-column, rotated, non-English, malformed PDFs) is shared across modules; GoldenCorpusTests and OcrGoldenCorpusTests execute it.
P0802: 9.38.3 Metadata Confidence Model
P0803: The merge policy (FR-META-003) resolves competing candidate values by confidence. For candidate value  from provider :
P0805: where  is the provider trust weight and  the field-match quality (exact ISBN match scores above fuzzy title match). The merged field takes ; the user may override any field, persisted with a user-decision provenance entry (FR-META-004). The concrete default weights and the match-scoring function are implemented configuration in the merge service and are pinned by ConfidenceMergeTests, which is the numeric oracle for this model; changing a default weight is a code change that fails those tests until they are updated deliberately. This closes the v1.0 context gap on provider weights.
P0806: 9.48.4 Per-Requirement Traceability
P0807: Every functional requirement, its business goal(s), its verification artifact, and its implementation status. Test names are real classes under C:\wamp64\www\Ogma-Library\tests\; TC- entries are planned test cases.

P0031: Confidential — Internal Use Only. This document is the intellectual property of Chwezi Core Systems. No part may be reproduced or disclosed without written permission.
P0033: Version 2.0 extends the v1.0 register for the classroom/LAN scope (grand-plan Phases 16–18, implemented) and the open-source and Extension SDK ambitions: it adds the student, teacher, and school administrator personas, the school as an institutional stakeholder and data controller, and the extension-developer community, and updates the influence-interest matrix, communication plan, approval matrix, and RACI accordingly.
P0035: 1.1Stakeholder Register for Ogma Library
P0036: This register identifies the stakeholders whose needs the Ogma Library application shall address. It separates the internal delivery team — the 3-to-5 person cross-functional group that builds, verifies, and launches the product — from the 8 external user personas whose jobs define the product's acceptance criteria, and, new in v2.0, from the institutional and community stakeholders introduced by the classroom product and the open-source/Extension ambitions. Each entry records role, primary interest, influence over requirements and release decisions, and the single concern that most shapes their requirements.
P0037: The product is owned by Chwezi Core Systems (chwezicore.com); the product owner is Peter Bamuhigire. The delivery team spans the grand-plan build phases (Phase 00 through Phase 23; Phases 00–18 implemented as of 2026-07-06 per _context/build-status.md).
P0038: 1.1.1Internal Delivery Team
P0039: These stakeholders execute the engagement and hold decision authority over scope, architecture, quality, and release.

P0131: The school's AI key never reaches a client, spend stays within quota, and revocation takes effect at the next connection

P0157: 1.2Influence-Interest Matrix for Ogma Library
P0158: This matrix classifies each stakeholder across 4 engagement quadrants derived from the Power-Interest Grid (Mendelow, 1981). Influence measures authority to shape requirements, scope, or release decisions. Interest measures how directly the product's output affects the stakeholder's daily work.
P0159: 1.2.1Quadrant Definitions
P0160: Manage Closely — High Influence, High Interest: primary engagement targets; every requirement traces to their stated needs and they participate in every relevant gate.
P0161: Keep Satisfied — High Influence, Low Interest: engaged at key milestones to prevent blocking decisions; not involved in day-to-day detail.
P0162: Keep Informed — Low Influence, High Interest: frequent communication ensures the product matches their jobs; feedback is captured but does not unilaterally gate delivery.
P0163: Monitor — Low Influence, Low Interest: passive stakeholders, notified of outcomes that affect them.

P0267: The 8 user personas sit in Keep Informed because none holds authority over scope, budget, or release; their high interest is honoured by making their stated jobs the source of traceable requirements. The v1.0 gap in the Keep Satisfied quadrant is now filled by the school (as classroom buyer and data controller) and the store reviewers — both can block a deployment or a channel without caring about day-to-day build detail.
P0268: 1.3Communication Plan for Ogma Library
P0269: This plan defines, for each stakeholder group, the information it receives, the cadence, the channel, and the accountable owner. The internal team communicates through engagement artefacts and gate reviews. External personas are represented through validation activities and in-product surfaces. New in v2.0: classroom-deployment communication (school, administrators, teachers, students) and community communication for the open-source/Extension ambitions.
P0270: 1.3.1Principles
P0271: Internal communication is artefact-driven: each phase gate produces a sign-off ledger entry, a baseline snapshot, and, where applicable, an ADR or Change Impact Analysis record.
P0272: Persona "communication" is delivered as product behaviour and validation evidence; no live end-users are contracted to this engagement.
P0273: Classroom deployments add a real outbound channel: the school receives a deployment pack (DPIA screening, audit-export guide, revocation runbook) because it carries data-controller obligations.
P0274: Gate-blocking failures and scope-changing spike outcomes are raised immediately, not held for a scheduled review.

P0384: 1.4Approval Matrix for Ogma Library
P0385: This matrix records which role approves each engagement artefact and which gate the approval satisfies, under the hybrid (Water-Scrum-Fall) governance model: a formal SRS baseline is signed off, then delivery proceeds across the grand-plan build phases under release gates. Baseline v2.0 supersedes the signed v1.0 baseline under a Change Impact Analysis and itself requires product-owner sign-off before Phases 19–23 artefacts are gated against it.
P0386: Under the hybrid cross-cutting rule, the hybrid-synchronization step runs after the Phase 02 Waterfall SRS is signed off and before any Phase 07 Agile artefact is generated; the kernel blocks Phase 07 outputs until python -m engine validate Ogma-Library passes the hybrid gate.
P0387: 1.4.1Approval Authority by Artefact
P0388: Each artefact has exactly one accountable approver; other roles prepare or review.

P0445: 1.4.2Approval Rules
P0446: Exactly one role is accountable per artefact. Where an artefact lists an author and a ratifier, the author owns the technical content and the Product Owner ratifies at the gate.
P0447: Any change to a baselined FR, non-functional requirement (NFR), or control (CTRL) requires a Change Impact Analysis entry with a rollback plan before acceptance — including changes to the 101-FR closed inventory.
P0448: No Phase 07 launch artefact is generated until the hybrid gate passes.
P0449: Each gate sign-off is recorded in the sign-off ledger before the next phase begins; each phase closure produces a baseline snapshot.
P0450: Classroom deployments add a second, external approval: the deploying school (STK-14) accepts its deployment DPIA and licensing terms; Chwezi Core cannot approve on the school's behalf because the school is the data controller.
P0451: The Product Owner and the Chwezi Core Systems executive function remain the same individual, so the Product Owner is the terminal internal approver for every artefact. The v2.0 model now names two genuinely external approval authorities — the deploying school (per deployment) and the store reviewers (per channel) — which removes the v1.0 open question about an independent approver for externally consequential artefacts.
P0452: 1.5RACI Matrix for Ogma Library
P0453: This matrix assigns Responsible, Accountable, Consulted, and Informed (RACI) roles across the 10 core engagement activities — the 7 v1.0 activities plus 3 added for the classroom product and the open-source/Extension ambitions. Each cell holds a single RACI code; a blank cell means no involvement.
P0454: 1.5.1RACI Definitions
P0455: RACI definitions follow the organisational responsibility model of IEEE Std 610.12-1990.
P0456: R — Responsible: executes the activity or produces the artefact.
P0457: A — Accountable: owns the outcome and holds final sign-off authority. Exactly one A per row.
P0458: C — Consulted: provides input before the decision; two-way communication.
P0459: I — Informed: notified of the outcome; one-way communication.

Personas are omitted from the columns to keep exactly one accountable role per row.

P0561: 1.5.4Notes
P0562: SRS baseline v2.0: the Engineering Lead and QA / Verification Engineer are jointly responsible for a verifiable specification; the Product Owner holds sole sign-off at the re-baseline gate, which also accepts the Change Impact Analysis superseding v1.0.
P0563: Classroom architecture and managed AI: the Engineering Lead is responsible for the host and client contexts (Kestrel listener, discovery, trust store, sync) and the AI Engineer for the school AI proxy, entitlements, and quotas. The deploying school is informed of the architecture through the deployment pack; it does not shape the codebase.
P0564: DPIA and minors' data compliance: the AI Engineer produces each screening; the deploying school is consulted because it is the data controller and must accept its deployment DPIA (approval matrix). The Product Owner remains accountable for shipping only screened features.
P0565: Extension SDK and open-source readiness: the Engineering Lead is responsible for interface stability and the read-API contract; QA is consulted on contract tests per plugin interface; the community is informed through repository releases.
P0566: Launch: no launch artefact is generated until the hybrid gate passes; store reviewers exercise their channel veto at Phase 22 rather than holding a RACI role inside the engagement.

P0025: \newpage
P0026: 11. Test Approach and Quality Philosophy
P0027: 1.11.1 Verification and Validation Philosophy (IEEE 1012)
P0028: This Test Strategy implements the Verification and Validation (V&V) framework defined in IEEE Std 1012 and aligns with the V&V approach baselined in SRS Section 8. Every test exists to confirm one of the four IEEE 1012 evaluation criteria for a named requirement:
P0029: Correctness. The implemented behaviour mirrors the stakeholder intent in _context/vision.md and the acceptance criterion beside the requirement.
P0030: Consistency. A behaviour defined once is enforced identically wherever it recurs — the privacy-tier model, for example, is exercised by payload-preview assertions in the personal AI path (tests/OgmaLibrary.Tests/Ai/PayloadPreviewViewModelTests.cs) and again in the classroom managed-AI path (tests/OgmaLibrary.Tests/SchoolAdmin/AiProxyEndpointHandlerTests.cs).
P0031: Completeness. Every edge case captured in the context files and the golden corpus has a covering test.
P0032: Verifiability. Every automated test asserts a deterministic pass/fail oracle. The per-check catalogue is ../29119-deterministic-checks.md. Where the SRS tagged a requirement [VERIFIABILITY-FAIL] (FR-AI-003 relevance, FR-AI-007 pedagogical soundness), tests assert only the deterministic structural sub-claim.
P0033: 1.21.2 The As-Built Test Estate (verified 2026-07-06)
P0034: The strategy in v0.1 was prospective. Version 2.0 governs a test estate that exists:
P0035: Test project
P0036: Content
P0037: Declared tests
P0038: tests/OgmaLibrary.Tests
P0039: Unit and integration tests, organized in 16 module folders (Section 3), including the golden-corpus harness and the Phase-13 AI evaluation harness
P0040: 637 executed cases in the controlled Release run
P0041: tests/OgmaLibrary.Tests.Ui
P0042: Avalonia headless UI tests (Avalonia.Headless.XUnit): render tests for reader, catalogue grid, advisor, health dashboard, school-admin panel, 3D shelf view; icon-catalog tests; navigation tests
P0043: 126 executed cases
P0044: tests/OgmaLibrary.Tests.Architecture
P0045: NetArchTest layering and egress-chokepoint rules (ArchitectureTests.cs)
P0046: 35 executed cases
P0047: Totals: 626 [Fact]/[Theory] declarations across 120 declaring files; 800 executed cases in the 2026-08-13 canonical Release run when theories expand (637 core, 126 UI, 37 architecture). Framework: xUnit 2.9.3 on .NET 10, Microsoft.NET.Test.Sdk 17.14.1, coverlet collector. The CI workflow is configured for Windows and macOS; cross-platform parity is a release gate, but current hosted-run evidence was not inspected in this overhaul. The latest local controlled run is in ../test-completion-report.md.
P0048: 1.31.3 Risk-Based Testing
P0049: Test depth is allocated by failure impact. The product principles — privacy, durability, reversibility, local-first availability — define the highest-risk failure classes.
P0050: Risk tier
P0051: Failure class
P0052: Required coverage depth
P0053: Where it lives today
P0054: R1 — Data loss
P0055: Loss of user-authored metadata, annotations, shelves, progress; destructive write-back without restore
P0056: Unit, integration, and fault-injection coverage mandatory; no waiver
P0057: Metadata/PdfWriteBackTests.cs, Ingestion/UnavailableFileFlagTests.cs, Reader/Phase09AnnotationTests.cs, Catalogue/MigrationTests.cs
P0058: R2 — Privacy breach
P0059: Off-device transmission without payload preview/confirmation; residual data after erasure; student data crossing profile boundaries
P0060: Integration and egress assertions mandatory on every AI and enrichment path
P0061: Ai/PayloadPreviewViewModelTests.cs, Ai/AiGatewayTests.cs, Privacy/EmbeddingErasureTests.cs, SchoolAdmin/SchoolAiHistoryManagementServiceTests.cs, ClassroomClient/StudentPrivateRepositoryTests.cs
P0062: R3 — Performance budget
P0063: An NFR-OGMA budget exceeded on the reference corpus
P0064: Benchmark coverage; trend-only until Phase 20 (Section 4)
P0065: Catalogue/Phase15SmartShelfPerformanceTests.cs, LanHost/LanHostLoadSmokeTests.cs, spike baselines in docs/benchmarks/
P0066: R4 — Recoverability
P0067: Job not resumable; index not repairable; corrupt input aborts a batch
P0068: Fault-injection coverage for every background job and parser entry point
P0069: Ingestion/JobManagementTests.cs, Search/IndexManagerServiceTests.cs, Ocr/OcrJobQueueServiceTests.cs
P0070: R5 — Functional defect
P0071: An acceptance criterion not met without R1–R4 impact
P0072: Unit and integration coverage proportional to module complexity
P0073: All 16 module folders
P0074: A failure in tier R1 or R2 blocks release regardless of aggregate pass rate (NFR-PROD-006, NFR-PROD-011).
P0075: 1.41.4 The Test Pyramid for This Desktop Application
P0076: There is no server tier to load-test in standalone mode, but there are three seams a server product does not have — native interop (PDFtoImage/SkiaSharp), the embedded database (SQLite/FTS5), and the WebView bridge — plus, since Phases 16–18, an opt-in Kestrel LAN host that does need concurrency testing.
P0077: Layer
P0078: Scope
P0079: Execution
P0080: Unit
P0081: Domain logic, view models, merge policy, ranking math
P0082: Every build; pre-push
P0083: Integration
P0084: Real SQLite migrations, real FTS5 index, real PDF parse/render over the golden corpus, LAN host endpoint tests, classroom sync
P0085: CI on every PR, both OSes
P0086: Headless UI
P0087: Avalonia.Headless render and interaction tests
P0088: CI on every PR, both OSes
P0089: Architecture
P0090: NetArchTest dependency and egress rules
P0091: CI on every PR
P0092: End-to-end
P0093: Driven flows on a built binary
P0094: Pre-release-candidate (Phase 21 sign-off)
P0095: Manual exploratory
P0096: Accessibility walkthrough (NVDA/VoiceOver), 3D visual fidelity, install/update/rollback on real hardware
P0097: Per release gate; Phases 21–22
P0098: 1.51.5 Entry Criteria
P0099: The requirement under test is baselined in the signed SRS, or explicitly tagged as draft-under-test.
P0100: Golden-corpus fixtures required by the level are generated and manifest-verified (Section 2).
P0101: For benchmarks: the reference-hardware anchor is W-REF-01/M-REF-01 per docs/governance/REFERENCE-HARDWARE.md; runs on other hardware are labelled trend-only.
P0102: The build under test compiles with warnings-as-errors and passes the preceding level.
P0103: 1.61.6 Exit Criteria
P0104: 100% of the level's test cases pass — zero failures, zero errors. As of the 2026-07-06 recorded run this criterion is not met: 1 failing test (HealthDashboardTests.BatchEnrichment_2000Books_CompletesWithRetry) is open; see ../test-completion-report.md.
P0105: Zero open R1 or R2 defects.
P0106: Every performance budget asserted at the level meets its threshold on W-REF-01, or carries a recorded, time-boxed waiver (max 90 days) per the governance protocol. No W-REF-01 measurements exist yet; this criterion binds at Phase 20.
P0107: Every requirement scoped to the level has at least one passing check in ../29119-deterministic-checks.md or a recorded [TRACE-GAP: <id>] in Section 8.
P0108: \newpage
P0109: 22. The Golden Corpus and Synthetic Performance Corpora
P0110: 2.12.1 As-Built Harness
P0111: The golden corpus is no longer a plan; it is code in tests/OgmaLibrary.Tests/GoldenCorpus/:
P0112: SyntheticCorpusGenerator.cs — generates the fixture PDFs synthetically (QuestPDF 2025.1.0), pinned by a generation seed. Because fixtures are generated, the repository redistributes no third-party PDFs, which closes the v0.1 [CONTEXT-GAP] on corpus licensing: every fixture is owned by Chwezi Core Systems by construction.
P0113: ManifestVerifier.cs — verifies each generated fixture against its manifest (content hash and expected properties); an unintended fixture change fails the build.
P0114: GoldenCorpusTests.cs — the corpus test entry point in the main suite.
P0115: tests/OgmaLibrary.Tests/Ocr/OcrGoldenCorpusTests.cs and the fixture set under tests/golden-corpus/ocr-pipeline/ — the OCR-specific corpus path (Tesseract 5.2.0 pipeline).
P0116: tests/GoldenCorpus/annotations/ — annotation-layer corpus fixtures used by the Reader tests.
P0117: 2.22.2 Fixture Classes and Oracles
P0118: Each fixture class targets a named failure class with a deterministic oracle:
P0119: Fixture class
P0120: Stresses
P0121: Oracle
P0122: Simple text PDF
P0123: Baseline extraction and render
P0124: All pages render; extracted text matches the stored reference after normalization; metadata and full-text search return the file
P0125: Scanned image-only PDF
P0126: Missing text layer; OCR path
P0127: Extraction yields 0 characters; file flagged for OCR; post-OCR search returns it marked OCR-derived (OcrGoldenCorpusTests)
P0128: Password-protected PDF
P0129: Encrypted load and consent flow
P0130: Detected at scan time; listed in scan health; wrong password rejected without crash; correct password renders (Pdf/PdfiumAdapterPasswordTests.cs, Security/PasswordProviderTests.cs)
P0131: Very large book (1,000+ pages)
P0132: Scale, memory, page cache
P0133: Catalogued without blocking the UI; reader opens to a target page from cache within budget (Reader/PageRenderCacheTests.cs)
P0134: Two-column academic paper
P0135: Reading order
P0136: Extracted text preserves column order against the reference
P0137: Malformed metadata PDF
P0138: Normalization and merge policy
P0139: Gaps flagged by the quality score; merge policy does not corrupt the catalogue; write-back failure leaves the original intact (Metadata/PdfWriteBackTests.cs)
P0140: Embedded outline/TOC
P0141: Outline parsing and indexing
P0142: Outline parsed into navigable entries; TOC indexed and searchable
P0143: Rotated pages
P0144: Render and annotation geometry
P0145: Correct orientation; annotation on a rotated page reloads at the same location (annotation corpus under tests/GoldenCorpus/annotations/)
P0146: Non-English text
P0147: Non-Latin extraction and search
P0148: No mojibake against the reference; a query in that script returns the file
P0149: Forms / unusual fonts
P0150: Font and form handling
P0151: Renders without missing-glyph failure; extraction degrades gracefully and flags the file
P0152: 2.32.3 Synthetic Performance Corpora
P0153: The scale corpora used by benchmarks and load tests are generated, not stored:
P0154: gc-perf-2000 — the 2,000-book catalogue corpus named in NFR-OGMA-002/003 measurement definitions (docs/governance/REFERENCE-HARDWARE.md), built by SyntheticCorpusGenerator from a fixed seed.
P0155: 500-book scene corpus — feeds the 3D shelf frame-rate budget (NFR-OGMA-006) and Shelf3D/SpineTextureGeneratorTests.cs.
P0156: Batch-enrichment corpus — feeds Metadata/HealthDashboardTests.cs (the BatchEnrichment_2000Books_CompletesWithRetry case — currently the suite's one failing test; see ../test-completion-report.md).
P0157: LAN load corpus — feeds LanHost/LanHostLoadSmokeTests.cs concurrency smoke checks.
P0158: 2.42.4 Corpus Governance
P0159: A fixture ships in the same change as its manifest entry and its expected-handling oracle; a fixture without an oracle is rejected at review.
P0160: Generation seeds are version-pinned so benchmark runs are reproducible; changing a seed is a test-infrastructure change requiring reviewer sign-off.
P0161: ManifestVerifier runs in the suite; a hash mismatch is a build failure, not a warning.
P0162: Corpus measurements on developer hardware are trend-only until Phase 20 (Section 4.1).
P0163: \newpage
P0164: 33. Test Layers and the Module Test Map

The unit/integration project tests/OgmaLibrary.Tests carries 16 module folders; tests/OgmaLibrary.Tests.Ui carries the headless UI layer; tests/OgmaLibrary.Tests.Architecture carries the layering rules. This section is the authoritative map from module to test location, verified against the repository on 2026-07-06.

P0223: 3.53.5 Layers Without Automated Coverage Yet (honest gaps)
P0224: Packaging — no install/update/rollback tests exist because no packaging configuration exists in the repo (Phase 22). Tracked in Section 9 (Phase 19–23 plan) and the go-live checklist.
P0225: Formal benchmarks on reference hardware — Phase 20. Current performance evidence is spike- and dev-box-trend only (spikes/RESULTS.md, docs/benchmarks/phase-13/, phase-14/, phase-15/).
P0226: Accessibility automation — no axe-style automated a11y checks yet; keyboard/screen-reader verification is Phase 21 scope.
P0227: SAST — no static-analysis security baseline in CI yet; Phase 19 scope.
P0228: 3.63.6 Layer-to-Risk Mapping
P0229: Catalogue, Ingestion, Metadata, and Reader folders carry the R1 mandate; Ai, Privacy, SchoolAdmin, and ClassroomClient carry R2; the benchmark estate carries R3; Ingestion, Ocr, and Search carry R4; everything else carries R5 plus its specific degradation cases. This mapping drives the coverage matrix in Section 8.

P0231: 44. Performance Testing
P0232: 4.14.1 Evidence Classification: Trend-Only Until Phase 20
P0233: The v0.1 [CONTEXT-GAP: reference-hardware specification] is closed. docs/governance/REFERENCE-HARDWARE.md (v1.0, decision CON-1) fixes two reference machines:
P0234: W-REF-01 (binding gate): Intel Core i5-10210U (4C/8T), 8 GB DDR4-2666, SATA SSD (~550 MB/s sequential read), Intel UHD 620, 1920×1080 @96 DPI, Windows 10 22H2, bundled fixed-version WebView2.
P0235: M-REF-01: MacBook Air M1 (2022 entry), 8 GB unified, Apple NVMe, macOS 13.6, WKWebView. Expected to exceed W-REF-01; the Windows value is the binding gate.
P0236: The classification rule is binding: every measurement taken before Phase 20 — spikes, developer machines, CI runners — is trend data only. Formal NFR acceptance evidence exists only when the measurement runs on W-REF-01/M-REF-01 (or owner-approved equivalents) in Phase 20. No document in this suite may present a pre-Phase-20 number as acceptance evidence.

Both scales are always labelled.

P0294: 4.44.4 Phase 20 Benchmark Gate Design
P0295: Each NFR-OGMA-001…007 budget gets a deterministic benchmark implementing the exact measurement definition above, runnable by one command per budget.
P0296: Benchmarks run on physical W-REF-01 and M-REF-01 machines (owner-procured or approved equivalents recorded in ADR-0020 per the Phase 20 plan), against seed-pinned corpora.
P0297: A budget miss on W-REF-01 fails the gate — a build failure, not a warning. M-REF-01 results are recorded; a macOS-only regression opens a defect.
P0298: Every gate run records: commit, corpus seed, machine identifier, raw distribution (not just the P95), and the pass/fail verdict, filed under docs/benchmarks/phase-20/.
P0299: NFR-OGMA-008 (annotation durability) and NFR-OGMA-009 (job recovery without duplicate work) are correctness NFRs verified by the existing fault-injection tests (Section 6), not by benchmarks.
P0300: 4.54.5 Benchmark Governance
P0301: A budget breach at Phase 20 is an R3 defect and blocks the affected release gate (G3/G4 in Section 7).
P0302: Trend regressions before Phase 20 (a CI perf test exceeding its in-test threshold) are triaged as defects but do not constitute NFR acceptance either way.
P0303: Benchmark corpora are generated from pinned seeds (Section 2.3); a seed change invalidates trend comparability and must be recorded.
P0304: \newpage
P0305: 55. Non-Functional Requirement Verification
P0306: 5.15.1 Verification-Method Taxonomy
P0307: Each non-functional requirement (NFR) carries one of the four IEEE-recognized verification methods:
P0308: Test (T) — an executable test asserts a deterministic pass/fail oracle.
P0309: Analysis (A) — benchmark data or telemetry is examined against a threshold.
P0310: Inspection (I) — an artifact (signature, audit record, focus indicator) is examined for a required property.
P0311: Demonstration (D) — a defined scenario is operated and the outcome observed.

P0413: 5.45.4 Measurability Summary
P0414: Every NFR now has a defined, deterministic verification method — no open [SMART-FAIL] remains in this section. What remains open is evidence, not measurability: the 7 NFR-OGMA performance budgets and the NFR-PROD scale budgets await Phase 20 reference-hardware runs; NFR-PROD-006 awaits the Phase 23 telemetry surface; NFR-PROD-012's signing half awaits Phase 22 packaging. These gaps are restated, with owners and target phases, in the Phase 06 go-live readiness register.
P0415: \newpage
P0416: 66. Reliability and Recovery Testing
P0417: 6.16.1 Scope
P0418: Reliability and recovery testing verifies that Ogma Library protects user-authored data and remains usable through failure. Every case targets an R1 (data-loss) or R4 (recoverability) risk and blocks release on failure. Version 2.0 names the implementing test class for each case; where a case is not yet automated it says so.
P0419: 6.26.2 Per-File Scan Failure Isolation (FR-LIB-005, FR-LIB-007)
P0420: Fault injected: a parse exception forced on one fixture in a mixed corpus (password-protected, malformed, unusual-font fixtures present).
P0421: Oracle: every valid file is catalogued; the failing file is recorded in the scan health report under the correct category; the scan completes; the process does not crash.
P0422: Implemented by: Ingestion/IngestionPipelineTests.cs, Ingestion/ScanHealthTests.cs.
P0423: 6.36.3 Transactional Metadata Write-Back (FR-META-005, NFR-PROD-010, NFR-PROD-012)
P0424: Fault injected: write failure forced after backup is taken and after the write begins, before verification completes.
P0425: Oracle: the original file is restored byte-for-byte from the backup; the catalogue records the write-back as failed; no partial PDF remains.
P0426: Implemented by: Metadata/PdfWriteBackTests.cs (PDFsharp write-back path in PdfWriteBackService).
P0427: 6.46.4 Resumable Background Jobs (FR-LIB-005, FR-SEARCH-006, NFR-OGMA-009)
P0428: Fault injected: each job type (thumbnailing, indexing, OCR, embedding, enrichment) interrupted mid-run by process termination.
P0429: Oracle: the job completes after restart; produced-output count equals the expected total; no duplicate output; completed work not redone.
P0430: Implemented by: Ingestion/JobManagementTests.cs, Ocr/OcrJobQueueServiceTests.cs, Search/EmbeddingGenerationWorkerTests.cs, Search/SearchExtractionWorkerTests.cs.
P0431: 6.56.5 Index Repair From Source (FR-SEARCH-002, FR-SEARCH-006)
P0432: Fault injected: the FTS5 external-content index and the embedding store corrupted on disk.
P0433: Oracle: rebuild from source returns results identical to the pre-corruption baseline; index size and indexed-book count match the source corpus.
P0434: Implemented by: Search/IndexManagerServiceTests.cs, Search/FtsIndexServiceTests.cs.
P0435: 6.66.6 Missing-File Metadata Preservation (FR-LIB-004)
P0436: Fault injected: a catalogued file deleted from disk before rescan.
P0437: Oracle: the record is flagged unavailable; every metadata field, shelf membership, note, highlight, and progress value remains retrievable; restoring the file re-matches by content hash.
P0438: Implemented by: Ingestion/UnavailableFileFlagTests.cs, Catalogue/BookIdentityServiceTests.cs.
P0439: 6.76.7 AI Degradation to Local Search (FR-AI-001, FR-SEARCH-001, NFR-PROD-001)
P0440: Fault injected: the configured IAiProvider returns an error or times out.
P0441: Oracle: the recommendation path reports the failure without crashing; local metadata and full-text search continue returning results; no core function blocked.
P0442: Implemented by: Ai/AiGatewayTests.cs, Ai/AdvisorServiceTests.cs; the default AiDisabledProvider binding covers the AI-off case structurally.
P0443: 6.86.8 Classroom Degradation and Sync Conflicts (FR-LAN-008, FR-CLIENT-006, FR-CLIENT-011) — new in v2.0
P0444: Fault injected: the LAN host becomes unreachable during a client session; conflicting per-field edits made offline on client and host.
P0445: Oracle: the client keeps local reading state and degrades to its own local library; on reconnect, sync applies latest-writer-wins per field with no silent data loss; the annotation-conflict flow surfaces the choice to the user.
P0446: Implemented by: ClassroomClient/ClassroomConnectionServiceTests.cs, ClassroomClient/ClassroomSyncServiceTests.cs, ClassroomClient/OfflineCacheServiceTests.cs.
P0447: 6.96.9 Catalogue Migration Reversibility (NFR-PROD-012)
P0448: Fault injected: a schema migration failure mid-apply.
P0449: Oracle: the migration aborts without partial schema change; the pre-migration backup restores the catalogue.
P0450: Implemented by: Catalogue/MigrationTests.cs across the 10 EF Core migrations.
P0451: 6.106.10 Not Yet Automated (honest gaps)
P0452: Update/rollback recovery — no packaging exists (Phase 22); the rollback drill in the Phase 06 deployment guide is a planned procedure, not a tested one.
P0453: At-rest encryption failure modes — Phase 19 scope; no at-rest encryption implemented yet.
P0454: Crash-free measurement — requires the Phase 23 opt-in telemetry surface.
P0455: 6.116.11 Reliability Exit Rule
P0456: No release candidate proceeds while any automated case in Sections 6.2–6.9 fails. A reliability failure is an R1/R4 defect and cannot be waived (Section 1.3). The current recorded run has all reliability cases passing; the single open failure (HealthDashboardTests.BatchEnrichment_2000Books_CompletesWithRetry) is an R5/R3-class batch-enrichment case, documented in ../test-completion-report.md.
P0457: \newpage
P0458: 77. Release Gates
P0459: 7.17.1 Gate Model
P0460: A release gate is a verifiable precondition that must pass before the corresponding release event is permitted: no public beta opens until every gate G1–G8 passes with formal evidence. Version 2.0 adds the column that was missing from v0.1 and that matters most: the current, honest status of each gate. The distinction is between spike/automated-test evidence (which exists today) and formal acceptance (a recorded gate run on reference hardware or a built release artifact — Phases 20–22). Nothing below is signed off.

P0516: Reading this table honestly: G5–G8 have their oracles automated and green in the current suite — strong evidence, but a gate passes only when its run is recorded against a release-candidate build with commit, corpus seed, and result, which has never been done. G1–G4 additionally lack their platform or hardware precondition. The v1.0 checklist that presented all 8 gates as checked was wrong and is withdrawn.
P0517: 7.37.3 Gate Governance
P0518: A gate is binary; there is no partial pass. "Automated evidence green" is a progress note, not a pass.
P0519: G5–G8 verify R1/R2/R4 risks and cannot be waived. G1–G4 may carry a time-boxed waiver (max 90 days) only by owner sign-off recorded in _registry/waivers.yaml.
P0520: Each formal gate run records commit, corpus seed, machine identifier, and result in docs/qa/BETA-GATES-STATUS.md (to be created at Phase 21 per the Phase 23 plan) so the beta decision is auditable.
P0521: Gate ownership: Peter Bamuhigire (owner/QA) for all 8. The Phase 06 go-live readiness register carries the same gates with owner, evidence pointer, and target phase — the two documents must never disagree.
P0522: \newpage
P0523: 88. Traceability and Coverage
P0524: 8.18.1 Coverage Principle
P0525: Every functional-requirement group and every key NFR maps to at least one test location that covers it; a requirement with no covering location is recorded as [TRACE-GAP: <id>]. The per-check traceability (requirement → check ID → implementing test class) lives in ../29119-deterministic-checks.md; this section is the group-level roll-up. Version 2.0 extends the matrix to the classroom scope (FR-LAN, FR-CLIENT, FR-ADMIN) and the promoted UX requirements.
P0526: 8.28.2 Functional-Requirement Coverage Matrix
P0527: FR group

P0563: 8.38.3 Verifiability-Constrained Requirements
P0564: Two requirements carry SRS [VERIFIABILITY-FAIL] tags because their quality dimension is a judgement call; automated coverage asserts only the deterministic structural sub-claim:
P0565: FR-AI-003 — each ranked card contains book identity, explanation, and confidence, and references only books in the user's collection (RecommendationPipelineTests); relevance itself is not gated. The Phase-13 evaluation harness (Phase13EvaluationHarnessTests, tests/evaluation/phase-13/ with queries.json and run-eval.ps1/.sh) tracks relevance as a scored trend, not a pass/fail gate.
P0566: FR-AI-007 — plan structure (sequence, rationale, estimated difficulty, checkpoints) asserted by ReadingPlanPipelineTests; pedagogical soundness not gated.
