P0592: 99. Test Plan for the Unbuilt Phases (19–23)
P0593: Phases 00–18 are implemented and covered by the estate in Sections 1–3. Phases 19–23 are planned and not started (docs/plans/grand-plan/phase-19/ through phase-23/, each marked "Not started"). This section is their forward test plan: entry criteria, test activities, tooling, environments, and exit criteria per phase. Estimates come from the grand-plan phase READMEs.
P0594: 9.19.1 Phase 19 — Security Hardening & Privacy/Compliance (3 engineer-weeks)
P0595: Entry criteria.
P0596: The one open functional failure (HealthDashboardTests.BatchEnrichment_2000Books_CompletesWithRetry) resolved — no security pass starts on a red suite.
P0597: The NU1903 restore blocker (SQLitePCLRaw.lib.e_sqlite3 2.1.10, GHSA-2m69-gcr7-jv3q) resolved by dependency bump, since a vulnerability-audit failure is itself Phase 19 subject matter.
P0598: Threat-model scope agreed: LAN host inbound surface (highest-risk new asset), untrusted-PDF parsing, credential storage, update trust chain, classroom minors' data.
P0599: Test activities and tooling.
P0600: SAST baseline in CI on both OS runners: CodeQL for C# (or owner-approved equivalent), plus dotnet list package --vulnerable as a per-PR audit job. Every finding triaged to fix, accepted-risk record, or false positive — zero unreviewed findings.
P0601: Fuzz/abuse tests against the LAN host: malformed range requests, path-traversal attempts against LanBookFileResolver (extending LanBookFileResolverTests), unauthenticated endpoint probes, session-revocation races.
P0602: Untrusted-PDF isolation tests: malformed and adversarial PDFs through the PDFtoImage/PdfPig paths asserting the parse failure is contained per-file (extends the golden corpus with hostile fixtures).
P0603: At-rest encryption verification for provider secrets and student data once implemented; CTRL-OGMA control-by-control verification with a recorded verdict per control.
P0604: DPIA execution for classroom processing of minors' data (screening service already exists — SchoolDpiaScreeningServiceTests; Phase 19 produces the assessed document).
P0605: Environment. CI runners (windows-latest, macos-latest) plus one analyst workstation; no reference hardware needed.
P0606: Exit criteria. SAST job green and required in branch protection; zero open high/critical findings without an owner-signed acceptance; all CTRL-OGMA controls verified with recorded verdicts; hostile-fixture corpus merged and green; DPIA signed by the owner.
P0607: 9.29.2 Phase 20 — Performance Engineering & Reliability (3 engineer-weeks)
P0608: Entry criteria.
P0609: Physical W-REF-01 and M-REF-01 machines (or owner-approved equivalents recorded in ADR-0020) procured and imaged per docs/governance/REFERENCE-HARDWARE.md.
P0610: Benchmark harness implements the exact measurement definitions in Section 4.2, one command per budget.
P0611: Seed-pinned corpora (gc-perf-2000, 500-book scene, 50,000-item V1 corpus) generated and manifest-verified.
P0612: Test activities and tooling.
P0613: Formal acceptance runs of NFR-OGMA-001…007 on W-REF-01 (binding) and M-REF-01 (recorded), full distributions filed under docs/benchmarks/phase-20/ with commit + seed + machine ID.
P0614: V1 scale runs (NFR-PROD-002/003/004) on the 50,000-item corpus.
P0615: LAN concurrency load tests promoting LanHostLoadSmokeTests to a sized profile: 30 concurrent classroom clients streaming (classroom-sized load), session churn, revocation under load.
P0616: Reliability soak: 24-hour scripted scan/index/read loop asserting no memory growth beyond an agreed ceiling and no UI stall > 100 ms (NFR-PROD-005 instrumentation).
P0617: Exit criteria. Every NFR-OGMA budget passes on W-REF-01 or carries an owner-signed, time-boxed waiver; G3 and G4 flip from trend to formally passed; results recorded in docs/qa/BETA-GATES-STATUS.md; CI perf jobs promoted from trend to hard gates.
P0618: 9.39.3 Phase 21 — Accessibility, Full i18n & Comprehensive QA (4 engineer-weeks)
P0619: Entry criteria. Phase 20 gates closed; es/it/de string freeze declared.
P0620: Test activities and tooling.
P0621: Automated axe-style accessibility checks integrated into the headless UI suite (Avalonia accessibility peers asserted for name/role/state per WCAG 2.2 SC 4.1.2), plus contrast assertions (SC 1.4.3) against both themes.
P0622: Manual screen-reader passes: NVDA on Windows, VoiceOver on macOS, over every core flow (setup, browse, open, read, annotate, search) with recorded scripts and verdicts; keyboard-only walkthrough per FR-UX-005 (no trap, visible focus per SC 2.4.11).
P0623: Localization QA: es/it/de resource completeness gates (resx parity check), pseudolocale expansion tests, runtime language-switch test extended to 5 languages (FR-UX-004).
P0624: Close the two UX trace gaps: automated coverage for FR-UX-003 (command palette) and FR-UX-006 (offline help).
P0625: Golden-corpus E2E sign-off run on built binaries, both platforms; G1 live WebView round-trip closed here.
P0626: Exit criteria. WCAG 2.2 AA verdict recorded per flow with zero blocking failures; 5-language resource parity at 100%; E2E suite green on both platforms; [TRACE-GAP: FR-UX-003] and [TRACE-GAP: FR-UX-006] closed.
P0627: 9.49.4 Phase 22 — Packaging, Signing & Store Submission (3 engineer-weeks)
P0628: Entry criteria. Phase 21 exit; signing certificates provisioned (Authenticode + Apple Developer ID) in secure key custody; packaging configuration authored per the Phase 06 deployment guide (none exists in the repo today).
P0629: Test activities.
P0630: Install/uninstall tests on clean Windows 10 22H2, Windows 11, macOS 13 VMs plus the physical reference machines: clean install launches; uninstall removes application files and preserves the user library folder.
P0631: Update-path tests on a staging Velopack feed: N−1 → N delta update applies; signature verification rejects a tampered descriptor and a tampered package (CTRL-OGMA-012/013 oracles).
P0632: Rollback drill: re-point staging channel to previous release; verify auto-update pulls the older build forward; migration backup-restore exercised end-to-end.
P0633: Store-pipeline validation: MSIX passes Windows App Certification Kit; DMG notarization succeeds; Mac App Store build passes App Review technical validation.
P0634: G5–G8 formal gate runs recorded against the release-candidate build.
P0635: Exit criteria. All packaging targets produced from one pipeline and installed successfully on all test images; tamper tests fail closed; rollback drill recorded; G2 formally closed; NFR-PROD-012 signing evidence recorded.
P0636: 9.59.5 Phase 23 — Beta, Launch & Post-Launch Operations + Extension SDK (3 engineer-weeks + soak)
P0637: Entry criteria. G1–G8 all formally green in docs/qa/BETA-GATES-STATUS.md; go-live readiness register (Phase 06) shows no blocked dimension; telemetry opt-in surface implemented and verified to transmit nothing by default.
P0638: Test activities.
P0639: Beta soak ≥ 2 weeks on the Beta channel with SLO monitoring: update-success ≥ 99.0%, crash-free ≥ 99.5%, release-host ≥ 99.9%, median update download ≤ 8 s at 25 Mbps.
P0640: Incident drills: one signing-key-compromise tabletop and one malicious-update drill against the staging feed, following the Phase 06 runbook; timing measured against severity response targets.
P0641: Extension SDK contract tests: public plugin interfaces (metadata, export, OCR, AI provider) get their own contract suites; FR-EXT trace gaps closed; every extension egress path asserted to route through the gateway privacy tiers.
P0642: Exit criteria. Soak completes with all SLOs inside budget or a recorded, owner-accepted exception; both drills executed with post-drill runbook updates; SDK contract suites green; owner signs the launch decision record.
P0643: 9.69.6 Cross-Phase Rules
P0644: Every new test family lands in ../29119-deterministic-checks.md with check IDs before its phase exits.
P0645: No phase exit is declared on assertion alone: each exit criterion names its evidence artifact (benchmark file, gate-status entry, signed DPIA, drill record).
P0646: The go-live readiness register and Section 7 gate table are updated in the same change as any phase exit.

P0026: 1.11. Business Goals
P0027: BG-001 Visible library — covers, shelves, metadata, multiple browsing modes.
P0028: BG-002 Usable library — progress, bookmarks, annotations, search, statistics.
P0029: BG-003 Intelligent library — an explainable AI advisor over the user's own collection.
P0030: BG-004 Durable ownership — files stay the user's files; the catalogue augments, never replaces.
P0031: BG-005 Private by default — on-device by default; every off-device transmission explicit, consented, reversible.
P0032: The classroom scope expansion (LAN/CLIENT/ADMIN, baseline v2.0) extends BG-001/BG-002 to a shared institutional collection and BG-005 to students as data subjects; no new business goal was created — the Change Impact Analysis entry for the expansion records this decision.

P0001: User Guide
P0002: Table of Contents
P0004: 1Ogma Library — User Manual
P0005: Field
P0006: Value
P0007: Product
P0008: Ogma Library development build; not a packaged or approved release
P0009: Document
P0010: User Manual
P0011: Owner
P0012: Chwezi Core Systems
P0013: Author
P0014: Peter Bamuhigire, Lead Consultant, Chwezi Core
P0015: Version
P0016: 2.1
P0017: Date
P0018: 2026-08-13
P0019: Status
P0020: Draft for owner sign-off
P0021: Audience
P0022: Readers, researchers, collectors, students, teachers, school administrators
P0023: Standards
P0024: ISO/IEC/IEEE 26514 (user documentation)
P0025: Ogma Library is a local-first desktop application that turns a folder of PDF files into a managed, searchable personal library — and, in classroom mode, into a shared school e-library. By default it transmits nothing off your device; every off-device action is opt-in, previewed, and reversible.
P0026: Screenshot evidence note: the images were captured by the implementation's headless/synthetic UI-test fixtures, including pseudolocale content. They demonstrate exercised development-build states, not visual acceptance, real-user usability, target-platform rendering, or release readiness. The current implementation still references Avalonia.Fonts.Inter and placeholder icon styling; controlled release screenshots require the design-system migration and manual Windows/macOS review. The missing 3D-shelf and settings evidence remains DOC-IMG-1.
P0027: 1.11. Getting Started
P0028: 1.1.11.1 First Run: Choose Your Library Folder
P0029: Ogma requires no account, no internet connection, and no configuration beyond one choice: the folder that holds your PDFs.
P0030: Launch Ogma Library.
P0031: On the welcome screen, click Choose library folder.
P0032: Select the top-level folder containing your PDFs (subfolders are included automatically).
P0033: Ogma begins scanning in the background. You can browse books as soon as they appear — you do not need to wait for the scan to finish.
P0035: The screenshot above shows the first-run state in French: the interface language is switchable between English and French at runtime (Settings, Language), with Spanish, Italian, and German planned for launch.
P0036: To open a single PDF without creating a library, click Open a PDF instead.
P0037: 1.1.21.2 What Scanning Does
P0038: Scanning discovers every PDF under your root folder, assigns each file a stable identity (relative path plus content hash), and generates covers and spine images in background jobs. Because identity follows content, renaming or moving a file does not lose its shelves, notes, or reading progress; deleting a file marks the book unavailable but preserves everything you added. Re-scans are incremental: unchanged files are skipped.
P0039: To re-scan at any time: open the menu and choose Rescan library. To exclude folders (for example a _drafts subfolder), add them to the excluded list in Settings before rescanning.
P0040: 1.22. Browsing Your Library
P0041: 1.2.12.1 Grid, List, Directory, and 3D Shelf
P0042: Four views show the same catalogue; a book opened from any view lands in the same detail panel and reader.
P0044: Grid — cover-first browsing; best for visual recognition.
P0045: List — dense rows with title, author, and status; best for large collections.
P0046: Directory — mirrors your folder structure.
P0047: 3D shelf — a spatial bookshelf rendered with real spine textures; hover to inspect, click to open. The 3D shelf is a browsing aid, never a gate: everything reachable in 3D is equally reachable in grid and list, including by keyboard.
P0049: 1.2.22.2 Sorting, Filtering, and Shelves
P0050: Open the Filters bar above the catalogue.
P0051: Combine filters (title, author, year, status, rating, tag, shelf, availability); results are the intersection of all active filters.
P0052: Click Clear all to return to the full catalogue.
P0053: Shelves are virtual: a book can sit on several shelves at once, independent of its folder. Create a shelf from the left sidebar, then add books from the detail panel or by bulk edit. Smart shelves are saved filters that maintain their own membership — for example "Currently reading" or "Missing metadata".
P0055: 1.2.32.3 The Book Detail Panel
P0056: Select any book to open its detail panel (right side), organised into File, Biblio, Reading, Enrichment, and AI tabs. From here you can read, edit metadata, run enrichment, or start OCR for scanned books.
P0057: 1.2.42.4 Repairing Metadata
P0058: In the detail panel, open the Enrichment tab and click Enrich.
P0059: Ogma detects ISBNs (from the filename, embedded metadata, and first pages) and looks them up in Google Books and Open Library.
P0060: Review each proposed field: accept, reject, or edit. Your manual edits always win over provider values.
P0061: Optional write-back to the PDF file happens only after your confirmation, with an automatic backup, a field-by-field diff, and a verify-and-restore safety net. A failed write-back leaves your original untouched.
P0062: 1.33. Reading and Annotating
P0063: 1.3.13.1 Reading
P0064: Open a book from any view. Ogma resumes at your last position automatically. Navigation: first/previous/next/last page, jump-to-page, and back/forward history, all available from the keyboard. Zoom modes (fit width, fit page, fixed percentage) and display modes (single page, two-page spread, continuous scroll) persist per book. Full-screen mode hides everything but the page; press Escape to exit.
P0065: Password-protected PDFs prompt once for the password with your consent; the credential is held in your operating system's credential store, never in the catalogue database.
P0066: 1.3.23.2 Bookmarks, Highlights, Notes, Layers, and Reading Memory
P0068: Select text and click Add note (or the highlight action) to annotate.
P0069: Use the side panel tabs to manage Annotations, Bookmarks, Annotation layers, and Reading memory.
P0070: Assign annotations to named layers (for example "thesis", "quotes", "to-verify") and show or hide layers independently.
P0071: Reading memory records, per book, why you opened it, what you highlighted, and how it ended (finished, abandoned, saved for later).
P0072: Annotations live in Ogma's local database and reload exactly where you left them. They are never written into your PDF files.
P0073: 1.3.33.3 Citations and Research Export
P0074: Select a passage and choose Copy citation to get a citation card carrying title, author, page, and the selected text. Export selected books, annotations, and citation cards to BibTeX, RIS, CSL JSON, or a Markdown digest from the catalogue's export menu.
P0075: 1.3.43.4 Searching Inside a Document
P0076: Press the in-document search shortcut, type a phrase, and Ogma highlights matches and lists the matching pages. For scanned (image-only) books, run Launch OCR from the detail panel first; OCR runs as a background job and its results are marked as OCR-derived in search.
P0077: 1.44. Searching the Whole Library
P0078: Type in the search box to search metadata as you type. Submit a query to search full text, notes, tags, descriptions, and tables of contents. Every result explains where it matched — title, author, a note, a page of text, or a semantic match — so you can trust why it appeared. Semantic search understands natural-language intent ("that book about cities and memory") using embeddings that are computed locally.
P0079: The Index Manager (Settings, Index) shows what is indexed, what is pending OCR, what failed, and how large the index is, with Rebuild index and Erase embeddings controls.
P0081: The screenshot above is from the pseudolocale build (text wrapped in [!! … !!]), which the team uses to prove that no interface string is hard-coded; your build shows plain English or French.
P0082: 1.55. The AI Reading Advisor and Your Privacy
P0083: 1.5.15.1 Privacy Tiers
P0084: AI in Ogma is optional and disabled by default. When you enable it, you choose one of four privacy tiers:
P0085: Tier
P0086: Name
P0087: What can leave your device
P0088: 0
P0089: Offline
P0090: Nothing. AI features are off. This is the default.
P0091: 1
P0092: Metadata-only
P0093: Titles, authors, tags, categories, descriptions, and your notes — never document content. Default for any cloud use.
P0094: 2
P0095: Content-aware
P0096: Selected document excerpts, only after an explicit per-library or per-query opt-in.
P0097: 3
P0098: Local AI
P0099: Nothing. A local model (Ollama) runs on your machine.
P0100: Before any off-device call, Ogma shows a payload preview: the exact data, the exact fields, and the destination provider. Nothing is sent until you confirm that preview.
P0101: 1.5.25.2 Using the Advisor
P0102: Enable AI in Settings and pick a provider (OpenAI, Anthropic-compatible, DeepSeek-compatible, or local Ollama). Keys are stored in your OS credential store.
P0103: Ask for recommendations in your own words. Each recommendation card states why it matched, what supported the match, and a confidence level — drawn from your collection, not the internet.
P0104: Generate a reading plan to get a sequenced path through your books with a rationale, estimated difficulty, and checkpoints per step.
P0105: The evidence-cited answer mode (ask a question, get an answer citing book and page) is planned for a later release and is not in this build.
P0106: 1.5.35.3 The Privacy Center
P0107: The Privacy Center (Settings, Privacy) lists your provider keys, your recent AI calls with their exact payloads and privacy tiers, and delete controls for query history and embeddings, plus an audit-log export. Every off-device transmission, tier change, and credential change is auditable there.
P0108: 1.66. The Library Health Dashboard
P0109: Open Library Health to audit your collection.
P0111: Each row (duplicates, missing covers, missing ISBNs, unavailable files, failed jobs) shows a count and opens the affected books with a remediation action — for example, running enrichment on the 12 books missing ISBNs, or locating the 1 unavailable file.
P0112: 1.77. Classroom Mode for Students
P0113: If your school runs an Ogma library host, you can read the shared collection from any computer on the school network.
P0114: 1.7.17.1 Joining a Classroom Library
P0115: Choose Join a library and either pick the discovered host or scan the QR join code / enter the join link your teacher gives you.
P0116: On first connection, Ogma shows the host's certificate fingerprint; your enrollment confirms it, and any later change to the host's identity is blocked until re-approved.
P0117: Sign in to your personal profile. Your reading progress, bookmarks, and notes belong to your profile alone — other students cannot see them.
P0118: 1.7.27.2 Reading, Searching, and AI in the Classroom
P0119: Books stream from the host; you never need to copy the whole file. Recently read books stay in a size-limited offline cache so a network drop does not interrupt you mid-chapter.
P0120: Search runs against the host's index with the same match explanations as personal search.
P0121: Smart search (AI) goes through your school's account, not your own. Before anything leaves the school's network, you see the privacy-tier label and the exact payload. The school default sends metadata only — never book content — unless your school administrator has explicitly enabled more.
P0122: You can delete your own AI query history at any time (Settings, Privacy).
P0123: If your enrollment is revoked, you lose access to the shared library at your next connection, but the notes you made stay on your machine.
P0124: 1.88. Host Mode and School Administration Basics
P0125: This section is for teachers and administrators. Host mode is off by default on every install; nothing listens on the network until you enable it.
P0126: 1.8.18.1 Enabling Host Mode
P0127: Open Sharing, then Library Host, and switch host mode on. The action is confirmed, audited, and reversible.
P0128: Ogma provisions a TLS certificate and advertises the host on the local network (mDNS). Clients see the certificate fingerprint on first connection.
P0129: Publish the folders and shelves you want shared. Only published content is ever visible or enumerable to clients — unpublished books do not exist as far as clients are concerned.
P0130: Generate join codes (QR) for your students and teachers.
P0131: 1.8.28.2 Managing People and Sessions
P0132: Enroll, edit, and revoke student and teacher profiles from the admin console. Active client sessions can be listed, limited, and revoked. Every connection, stream, and denial is written to the host activity log, which you can export.
P0133: 1.8.38.3 Managed AI for the Classroom
P0134: Store your school's AI key on the host; it lives in the host's OS credential store and is never sent to any client.
P0135: All student AI queries route through the host's gateway under your policy: the class default is metadata-only, and content-aware analysis requires your explicit opt-in.
P0136: Set per-student and per-class quotas and per-student rate limits; the admin dashboard shows usage, cost, and quota utilisation.
P0137: Students can delete their own AI history; you can purge all student AI history for the institution.
P0138: The data-protection posture of classroom mode — including how students' (minors') reading and AI-query data is protected — is documented in the project's Data Protection Impact Assessment (09-governance-compliance/03-dpia/). Teacher dashboards are aggregate by design; per-student visibility is disclosed to the students it covers.
P0139: 1.99. Troubleshooting
P0140: Symptom
P0141: Cause
P0142: Resolution
P0143: A book shows as unavailable
P0144: Its file was moved outside the library root or deleted
P0145: Restore or relocate the file, then rescan; your notes and progress are intact either way
P0146: A scanned book returns no search hits
P0147: It has no text layer and has not been OCR-processed
P0148: Open the detail panel and click Launch OCR; hits appear when the job completes, marked OCR-derived
P0149: The AI panel is greyed out
P0150: AI is disabled (Tier 0) or no provider is configured
P0151: Enable AI in Settings and select a provider, or leave it off — no reading feature depends on it
P0152: A classroom client cannot connect
P0153: Host offline, enrollment revoked, or changed host certificate
P0154: Check host status with the administrator; re-approve the fingerprint if the host was legitimately reinstalled
P0155: A metadata write-back reports failure
P0156: The PDF is locked, read-only, or malformed
P0157: The original file is untouched by design; review the failure detail and retry after unlocking the file
P0158: Search feels stale after a big import
P0159: The index has not caught up
P0160: Open the Index Manager and check progress, or run Rebuild index
P0161: 1.1010. Getting Help
P0162: In-app help is planned (FR-UX-006) and not yet in this build; this manual and the FAQ (faq.md) are the current references.
P0163: Support: Chwezi Core Systems — chwezicore.com.

P0188: 2.11. System Requirements
P0189: Requirement
P0190: Minimum (matches reference machine W-REF-01 / M-REF-01)
P0191: Operating system
P0192: Windows 10 22H2 or later; macOS 13.6 or later
P0193: CPU / RAM
P0194: 4-core CPU, 8 GB RAM
P0195: Storage
P0196: SSD recommended; allow roughly 1–3 GB of sidecar space (covers, thumbnails, extracted text, index) per 2,000 books, in addition to your PDFs
P0197: Display
P0198: 1920×1080 or better
P0199: WebView
P0200: Windows: WebView2 Runtime (usually preinstalled on Windows 10/11); macOS: WKWebView (built in)
P0201: Network
P0202: None required. Internet is needed only for optional metadata lookups and optional cloud AI
P0203: 2.22. Prerequisites (from-source install)
P0204: .NET 10 SDK — install from dotnet.microsoft.com; verify with dotnet --version (must report 10.x).
P0205: Git — verify with git --version.
P0206: Windows only: WebView2 Runtime if not already present (required by the 3D shelf).
P0207: Optional: Ollama if you want local AI (Tier 3) or local embeddings — any recent release; verify with ollama --version.
P0208: Optional: an API key for OpenAI, an Anthropic-compatible provider, or a DeepSeek-compatible provider if you want cloud AI. Not required for any core feature.
P0209: 2.33. Installation Steps (Windows and macOS)
P0210: Clone the repository:
P0211: git clone <repository URL> Ogma-Librarycd Ogma-Library
P0212: Build (warnings are errors; a clean build is the expected state):
P0213: dotnet build OgmaLibrary.sln -c Release
P0214: Run the desktop application:
P0215: dotnet run --project src/OgmaLibrary.App -c Release
P0216: The catalogue database and sidecar assets are created on first run next to your chosen library folder and in the per-user application-data directory; no administrator rights are needed.
P0217: 2.44. Configuration
P0218: All configuration is in-app (Settings): language (English/French), theme (light/dark/system), excluded folders, AI provider and privacy tier, index options.
P0219: AI provider keys are stored in the operating system credential store (Windows Credential Manager / macOS Keychain), never in files you could accidentally commit or copy.
P0220: Classroom host mode is off by default and is enabled only from Sharing, Library Host (see the User Manual §8).
P0221: 2.55. Post-Installation Verification
P0222: Launch the app; the welcome screen appears within seconds on reference-class hardware (the formal cold-start budget NFR-OGMA-001 is ≤ 3 s at P95, verified at Phase 20).
P0223: Choose a folder containing at least one PDF; the book appears in the catalogue while scanning continues.
P0224: Open the book; a page renders and the reader resumes position after close/reopen.
P0225: Optional full check: run dotnet test OgmaLibrary.sln -c Release --nologo; the controlled 2026-08-13 baseline executed 800 passing cases at commit 26df98322b7934919ddaba513ac02d9aaa44edbb.
P0226: 2.66. Upgrading
P0227: Until packaged auto-update ships (Phase 22, signed and reversible per the packaging decision ADR-0009), upgrading is git pull followed by a rebuild. Database schema migrations run automatically and are designed to be idempotent and backed up; your catalogue, annotations, and settings are preserved across upgrades.
P0228: 2.77. Uninstallation
P0229: Delete the cloned repository folder.
P0230: Delete the per-user application-data directory if you want to remove the catalogue database, sidecar assets, and settings. Your PDF files are never touched: Ogma augments your folder, it does not move or convert your books.
P0231: Remove any stored provider keys from the OS credential store (Windows Credential Manager / macOS Keychain, entries prefixed with the application name).
P0232: 2.88. Common Issues and Solutions
P0233: Issue
P0234: Cause
P0235: Solution
P0236: dotnet build fails on SDK version
P0237: .NET SDK older than 10
P0238: Install the .NET 10 SDK and re-run dotnet --version
P0239: 3D shelf tab is blank (Windows)
P0240: WebView2 Runtime missing
P0241: Install the WebView2 Runtime and restart the app; grid and list views work regardless
P0242: Local AI features unavailable
P0243: Ollama not installed or not running
P0244: Install and start Ollama, then re-select the local provider in Settings — or use Tier 0/1 instead
P0245: Build warnings treated as errors after local edits
P0246: The solution builds with warnings-as-errors by design
P0247: Fix the warning; do not disable the setting
P0248: macOS blocks the unsigned dev build
P0249: Code signing and notarization arrive with Phase 22
P0250: Right-click, Open for a from-source development build; packaged notarized builds will remove this step
P0252: 2.9phase: "08" document: FAQ
P0253: 3Ogma Library — Frequently Asked Questions
P0254: Field
P0255: Value
P0256: Product
P0257: Ogma Library (development snapshot, grand-plan Phases 00–18)
P0258: Document
P0259: FAQ
P0260: Owner
P0261: Chwezi Core Systems
P0262: Author
P0263: Peter Bamuhigire, Lead Consultant, Chwezi Core
P0264: Version
P0265: 2.0
P0266: Date
P0267: 2026-07-06
P0268: Status
P0269: Draft for owner sign-off
P0270: 3.1General
P0271: Q1: What is Ogma Library?
P0272: A local-first desktop application that turns a folder of PDFs into a managed, searchable library: covers and shelves (including a 3D shelf), reading progress and annotations, metadata repair, full-text and semantic search, an optional AI reading advisor, and an optional classroom mode that shares a collection over a school network. See the User Manual (user-manual.md) for procedures.
P0273: Q2: Does Ogma change or move my PDF files?
P0274: No. Your files stay where they are, in their original format. The catalogue database augments your folder; it never replaces it. The only operation that touches a PDF is optional metadata write-back, which runs only after your confirmation, creates a backup first, shows a field diff, verifies the result, and restores the original on any failure.
P0275: Q3: Does Ogma work offline?
P0276: Yes — offline is the normal state. Reading, browsing, annotation, metadata editing, search, and local AI (via Ollama) all work without internet. Only two features ever use the network, both opt-in: external metadata lookups (Google Books, Open Library) and cloud AI.
P0277: 3.2Privacy and Data
P0278: Q4: What data leaves my device?
P0279: Nothing, by default. Ogma's default privacy tier is Offline and the default install transmits 0 bytes. If you opt into cloud AI or metadata lookups, a minimised payload is shown to you in a preview — exact content, exact fields, destination provider — and is sent only after you confirm it. The Privacy Center lists every past call with its exact payload.
P0280: Q5: Where are my AI provider keys stored?
P0281: In your operating system's credential store (Windows Credential Manager or macOS Keychain) — never in the catalogue database, configuration files, exports, or backups. Removing a provider deletes its stored key.
P0282: Q6: Can I delete what the AI has stored?
P0283: Yes. You can delete AI query history and derived embeddings for one document or the whole library in a single operation, and the Privacy Center surfaces your provider's own deletion mechanism for anything sent off-device. You can also disable retention entirely.
P0284: Q7: Will my documents be used to train AI models?
P0285: The provider no-training flag defaults to opt-out. Enabling training would require a separate, explicit opt-in, independent of enabling the feature itself.
P0286: 3.3AI Features
P0287: Q8: Do I need an AI subscription or key to use Ogma?
P0288: No. AI is fully optional and disabled by default; no core feature depends on it. If you want AI, you can run it entirely locally with Ollama (no key, no cloud), or bring a key for OpenAI, an Anthropic-compatible provider, or a DeepSeek-compatible provider.
P0289: Q9: What does the AI actually do?
P0290: It recommends books from your own collection — with an explanation and a confidence level per recommendation — and generates reading plans (sequence, rationale, difficulty, checkpoints). A question-answering mode that cites book and page is planned but not in this build.
P0291: Q10: How do I know what a cloud AI call will cost?
P0292: Each cloud call displays a model-usage and cost estimate, and the school/admin dashboard (in classroom mode) totals usage and cost against quotas.
P0293: 3.4Classroom Mode
P0294: Q11: How do students join a school library?
P0295: The school administrator enables host mode and hands out QR join codes or join links. A student picks the discovered host (or scans the code), confirms the host's certificate fingerprint on first connection, and signs into a personal profile. See User Manual §7.
P0296: Q12: Can my teacher see what I read and write?
P0297: Your notes, bookmarks, and progress are private to your profile — other students can never see them. Teachers see class-level aggregate dashboards; any per-student visibility is disclosed to the students it covers rather than collected covertly. Your AI query history is yours to delete.
P0298: Q13: Does classroom AI expose students' book content to a cloud provider?
P0299: Not by default. Student AI queries route through the school's host under the school's key, and the class default tier is metadata-only — book content is never included unless the school administrator explicitly opts the class into content-aware mode, and every student still sees the payload preview before anything is sent.
P0300: Q14: What happens if the school network or host goes down mid-lesson?
P0301: Recently read books remain available from the client's size-limited offline cache, the client shows its offline state, and reading state re-syncs when the host returns. A host outage never destroys a student's local notes.
P0302: 3.5Installation and Licensing
P0303: Q15: How do I install Ogma today?
P0304: By building from source with the .NET 10 SDK — see installation-guide.md. Packaged installers (direct download, Microsoft Store, Mac App Store) are Phase 22 scope and are not yet available. Linux support of the classroom client secret storage exists in code, but Windows and macOS are the supported desktop targets.
P0305: Q16: What will Ogma cost?
P0306: The working pricing hypothesis is a one-time license (no subscription) with an optional annual update plan, plus separate education licensing for classroom hosts and seats. Final pricing is an open owner decision; nothing in this FAQ is a price commitment.
P0307: Q17: Which languages does the interface support?
P0308: English and French are complete and switchable at runtime in this build. Spanish, Italian, and German complete the launch scope (grand-plan Phase 21).

P0311: 4Ogma Library — Release Notes, v0.x Development Snapshot
P0312: Field
P0313: Value
P0314: Product
P0315: Ogma Library
P0316: Release
P0317: v0.x development snapshot (grand-plan Phases 00–18; not a public release)
P0318: Snapshot date
P0319: 2026-07-06 (inspected commit 26df983 dated 2026-07-07)
P0320: Owner
P0321: Chwezi Core Systems
P0322: Author
P0323: Peter Bamuhigire, Lead Consultant, Chwezi Core
P0324: Document version
P0325: 2.0 — Draft for owner sign-off
P0326: This snapshot documents what the codebase at C:\wamp64\www\Ogma-Library actually delivers as of the Phases 00–18 build (299 commits through 2026-07-07, 800 passing executed cases in the 2026-08-13 canonical Release run). It is a development snapshot, not a public release: packaging, signing, and store distribution are Phase 22 scope. Identifier note: this project uses module-scoped requirement identifiers (FR-LIB-001 and similar) rather than flat FR-001-style numbering; every identifier below traces to the baselined inventory in _context/features.md.
P0327: 4.1Highlights
P0328: A complete local-first PDF library — scanning, catalogue, metadata repair, reader with durable annotations, full-text and semantic search, a privacy-tiered AI advisor, and a 3D shelf — plus the full classroom stack: LAN host mode, per-student classroom clients, and school-administered managed AI.
P0329: 4.2Delivered Features by Module
P0330: 4.2.1Library setup and scanning (FR-LIB-001 through FR-LIB-007)
P0331: Root selection with rescan; recursive discovery with exclusions; stable identity via path plus content hash so renames re-match; unavailable files preserved with all user data; background thumbnail/spine generation with visible, retryable failures; incremental rescan; scan health report.
P0332: 4.2.2Catalogue browsing (FR-CAT-001 through FR-CAT-006)
P0333: Grid, list, directory, and 3D shelf views over one catalogue; combinable, clearable sort and filter; virtual shelves with multi-membership; full metadata inspector; previewed, undoable bulk edit; smart shelves. Not included: the Work/Edition duplicate-grouping model (FR-CAT-007).
P0334: 4.2.3Metadata enrichment (FR-META-001 through FR-META-008)
P0335: ISBN detection, normalisation, and ranking; Google Books and Open Library lookups with source, timestamp, and confidence; reviewable confidence merge; field-level provenance; reversible PDF write-back (backup, diff, verify, restore); rate-limited batch enrichment; metadata quality score; library health dashboard.
P0336: 4.2.4PDF reader and annotations (FR-READ-001 through FR-READ-011, FR-READ-013 through FR-READ-015)
P0337: Open/resume, full navigation with history, zoom and display modes with persistence, full-screen, in-document search, bookmarks, durable highlights and notes, password-protected PDFs via the OS credential flow, background OCR (Tesseract) with OCR-derived labelling, citation capture, citation/research export (BibTeX, RIS, CSL JSON, Markdown digest), reading memory, and annotation layers.
P0338: 4.2.5Search and indexing (FR-SEARCH-001 through FR-SEARCH-006)
P0339: Type-ahead metadata search; FTS5 full-text index over text, notes, tags, descriptions, and tables of contents; match-location explanations; semantic search on local embeddings; hybrid ranking; index manager with rebuild and erase-embeddings controls.
P0340: 4.2.6AI advisor and privacy (FR-AI-001 through FR-AI-007, FR-AI-009 through FR-AI-011)
P0341: Complete disable (default); provider choice — OpenAI, Anthropic-compatible, DeepSeek-compatible, Ollama — behind one gateway; explained, confidence-rated recommendations; metadata-only default with payload preview; content-aware opt-in with privacy labels; local embeddings; reading plans; local query history with deletion and retention controls; per-call usage and cost estimates; Privacy Center with exact-payload call log and audit export.
P0342: 4.2.7LAN host mode (FR-LAN-001 through FR-LAN-010)
P0343: Opt-in host (no listener by default), TLS catalogue and range-request PDF streaming with path validation, mDNS discovery, certificate provisioning with trust-on-first-use fingerprints, per-profile credentials and QR join codes, session management, non-destructive degradation, publish-scope enforcement, exportable activity audit.
P0344: 4.2.8Classroom client (FR-CLIENT-001 through FR-CLIENT-013)
P0345: Host discovery and connection, per-student private profiles, roles (student/teacher/administrator), streamed reading, bounded offline cache with purge, reading-state sync with per-field conflict rules, host-index search with explanations, student privacy isolation, enrollment/revocation semantics, strict read-only access to host content, host-outage degradation, aggregate-only teacher dashboards with disclosed visibility, entitlements.
P0346: 4.2.9School administration and managed AI (FR-ADMIN-001 through FR-ADMIN-013)
P0347: Folder publish/unpublish, shared shelves, profile management, school AI keys in the host OS credential store (never sent to clients), host-routed student AI queries, metadata-only class default with admin-gated content-aware mode, student payload preview, per-student and per-class quotas, per-student rate limits, usage/cost/quota dashboard, host-catalogue-cited classroom answers, student self-deletion of AI history, institution-wide AI-history purge.
P0348: 4.2.10Platform and quality
P0349: .NET 10 solution with warnings-as-errors and XML docs; architecture tests enforcing inward dependencies and the single network-egress chokepoint; English and French interface resources with pseudolocale tests; golden-corpus test harness; AI evaluation harness; 10 EF Core migrations up to the Phase 18 school-admin schema.
P0350: 4.3Known Limitations
P0351: Split view (FR-READ-012) is not implemented — you cannot yet read two documents or two locations side by side.
P0352: The evidence-cited AI answer mode (FR-AI-008) is not implemented — the advisor recommends and plans, but does not answer questions with citations. Its classroom counterpart surface depends on it.
P0353: The Work/Edition model (FR-CAT-007), command palette (FR-UX-003), and in-app help (FR-UX-006) are not implemented.
P0354: Grand-plan Phases 19–23 are outstanding: security hardening (including optional at-rest encryption), formal performance acceptance on reference hardware (all current performance numbers are trend-only), the WCAG 2.2 AA audit, Spanish/Italian/German localization, packaging/signing/store submission, and beta/launch operations.
P0355: Premium icon procurement is open; placeholder and stock icons are in use (release blocker by policy).
P0356: No production screenshots exist for the 3D shelf or settings surfaces (tracking item DOC-IMG-1); manual screenshots come from the development build on a synthetic corpus.
P0357: The inspected baseline uses .NET 10, EF Core SQLite 10.0.9, Microsoft.Extensions 10.0.9, Avalonia 11.3.17, and SQLitePCLRaw.bundle_e_sqlite3 3.0.3. ADR-0016 records the current runtime baseline.

P0367: 4.5Upgrade and Migration Notes
